(b) `202607220040:13-19` made candidate identity unique only over non-cancelled rows, and `confirm_entry_task_candidates_v6` records a resolution row only for a disposition **other than** `'confirmed'` (`202607220044:1357-1358`) — so a confirmed candidate leaves no ledger entry, the `2C_TERMINAL_DISPOSITION` gate never fires for it, and cancelling frees the slot for a duplicate the entry surface immediately re-offers. Restoring afterwards is a bare `23505`, which `apply.ts` maps to `undeclared_failure` with `retryable: true`.
- **Alternatives considered:** *One token for both* (rejected: different causes with different remedies — a creation-undone task is permanently deleted, a slot-taken task is blocked by a duplicate the user can still cancel — and telling a user their task was deleted when it is merely blocked is a lie the copy would have to carry). *Guard every field-undo rather than the cancel-undo* (rejected: unnecessary, and it would refuse legitimate compensations; the exhaustiveness argument is proven instead). *Let the `23505` surface and add a mapper case* (rejected: 2E-UPDATE-017 makes the vocabulary a closed list of `2E_*` DETAIL tokens, and a raw SQLSTATE is not a member of it). *Exclude slot-taken tasks from the listing too* (rejected: hiding the row hides the only place the situation is legible).
- **Decision:** (1) Two `private` predicates, `task_creation_undone` and `task_candidate_slot_taken`, each defined **once** and read by every door. (2) `2E_CREATION_UNDONE` (`55P03`) closes the cancel-undo and `restore_task`; the candidate listing **excludes** such tasks through the same predicate, by a body-only `create or replace` of `list_task_command_candidates`. (3) `2E_CANDIDATE_REMATERIALIZED` (`55P03`) closes the same two doors for the slot collision, backed by a `unique_violation` trap on both status writes for the concurrent case; the listing does **not** exclude those tasks. (4) `task_candidate_slot_taken` mirrors both partial indexes exactly, **including their absence of an owner column**. (5) The creation-family rows are locked `for update` **before** `public.tasks`, everywhere they are read under a write.
- **Reason:** The cancel-undo is the only field-undo that can pass the ten-column guard on a creation-undone task, because that guard compares `status` against the status the forward action *left*, a creation-undo forces the row to `cancelled`, and `cancel_task` is the sole action in §11.2 whose target status is `cancelled` — `set_status` is bounded to the six non-terminal precisely so it cannot be a second route. That is an argument about the other fourteen actions, so the pgTAP suite proves it with a rename rather than asserting it. Owner-scoping the occupant of `task_candidate_slot_taken` would make the predicate **laxer** than the constraint it anticipates, which is the one direction that lets a `23505` through; cross-owner occupancy is unrepresentable anyway, so the difference is theoretical while being at least as strict as the index is not. The lock order is forced: `public.undo_operation` takes the operation row `for update` and only then calls a handler that writes `public.tasks` (`202607250052:654-657`), so acquiring the task first would give the two paths opposite orders and a deadlock. The lock covers the family regardless of `status` while the refusal reads only `undone` rows, because locking only the `undone` ones would leave an `available` row free to become one underneath.
- **Consequences:** A deleted task is a candidate for **nothing** — not `restore_task`, not `rename_task`, not the recovery listing — which is stricter than §13.6 asks and is the correct reading of "treated as deleted". The comment at `taxonomy.ts:301-305` claiming re-confirmation was gated by the resolution ledger was **false** and is corrected in the same change. The `unique_violation` trap opens one subtransaction per status command; that cost is accepted against never surfacing an undeclared SQLSTATE that a mapper would call retryable. The concurrent path the trap exists for is not provoked by pgTAP — it needs two sessions — and is recorded as owed with the remote smoke.

## ADR-049 — A declared failure code is retired when nothing can raise it

- **Date:** 2026-07-27
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Slice 2E.5 on branch `codex/phase-2e-natural-language-task-updates`.
- **Context:** `2E_ACTION_NOT_ENABLED` was declared by Slice 2E.4 to refuse `cancel_task` and `restore_task` until confirmation evidence existed. With both enabled and all fifteen actions resolved from the taxonomy `case`, its `else` terminator is unreachable while the closed enum and that `case` list the same members.
- **Problem:** Keep a declared, localized, pgTAP-asserted code whose raise no state can reach, or retire it.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep it as the terminator's token* (rejected — see Reason). *Delete the terminator too* (rejected: an action added to the enum and forgotten in the taxonomy would fall through with null policy arrays and be validated against nothing).
- **Decision:** The token is removed from `TASK_COMMAND_ERROR_DETAILS`, from `copy.ts`, from the pgTAP catalog arrays and from every function body. The `else` survives, raising the bare `22023 'Invalid task command action'` that step 2 already gives an unknown action. A post-deploy guard greps every shipped body for the retired token and fails the deployment if one still names it — which is why the migration's own prose must not mention it inside a function.
- **Reason:** This phase has twice rejected a guard that no reachable state can provoke — the two tautological reminder checks of `202607260058` — on the grounds that for a declared member of a closed error vocabulary it is the same defect as a missing raise. Applying that standard to a *guard* but not to a *code* would be inconsistent. The replacement refusal is provokable through step 2, so the terminator remains meaningful without adding an unreachable declaration.
- **Consequences:** `TASK_COMMAND_ERROR_DETAILS` goes 9 → 11 rather than 9 → 12. The retirement is enforced at deploy rather than by convention. A future action added to the enum without a taxonomy branch is refused as an invalid action rather than as an unavailable one, which is truthful: it has no policy, so it is not an action this contract knows.

## ADR-050 — A multi-step command continues by re-derivation from a pinned clock

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Slice 2E.7 on branch `codex/phase-2e-natural-language-task-updates`.
- **Context:** Phase 2E's flow is several round trips — parse, maybe disambiguate, preview, maybe confirm, apply — and every step after the first needs the command the first step produced. PRD §11.1 forbids the obvious shortcut by name: "No state is stored in the client as the source of truth. A preview is recomputed server-side and re-fingerprinted on every render."
- **Problem:** Something has to survive the round trip, and the three candidates each break a different guarantee.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Re-parse the command text on every step* (rejected: a second billed call that may legitimately return a **different** command than the one previewed, so the user applies something they never saw — and 2E-COMMAND-013 makes a re-parse failure mid-flow an outcome with nowhere to go). *Carry the computed preview* (rejected by PRD §11.1 directly). *Persist the session server-side* (rejected: a new table, a new lifecycle, a new expiry vocabulary and a new cleanup job, for state whose entire purpose is to be reconstructible).
- **Decision:** The client round-trips a validated envelope carrying the model's **normalized proposal** and the instant the session was issued at. Every step re-runs `validateTaskCommand`, `loadTaskCandidates` and `rankTaskCandidates` against that same instant. The envelope also carries the explicit selection and a staleness witness `{taskId, updatedAt}` from the preview that was actually rendered; a writing path requires the witness and refuses without it.
- **Reason:** **Pinning `issuedAt` is the whole mechanism, not a detail.** `loadTaskCandidates` derives `p_observed_before` from the instant it is given (`candidates.ts:231`) and the RPC echoes it onto every row, from where `buildTaskCommandPreview` carries it into `preview.observedBefore` and `buildApplyPayload` hashes it. Minting a fresh instant per step would therefore change the fingerprint on every step of one operation key, and three things break at once: 2E-IDEMPOTENCY-004's replay returns `2E_IDEMPOTENCY_MISMATCH` instead of the original outcome, a double-submit is refused rather than replayed, and a destructive retry after a `2E_TRANSITION_INTEGRITY` can **never** succeed, because the surviving confirmation row is bound to the first attempt's digest. A first draft of this design justified *not* pinning it on the claim that `observedBefore` "is what the twelve-column staleness gate compares against"; that is false — the gate at `202607260058:1121-1136` compares twelve `locked_task` columns against values derived from `p_pre_state`, and `p_observed_before` is only format-validated. Staleness lives in `expected` and `p_pre_state`; the pinned instant carries determinism and request identity.
- **Consequences:** The envelope is untrusted input and is parsed by a schema like any other boundary, with a version literal so an envelope minted by an older deployment is refused rather than best-effort-read. Tampering buys nothing — the tampered command is re-validated, re-previewed and re-rendered, so the user still sees what they are about to apply, and every authorization decision is the database's against `auth.uid()`. What tampering must not be able to do is *skip* a check, which is why the staleness witness is **required** on writing paths: an envelope that reached one without a witness is an envelope no server render produced, and honouring it would make 2E-PREVIEW-006 structurally unreachable. `clarificationUsed` is likewise derived from the envelope carrying an answer rather than carried as a boolean, so the bounded-once guarantee of 2E-NOMATCH-008 is a property of the shape. A command whose session is abandoned costs nothing: no row was written.

## ADR-051 — The destructive confirmation is issued by the render, and the dialog is hand-rolled

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Slice 2E.7 on branch `codex/phase-2e-natural-language-task-updates`.
- **Context:** ADR-047 established that the confirmation is a server-minted row bound to a fingerprint the RPC derives itself. Slice 2E.7 has to decide *when* that row is minted, and what the confirmation step looks like. 2E-A11Y-004 requires "an appropriate dialog role with an accessible name and description".
- **Problem:** (a) A first design issued the confirmation inside the Confirm action, immediately before consuming it. (b) A native `<dialog>` with `showModal()` is the obvious way to satisfy 2E-A11Y-004.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Issue at Confirm time* (rejected — see Reason). *Native `<dialog>` + `showModal()`* (rejected on executed evidence). *An inline confirm-and-apply control with no dialog* (rejected: 2E-A11Y-004 asks for a dialog role, and a destructive action that shares its visual weight with an ordinary Apply is exactly the accident 2E-DESTRUCTIVE-001 exists to prevent).
- **Decision:** (1) The confirmation is issued by the round that **renders** the preview, never by the one that consumes it. (2) The dialog is a hand-rolled `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` with its own focus management. The same placement rule applies to the creation confirmation of 2E-NOMATCH-005.
- **Reason:** (a) Issuing and consuming in one action binds the token to whatever the envelope says **at Confirm time**, which is not necessarily the preview the user saw. The draft's own stated defence — "editing the patch after issuance fails `2E_CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`" — was unreachable in its own flow, because there was no issuance to be *before*. That is a security defect rather than a style point: 2E-DESTRUCTIVE-003 says "a changed proposal invalidates prior confirmation", and there is no prior confirmation to invalidate if it is minted last. (b) **jsdom 29.1.1 — the version this repository runs — has no `showModal`, `show` or `close` on `HTMLDialogElement.prototype`; `showModal()` throws `TypeError`.** Verified by executing jsdom, not by reading the version number. Vitest with jsdom is the only accessibility gate CI runs, so a native dialog would leave 2E-A11Y-004 asserted by nobody and shipping on a manual read of the source.
- **Consequences:** Every render of a destructive preview mints a confirmation, so an abandoned flow leaves an unspent row — which ADR-047 already accepts, and which a single subsequent edit to the task makes unappliable. The three behaviours `showModal()` would have supplied (initial focus, a Tab cycle, Escape-restores-focus) are implemented and each has a test. That decision paid for itself immediately: the focus-trap test found that `input:not([disabled])` matches `type="hidden"` fields, so "the first focusable element" resolved to a hidden form field, `.focus()` on it was a silent no-op, and the dialog opened with focus still on the page behind it. The selector now excludes hidden inputs. Background inerting uses the focus cycle rather than the `inert` attribute, which jsdom does not reflect either.

## ADR-052 — `task_command` is its own analytics surface, and the remote smoke reads the vocabulary rather than restating it

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Slice 2E.7 on branch `codex/phase-2e-natural-language-task-updates`.
- **Context:** 2E-ANALYTICS-005 requires the command surface value in all three allowlists "in the same migration and commit as the first emitting code". 2E-ANALYTICS-006 requires `scripts/remote-product-events-smoke.mjs` to import the vocabulary from the contracts module "so a TypeScript addition cannot drift from the smoke". PRD §3.4 already records that the smoke's hard-coded list had fallen behind.
- **Problem:** (a) The console mounts on both Chat and the task surface, so neither `work` nor a new `chat` surface describes it. (b) The smoke is plain Node and this repository carries no `tsx`, `ts-node` or esbuild register hook, so a literal `import` of the `.ts` module is not available.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Fold the console into the `work` surface* (rejected: it would attribute every command driven from Chat to the task surface, making "where do commands come from" unanswerable — which is one of PRD §18's questions). *Add a `chat` surface and use it from Chat, `work` from the task page* (rejected: the same feature would report under two surfaces, so no single query counts commands). *Generate a JSON vocabulary file at build time* (rejected: a third artifact that can go stale, and nothing would notice if the generator stopped running). *Leave the smoke restating the list and add a test comparing them* (rejected: that is the situation that already drifted).
- **Decision:** (1) `task_command` is a surface of its own; which mount a command came from travels as the bounded `commandOrigin` **property**. (2) A shared `scripts/product-event-vocabulary.mjs` reads the two `as const` declarations out of `contracts.ts` and throws on anything it cannot parse completely; the smoke imports it, and a Vitest case holds the reader to the real imported constants by exact equality.
- **Reason:** The surface names the *feature* and the property names the *mount*, which keeps both questions answerable from one closed vocabulary. For the smoke, reading the single source of truth is the same guarantee an import would give, by the only mechanism available: there remains exactly one place either list is written down. Failing loudly rather than falling back is the point — a silent empty list would turn the smoke green while asserting nothing, which is precisely the failure 2E-ANALYTICS-006 exists to prevent. The Vitest mirror is what makes this a reader rather than a fourth copy, and it runs in the `app` job rather than in a manual remote run nobody is watching.
- **Consequences:** Making the vocabulary authoritative immediately exposed that the smoke's event matrix was missing `question_resolved`, `question_effect_previewed` and `question_reinterpret_applied`; all three are now exercised. The `policyVersion` property is validated as a *shape* (`YYYY-MM-DD.N`) rather than against today's literal, because PRD §10.4 requires that version to be bumped whenever a weight or threshold changes, and a pinned literal would make every legitimate bump reject live events. Band, outcome and evidence literals **are** pinned exactly, because those vocabularies are closed and a CHECK literal is permanent.

## ADR-053 — `2E-COMMAND-012` is reclassified to Phase 2F rather than satisfied by a signature change in a closeout slice

- **Date:** 2026-07-28
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Slice 2E.8 on branch `codex/phase-2e-natural-language-task-updates`. Carried into `docs/initiatives/phase-2e/PHASE_2E_PRD.md` revision 4.
- **Context:** 2E-COMMAND-012 requires that "prompt and strategy versions of a command proposal are recorded on the resulting operation, since `ai_usage_events` has no column for them". 2E-PROVENANCE-001 restates the substance. Slice 2E.7 left the choice to Slice 2E.8 in explicit terms: add the column, or record a reclassification in the PRD.
- **Problem:** What exists is `TASK_COMMAND_PROMPT_VERSION` and `TASK_COMMAND_STRATEGY_VERSION` as build constants, pinned by `src/lib/ai/task-command-contract.test.ts`, carried on the command session and available wherever the operation is written. What does not exist is any persisted column — not on `undo_operations`, not on `ai_usage_events`, not in any Phase 2E RPC's argument list. Every route to one runs through a function signature change: `apply_task_command` (7 arguments), `create_task_command` (6), or `record_ai_usage` (10).
- **The constraint that decides this, and it is a PostgreSQL fact rather than a preference:** none of the three can be extended by `create or replace`. A different argument list is a *different function*, so `create or replace` produces an overload instead of a replacement. Two live overloads of `record_ai_usage` would make every existing 10-key PostgREST call ambiguous — `function is not unique` — breaking capture extraction, chat, review and file analysis at once. Each therefore requires `drop function` followed by a complete re-declaration of the body.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Re-declare `apply_task_command` with two more arguments* (rejected: ~1,460 lines of body re-pasted, on the phase's most load-bearing function, in the last slice, with no local pgTAP and a four-minute CI round trip as the only SQL evidence — the exact edit class `PHASE_2E_PROGRESS.md` names as the highest-risk in this repository). *Drop and re-declare `record_ai_usage` with two new nullable columns on `ai_usage_events`* (rejected on blast radius, and it is the closest call: the body is only ~110 lines and migration `202607250055` already contains it verbatim, but the function is shared by **every** AI path in the product and its exact signature is pinned by two `::regprocedure` casts in `phase_2e_task_command_ai_usage.sql`, a `has_function` type array in `ai_usage_rls.sql`, hand-written generated types, and the Deno worker — six-plus files that must move in lockstep, verifiable only through CI). *Stuff the versions into `p_patch` or `p_pre_state`* (rejected: both are hashed into the request fingerprint, so provenance would change command identity and break replay). *A separate stamping RPC writing the versions after the fact* (rejected: non-atomic provenance is a claim about a write that may not have happened, and it adds an RPC to the versioned inventory to work around a schema gap).
- **Decision:** Reclassify 2E-COMMAND-012 to Phase 2F. Record it in the PRD as **not delivered**, not as satisfied under a redefinition. Count it against the phase in the traceability matrix and the final report.
- **Reason:** The requirement's own justification clause — "since `ai_usage_events` has no column for them" — shows it was framed as a workaround for a ledger gap rather than as a first-order product obligation, and Phase 2F already has to widen that same table for task embeddings (PRD §22), which makes it the natural home for one migration that fixes both. Against that, the cost is a drop-and-recreate of a production-critical shared function during convergence-and-closeout, whose entire purpose is to stop changing things and verify what exists. A closeout slice that ships its riskiest schema change last has misunderstood what closeout is for.
- **Consequences:** Attributing a bad command to the prompt that produced it requires joining `ai_usage_events.created_at` to the deploy history rather than reading a column — weaker than the requirement intends, which is why this is recorded as deferred rather than absorbed. The residual risk is stated in identical terms in the PRD, `PHASE_2E_FINAL_REPORT.md` §7.1, `PHASE_2E_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md` and `TODO.md`, so no reader of any one of them can conclude the requirement was met. Phase 2E therefore closes with 117 of 122 requirements complete rather than 118, and the phase report says so in its first table.

## ADR-054 - "Activity" remains a task created by command; no activity entity exists

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.1 (PRD 2F-DECISION-001).
- **Context:** `PHASE_2E_PRD.md` SS22 left open "whether 'activity' deserves a genuine entity distinct from a task, which Phase 2E deliberately did not invent". Phase 2E shipped "activity = a task created by command" (`created_by = 'agent'`, `status = 'inbox'`, null source columns), and the question has stood unanswered since.
- **Problem:** An open question in a normative document reads as pending work. Leaving it open invites a future phase to invent an entity type, table, RLS policy and projection surface for a distinction no usage has demanded.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Introduce an activity entity now* (rejected: zero observed problems with the task representation, zero real usage evidence, and a second task-like domain would need its own write path - the exact sprawl Phase 2F exists to end). *Leave the question open* (rejected: an undecided question is re-litigated by every reader).
- **Decision:** "Activity" remains a task created by command. No activity entity, table, or projection is planned.
- **Reason:** The domain has no activity table, `entity_is_owned` has no `'activity'` branch, and the task representation has produced zero observed defects. Deciding "no, with a named reopening condition" closes the question at zero cost; inventing the entity would cost a write path.
- **Consequences:** The reopening condition is **observed real-usage evidence**: recurring, recorded user need for behaviour a task cannot express (its own lifecycle vocabulary, its own surfaces, or aggregation a task projection cannot provide), surfaced through the Phase 2F command-funnel reader or explicit owner request. Absent that evidence, no future phase should reopen this.

## ADR-055 - The semantic-retrieval evidence standard: two tiers, named exclusions, and a 90-day expiry

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.1 (PRD 2F-DECISION-002, 2F-MEASURE-002..006).
- **Context:** `PHASE_2E_PRD.md` SS22 defers semantic task retrieval to "should the measured baseline later justify" it, without defining "justify". The Phase 2F gate package (Gate 5 / F10) specified a measurable standard; the PRD encodes it; this ADR is its durable record.
- **Problem:** An undefined evidence gate is decided by taste. A threshold computable two ways is decided twice. A gate nothing fires is the same as no gate.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Build embeddings now* (rejected: zero real commands typed at decision time - the largest investment against the weakest evidence). *A single high threshold* (rejected: it would forbid the cheap experiment that answers the question sooner). *No expiry* (rejected: a permanently pending gate blocks nothing and decides nothing).
- **Decision:** Two tiers, measured over **qualifying commands** only (a model-parsed proposal for a real user; smoke-created users, disposable-fixture users and fixture-targeted commands excluded by construction - `product_events.user_id references auth.users(id) on delete cascade` plus fail-closed smoke cleanup plus the reader's own owner-scoping; `outcomeCategory = 'unsupported'` excluded from the qualifying denominator):
  1. **Spike tier - 50 qualifying commands / 10 distinct active days / a 14-day window** authorizes only a time-boxed (<=3 days) offline replay spike: replay recorded failed-match commands against an offline embedding index and measure whether outcomes would have changed. Never infrastructure - no pipeline, no job type, no backfill, no `source_type` widening, no index.
  2. **Planning tier - 150 qualifying commands / 20 distinct active days / a 30-day window / >=2 distinct real users**, with `no_match` rate >= 20% or no-match-to-creation rate >= 15%, may authorize *planning* a later semantic-retrieval phase. `no_match` means a qualifying command whose final outcome is `still_unmatched` or `creation_offered` (`src/features/task-commands/outcomes.ts:37-40`; no outcome member is literally named `no_match`). No-match-to-creation is computed from `task_command_applied.applyRoute = 'created'`. The distinct-user count is out of an owner-scoped reader's range and is verified by a privileged read at evaluation time. At one user, this tier authorizes nothing beyond the spike.
- **Reason:** The spike answers the better question ("would embeddings have helped *these* commands?") at the cost of a script; the planning tier guards the expensive commitment behind statistics a 14-scenario corpus cannot supply.
- **Consequences:** **Permanently non-authorizing, alone or together:** unsupported-command refusal volume, adoption/command volume, one-step rate, ambiguity rate, latency. **Expiry:** at 90 days without a met threshold, an ADR removes semantic retrieval from the active roadmap until a new demand signal appears. Because nothing in this repository fires on a date and Phase 2F ends before day 90, this ADR creates the dated expiry entry in `docs/TODO.md` - its date computed from the 2F.5 reader's go-live, placeholder until then - and 2F-OPERATIONS-006 verifies that entry exists at closeout.

## ADR-056 - Phase-letter reconciliation: "Phase 2F" names the approved PRD, and the displaced scope keeps its name

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.1 (PRD 2F-DECISION-003).
- **Context:** Two documents defined "Phase 2F" incompatibly: `PHASE_2_PLAN.md` SS"Phase 2F - Retroactive history and finish" (occurrence/date semantics, timeline placement, review invalidation, recurrence, pagination/responsive/localization closeout) and the Phase 2E closeout lineage (semantic retrieval + provenance, per SS22 and ADR-053). The approved `docs/initiatives/phase-2f/PHASE_2F_PRD.md` Revision 4 follows neither as written.
- **Problem:** In a repository navigated by documentation-first sessions, a phase letter pointing at two different plans is how a future session builds the wrong thing.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Renumber the new phase* (rejected: the Phase 2E closeout, ADR-053, `TODO.md` and three review documents already name Phase 2F as the successor). *Silently edit the old plan* (rejected: the displaced scope is real future work and deleting it would lose it).
- **Decision:** "Phase 2F" denotes `docs/initiatives/phase-2f/PHASE_2F_PRD.md` (One Write Path). `PHASE_2_PLAN.md`'s Phase 2F section and `TODO.md`'s Phase 2F line are re-pointed at the PRD, and the displaced scope - retroactive history, date semantics, review invalidation, recurrence - is recorded as unscheduled future work, explicitly gated on repairing review generation first (`TODO.md` M11).
- **Reason:** The successor documents outnumber and outrank the original plan sketch, and the sketch's scope was already rejected on first-principles review (`PHASE_2F_PROPOSAL.md` SS0) - not deleted, deferred.
- **Consequences:** `PHASE_2_PLAN.md` stops being the authority for post-2E phase scope; `docs/STATE.md` SSsource-of-truth order already ranks it below STATE/TODO/DECISIONS. Future phases are scoped by their own PRDs, not by the 2026-07 letter plan.

## ADR-057 - AI provenance (2E-COMMAND-012) is deferred past Phase 2F, behind an executed-dry-run reopening gate

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.1 (PRD 2F-DECISION-004). Supplements ADR-053; does not supersede its account of why Phase 2E deferred the requirement.
- **Context:** ADR-053 reclassified 2E-COMMAND-012 (prompt/strategy versions persisted on the operation) to Phase 2F, reasoning that Phase 2F would widen `ai_usage_events` for task embeddings anyway. Phase 2F's first-principles review removed the embeddings widening, voiding that coupling; the Phase 2F gate package then required a hosted dry run of the `record_ai_usage` drop-and-recreate before the migration could enter the phase (Gate 1). The dry run could not execute: no disposable hosted project exists, the only linked project is production, and Docker is unavailable on the workstation.
- **Problem:** Shipping the highest-blast-radius shared-function change without its dry run would violate the phase's own standard - an unexecuted gate is a claim. Dropping the requirement silently would repeat the drift this repository documents against.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Ship it anyway* (rejected: the standard exists because this repository has already paid for verifiers that never ran). *Run the dry run against the linked project* (rejected: that is a production migration against the function every AI path shares). *Abandon the requirement* (rejected: the residual risk ADR-053 records is real and undischarged).
- **Decision:** Provenance leaves Phase 2F. `scripts/phase-2f-gate1-record-ai-usage-dry-run.sql` - complete, self-verifying, refusing to run unless the target database is explicitly marked disposable - is preserved as the **reopening gate**: the work re-enters a future phase only after that script has executed and passed against a disposable hosted project, with the transcript captured to `docs/reports/`.
- **Reason:** The change itself was never shown unsafe; it is unproven, and the artifact that proves it costs one disposable project and one command whenever an owner chooses to spend that.
- **Consequences:** The ADR-053 residual risk persists and is restated, not absorbed: attributing a bad command to the prompt that produced it requires joining `ai_usage_events.created_at` to the deploy history rather than reading a column. `docs/TODO.md` carries the deferral with the reopening condition. `record_ai_usage`'s signature is unchanged through Phase 2F (PRD SS12), and no Phase 2F acceptance criterion references a provenance column.

## ADR-058 - The command-funnel reader excludes `is_synthetic` rows as a fourth mechanism, and that mechanism is hygiene rather than a trust boundary

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.5 before implementation, because it amends a merged requirement (PRD 2F-MEASURE-002). Supplements ADR-055; does not change either evidence tier.
- **Context:** 2F-MEASURE-002 states that "qualifying command" exclusion "works by construction through **three** real mechanisms, not email-pattern heuristics": the `product_events.user_id` cascade to `auth.users`, the smoke cleanup, and the reader's own owner-scoping. The requirement was written from the migration's ownership model. It did not account for a column the same migration already carries.
- **Problem:** `public.product_events.is_synthetic` has existed since `202607170024`, has its own partial index (`product_events_synthetic_created_idx ... where is_synthetic`) built precisely so synthetic traffic can be found and purged, and **every** remote smoke marks its traffic with it and asserts it (`scripts/remote-product-events-smoke.mjs:41,284,367,379`). A reader that ignored the column would count fixture traffic the repository has already labelled as fixture traffic - leaving a real, cheap, already-implemented exclusion undone while the requirement text claims exclusion is complete.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Implement only the three named mechanisms* (rejected: repository truth outranks planning text, and the ignored rows are exactly the ones the gate must not count). *Claim the fourth mechanism closes 2F-MEASURE-002's residual* (rejected: the authenticated Playwright journeys drive the real application as disposable fixture users and therefore emit **non-synthetic** events, so orphan rows remain possible - the residual is narrowed, not removed). *Rely on `is_synthetic` as the primary exclusion* (rejected: see the second half of the decision).
- **Decision:** The reader excludes rows with `is_synthetic = true` and reports the excluded count. This is recorded as a **fourth** mechanism, and it is explicitly classified as **hygiene, not a trust boundary**: `synthetic` is a client-supplied field of `ProductEventPayload` (`contracts.ts:267,507,521`) that reaches `p_is_synthetic` through the `recordProductInteraction` Server Action, so a client can mark its own events synthetic. Mechanisms (i)-(iii) are enforced by the database - a foreign key, a deletion, and an RLS policy. The fourth is not, and it is never the only thing separating fixture data from the gate: owner-scoping is.
- **Reason:** Using an existing labelled-fixture signal costs one predicate and removes a known contaminant. Pretending that signal is as strong as RLS would be the more expensive mistake, because a future reader could then be built to trust it alone.
- **Consequences:** 2F-MEASURE-002's "three mechanisms" reads as four, with the fourth's weaker guarantee stated wherever it is relied on. The residual the requirement owns - orphan non-synthetic events from a run that died before its cleanup - survives unchanged and stays pointed at 2F-OPERATIONS-004's cleanup verifier in Slice 2F.6. Any future funnel consumer inherits the classification: `is_synthetic` may filter, never authorize.

## ADR-059 - The end-to-end match baseline runs in an opt-in Vitest lane against the deployed project, using the real loader and the real scorer

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.5 (PRD 2F-MEASURE-007). Discharges the 2E-MATCH-018 scope caveat by like-for-like measurement.
- **Context:** 2E-MATCH-018 shipped a pinned match-quality baseline computed by `src/features/task-commands/match-baseline.test.ts` over a committed 14-scenario corpus. Its caveat, restated in `docs/reports/phase-2e/PHASE_2E_FINAL_REPORT.md` (section 3 and line 145), is that the corpus supplies `prefilterTier`, `tokenOverlap` and `queryTokenCount` **by hand**, so the rates measure the scoring layer against declared SQL verdicts rather than end-to-end matching - and that "an end-to-end corpus needs a database and therefore needs deployment". 2F-MEASURE-007 requires the end-to-end measurement, published pinned and labelled.
- **Problem:** The only piece missing from an end-to-end measurement is candidate rows SQL actually produced. The candidate loader and the scorer are TypeScript (`candidates.ts`, `matching.ts`); this repository carries no `tsx`, `ts-node` or esbuild register hook, so plain Node cannot import them, and `vitest.config.ts` includes `src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}`, so a credentialed test placed under `src/` would run in CI without credentials and fail.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Reimplement the scorer in plain Node* (rejected: the duplicate would be the thing measured, and duplicated business logic is what the single-source discipline forbids). *A one-off throwaway harness* (rejected: "published pinned" requires a reproducible pin; a transcript nobody can re-run is a quotation, not a baseline). *A Playwright journey* (rejected: PRD section 10 gives Slice 2F.5 no journey cell, and a browser adds nothing the RPC path lacks). *Add `tsx`* (rejected: a dependency for one file). *Seed the corpus through `create_task_command`* (rejected on measurement grounds: the RPC consumes a server-issued confirmation bound to a fingerprint and produces title-only tasks, so building varied statuses, due dates and relations would need a confirmation round per field and would still narrow the corpus below the scoring-layer baseline it must discharge).
- **Decision:** The baseline lives in `src/features/task-commands/end-to-end-match-baseline.remote.test.ts`, excluded from `vitest.config.ts` and run through a dedicated `vitest.remote.config.ts` by an explicit npm script. It seeds disposable fixture tasks with the **service-role** client - the pattern `scripts/remote-phase-2e-smoke.mjs:154-158` already uses, untouched by Slice 2F.4's revocation, which revoked from `authenticated` only - then resolves each corpus command through the real `loadTaskCandidates` against the deployed `list_task_command_candidates` and scores the returned rows with the real `rankTaskCandidates`. Seeding is fixture setup, not the contract under measurement. Fixtures are deleted in a `finally` with the deletion asserted before the process may exit 0.
- **Reason:** The measurement that discharges the caveat has to use the production loader and the production scorer, or it measures a copy. An opt-in lane is the smallest mechanism that reaches them from a credentialed run without putting credentials in CI or a transpiler in the dependency tree.
- **Consequences:** A second Vitest configuration exists; a Vitest case asserts that the default configuration excludes the remote pattern, so a future config edit that would drag credentialed tests into CI fails in CI. Both baselines are published side by side with their scope labels, and cross-scope comparison stays prohibited wherever either number is quoted.

## ADR-060 - The semantic-retrieval expiry is dated from the Slice 2F.5 merge, and the date is a repository fact

- **Date:** 2026-07-29
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.5 (PRD 2F-MEASURE-006). Completes ADR-055's expiry mechanism.
- **Context:** ADR-055 created the 90-day expiry and, because nothing in this repository fires on a date and Phase 2F ends before day 90, made the mechanism an explicit dated entry in `docs/TODO.md` whose date is "computed from the 2F.5 reader's go-live, placeholder until then". `docs/TODO.md:30` still carries the placeholder, and 2F-OPERATIONS-006 verifies at closeout that the entry exists **dated**.
- **Problem:** "Go-live" needs one definition a future session can verify without reconstructing history. A reader's first execution is recorded nowhere; a deployment date does not exist for a slice that deploys nothing.
- **Alternatives considered:** *The reader's first-run date* (rejected: unrecorded, unverifiable, re-derivable only from a transcript). *The date the PRD was accepted* (rejected: starts the clock before the instrument exists). *No fixed anchor, decided at closeout* (rejected: that is the permanently pending gate ADR-055 refuses).
- **Decision:** Go-live is the **merge date of the Slice 2F.5 pull request** - a git fact, permanently checkable. The expiry is go-live + 90 days, written as a concrete ISO date into `docs/TODO.md`, and `expiryDateFromGoLive` in the reader computes it from any go-live so the arithmetic is never done by hand.
- **Reason:** The merge is the moment the gate became computable, it is the only candidate anchor that is already a durable repository fact, and it is the one a closeout verifier can confirm without trusting prose.
- **Consequences:** The dated entry is 2F-OPERATIONS-006's verification target at closeout. If no threshold is met by the expiry date, an ADR removes semantic retrieval from the active roadmap until a new demand signal appears - the consequence ADR-055 already committed to, now with a date it can actually reach.
- **Discharged at closeout (2026-07-30), recorded here rather than by editing the Context above.** This ADR's Context says "`docs/TODO.md:30` still carries the placeholder". That was true when it was written and is no longer: `TODO.md` carries the concrete date **`2026-10-27`**, and `src/lib/closeout/phase-2f-documentation.test.ts` re-computes it from go-live `2026-07-29` through `expiryDateFromGoLive` on every CI run rather than searching for the string. The Context is left standing because it is a point-in-time record and rewriting an accepted ADR to look cleaner is the drift this repository documents against.

## ADR-061 - The Phase 2F closeout writes nothing to production, and the privilege posture is cited from executed sources rather than re-measured

- **Date:** 2026-07-30
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.6 before implementation, because it removes a mechanism the slice's own initial draft had specified.
- **Context:** `2F-OPERATIONS-004` requires a cleanup verifier that "proves zero fixture residue across every table the phase's probes touch, executed against the deployed project". The initial Slice 2F.6 PRD additionally proposed an opt-in `--posture` mode: mint one disposable fixture user against the deployed project, prove the task `SELECT` and the Option C reminder `INSERT` succeed as positive controls, then prove task `INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` and reminder `UPDATE/DELETE` are refused with `42501`, then delete the user and assert the deletion.
- **Problem:** The reasoning for it was sound in isolation — a denial proof is vacuous if the same session cannot write anything at all, so a positive control is required — and wrong in context. Three separate objections, each verified: (1) the property is already proven three independent ways, so the mode buys **no new evidence**; (2) the census counts *every* reminder in the project through `service_role` (buckets 6-9 plus the `overdueLive`/`distinctOwners` breakdowns), so the mode's own fixture reminder can move the numbers the closeout **stop-gate** reads, putting the gate downstream of its own fixture; (3) minting an auth user in production is not one row — `on_auth_user_created` fires `public.handle_new_user` (`202607160001:50`), writing `profiles` and `agent_preferences`, and `run_all_heartbeats` enrols the user by `select id from auth.users` (`202607170016:612`), writing a `heartbeat_runs` row every hourly tick until it is deleted. A run killed between creation and teardown leaves four table families and a recurring write, and three of those tables are not in the verifier's own scan list.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep `--posture` and order the census before it* (rejected: it still writes to production for no new evidence, and "run these in this order or the gate lies" is a footgun left for the next session). *Keep it but drop the reminder `INSERT` positive control* (rejected: without a positive control the five denials can all pass because the token is broken, which is exactly the vacuity `2F-REVOKE-004` exists to prevent). *Read the deployed catalog directly instead* (rejected as unavailable, not as wrong: `supabase db dump --linked` needs Docker, which this workstation does not have — the CLI reaches the remote database and then fails on the pg_dump image).
- **Decision:** The Phase 2F closeout **writes nothing to any environment**. `--posture` is deleted. The cleanup verifier keeps only the posture checks it can make write-free: the two asserted `service_role` refusals (`product_events`, `task_command_confirmations`), `anon` denied on `tasks` and `reminders` through the publishable key after a privileged positive control on the same table, and object-absence probes read from PostgREST's OpenAPI definition. The `authenticated` `UPDATE`/`DELETE` denial is **cited, not re-measured**, from three executed sources: `supabase/tests/phase_2f_task_write_grants.sql` on every CI `database` run, including its re-grant/re-revoke non-vacuity; the nine fail-closed post-deploy assertion groups of `202607300063` executed against the deployed catalog on 2026-07-29; and Slice 2F.4's deployment session, where the denial was observed on a session minted **before** the migration as well as a fresh one.
- **Reason:** A closeout slice's product is trustworthy evidence. Adding the phase's only production write to obtain a fourth copy of a proof already held three times is a net increase in risk and a net decrease in the honesty of the "read-only" claim the rollback posture rests on.
- **Consequences:** The `authenticated` denial's provenance is a citation and is labelled as one wherever it appears — `SECURITY.md`'s closeout section names all three sources and the Docker constraint. Any future closeout that wants a live re-measurement must first solve the census-ordering and fixture-footprint problems this decision names, not merely re-propose the mode. The verifier's write-free property is mechanical: a CI case asserts its source contains no `insert`, `update`, `delete`, `upsert` or `rpc` call, and the same case exists for the census.

## ADR-062 - A closeout generator must resolve what it declares, and derive ownership from the plan's own table

- **Date:** 2026-07-30
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.6. Supersedes the house pattern of `generate-phase-2c/2d/2e/2x-traceability.mjs` for future phases without editing them.
- **Context:** Four traceability generators exist. Each parses its PRD's requirement inventory, fails closed on an inventory drift, and then prints a hand-written evidence map beside it. Nothing resolves the artifact names, the slice ownership or the statuses those maps assert.
- **Problem:** That leaves the exact failure mode `PHASE_2E_SLICE_08_REPORT.md` §4.2 named and Slice 2F.5 then hit three times in one slice: a check that reads its own declared input proves nothing. A matrix can name a deleted file, attribute a requirement to a slice that never touched it, or cite a CI gate that no workflow step runs, and every generator before this one would emit it happily.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Copy the house pattern* (rejected: it is the thing under criticism, and Phase 2F's whole discipline is that an unexecuted verifier is a claim). *Derive ownership per requirement from the reports rather than the plan* (rejected: the reports describe work done, so a requirement nobody did would simply be unowned and unnoticed; the plan is where ownership is *asserted* and therefore where drift is meaningful). *Encode every artifact path in the PRD instead of the generator* (rejected: the PRD would then be the hand-written array, moved).
- **Decision:** A closeout generator (a) parses the requirement inventory by a **stated declaration-anchored rule** and additionally proves every *reference* resolves; (b) derives slice ownership from the phase plan's own epic table as **two relations** — `owns`, at most one slice, and `owed`, many — because a single-relation reading of Phase 2F §7 fires seven false failures on correct content; (c) **resolves every artifact and acceptance path on disk** and every `npm run` gate in `package.json`; (d) resolves CI gates by kind, a job-level gate to a workflow job *and step*, a suite-level gate to a file that is inside a path the workflow actually executes; (e) cross-checks the plan's gate ledger against the per-slice acceptance artifacts; (f) refuses a non-namespace requirement *declaration* while permitting a recorded cross-phase *reference*; and (g) proves each of those in both directions against fixture repositories — one deliberate defect each, plus positive controls.
- **Reason:** Each leg closes a class the previous generators could not see, and (g) is what stops the generator from becoming the next thing nobody checked. The distinction in (f) is load-bearing rather than pedantic: a guard keyed on appearance would red the build on the two owner-authorized sentences that carry the ADR-053/ADR-057 and 2E-MATCH-018 traceability chains, and the predictable repair would be to delete truthful cross-phase references.
- **Consequences:** `scripts/generate-phase-2f-traceability.mjs` is the reference implementation and `src/lib/closeout/phase-2f-traceability.test.ts` its proof. The four earlier generators are **not** retrofitted: their phases are closed, their matrices are historical records, and rewriting them would change accepted artifacts to satisfy a standard adopted after them. A future phase's generator starts from this one. The suite-level resolution in (d) also makes ADR-059's exclusion hazard mechanical for the first time.

## ADR-063 - An unexecuted gate cell is corrected, never back-filled

- **Date:** 2026-07-30
- **Status:** Accepted; recorded in Phase 2F Slice 2F.6. Applies `2F-PRECOND-003` to the phase's own gate ledger.
- **Context:** `docs/initiatives/phase-2f/PHASE_2F_PRD.md` §10 is the phase's validation-gate matrix, and its own footnote says "a cell counts only when executed; the slice report names the session". `2F-PRECOND-003` adds that "an unexecuted gate may not be cited as evidence anywhere in the phase". Slice 2F.6's convergence audit swept the ledger cell by cell for the first time and found three inaccurate cells, one of which claims an execution that never happened: 2F.4's authenticated-journeys cell reads `● regression`, while `PHASE_2F_SLICE_04_ACCEPTANCE.md` §11 enumerates all sixteen acceptance gates and not one is a Playwright journey.
- **Problem:** Two repairs are available and they are not equivalent. Executing the journeys now, against merged `main`, would make the cell true — but it would make it true *at closeout*, not in the session the cell claims, and the ledger's value is that it records when a gate ran. Correcting the cell tells the truth but appears to subtract evidence from an accepted slice.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Back-fill by executing the journeys and leaving the cell* (rejected: it would satisfy the mark while defeating what the mark means, and the next reader could not tell a same-session gate from a retro-fitted one). *Leave the cell and footnote it* (rejected: a footnote on a ledger cell is how an unexecuted gate survives into a final report). *Widen the cell's annotation to "regression covered otherwise"* (rejected as the same evasion with more words).
- **Decision:** The cell is set to `—` and the correction is recorded as PRD Revision 4.3-c, naming what 2F.4 executed **instead**: 14/14 production-flow checks through PostgREST with a real end-user token, plus the full remote suite at exit 0 — stronger evidence for a slice that changed zero `src/` files and whose subject was a privilege revocation, not a surface. Slice 2F.6 executes its own §10 journeys cell against merged `main`, which gives the phase an executed end-state journey proof rather than a retro-fitted one.
- **Reason:** The correction costs an accepted slice a mark it never earned and costs the phase nothing real, because the property the mark stood for was proven by a mechanism better suited to it. The alternative costs the ledger its meaning permanently.
- **Consequences:** The sweep that found this is now `A14` in the closeout contract and has a mechanical partner in the generator, which fails when a `●` cell's slice has no acceptance-bearing artifact to name its session. Future phases inherit both. Two further ledger corrections travel with it (Revision 4.3-a and 4.3-b), and the same audit corrected a misattributed CI run id in `STATE.md`/`TODO.md` and recorded a third flaky component test found while verifying it — neither smoothed, both filed.


## ADR-060 - The reminder lifecycle gets its own narrow SECURITY DEFINER boundary, and Phase 2F's revoked grant stays revoked

**Date:** 2026-07-31. **Context:** UX-12 (Slice G5), owner decision DEC-6.

UX-12 proposed row actions for snooze, cancel and reschedule "through a validated Server Action that writes the reminder's own columns", and set its own precondition: confirm, before implementing, which reminder mutations are reserved to the command path. The audit ran in Slice G2 and stopped the slice. `202607300063:92` revokes `update` and `delete` on `public.reminders` from `authenticated` on the 2F-REMINDER-003 determination, and **asserts that posture at deploy time** — the proposed Server Action would run as `authenticated` and be refused by the grant. Routing through `apply_task_command` does not rescue it either: that function reconciles reminders as a side effect of *task state*, and a reminder created by `createReminder` has no task to command.

**Decision.** One narrow `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC, `public.apply_reminder_command_v1`, admitting five named commands and nothing else. `authenticated`'s grant posture is unchanged: `select` and `insert`, the latter being the Option C authoring exception. The migration re-asserts the revocation in its own post-deploy block, so a future migration that re-granted either privilege fails to apply.

**Rejected: re-granting `update`.** The smallest diff, and it reverses a recorded determination, weakens One Write Path for this table, and would fail `202607300063`'s own assertion.

**Rejected: shipping the read-only half only** (links and localized status). It would leave the page saying "create only" while implying more, and the two read halves were already deliberately withheld in G2 for exactly that reason.

**Consequences.** A second reminder-mutation authority now exists alongside `apply_task_command`, which is what UX-12 warned about — so the boundary states in SQL which transitions belong to it (owner-initiated lifecycle) and which stay with the command path (derived reconciliation from task state), and the two are disjoint by construction: `apply_task_command` never runs from a reminder id, and this RPC never touches a task. It is reversible: the RPC can be dropped without touching anything else. Using `undo_operations` as the idempotency ledger obliged the slice to ship a registered compensation handler, because `undo_operations_registered_handler` (`202607250052:764`) refuses a row whose `action_type` has none — which is a feature, not a cost: the slice cannot record an operation it could not reverse.

## ADR-061 - Owner-visible snooze is a schedule movement, not the dormant `snoozed` status

**Date:** 2026-07-31. **Context:** UX-12 (Slice G5), owner decision DEC-7, and 2F-REMINDER-004.

DEC-6 authorized `snooze_reminder` as a named operation. The repository cannot deliver a firing snooze under the name's obvious implementation, and the investigation found this before any code was written.

`reminders.status = 'snoozed'` is dead vocabulary. Nothing writes it; nothing reads `reminders.snoozed_until`; `run_user_heartbeat` (`202607170016:508-512`) fires on `status = 'scheduled' and remind_at <= now()` and has no reactivation branch. The Phase 2F closeout says so in an executable assertion — `src/lib/closeout/phase-2f-census.test.ts:152`, *"never fire; no reactivation path exists"* — and measured the bucket at zero rows against the deployed project.

Worse than a missing branch: **the two halves of the Phase 2E/2F reminder contract disagree about whether `snoozed` is live.** `apply_task_command`'s close half (`202607270060:1570`) and `list_task_command_candidates` see `status = 'scheduled'` only; `private.undo_apply_task_command` (`202607270060:2047`) and `private.undo_create_task_command` (`202607290062:753,767,780`) treat `status in ('scheduled','snoozed')` as the live set. The disagreement is inert **only because no `snoozed` row can exist**. Creating the first one would mean completing a task no longer cancels that task's snoozed reminder while both undo handlers still count it — a behaviour change in four `SECURITY DEFINER` bodies Slice G5 was not authorized to touch.

**Decision.** `snooze` moves `remind_at` to a bounded future instant and leaves the row `scheduled`. `snoozed_until` is left null — writing it to make a dormant column look used would be decoration, since nothing consumes it. `status = 'snoozed'` is never written, and the migration's post-deploy block greps the function body to keep it that way.

**Rejected: writing `status = 'snoozed'` and adding heartbeat reactivation**, mirroring the live `pending_questions` pattern (`202607230048`). It requires modifying `run_user_heartbeat` — the Phase 1 delivery engine, outside the boundary DEC-6 scoped — plus reconciling the two disagreeing live sets, and it reverses a recorded determination whose census reads zero. Materially larger blast radius than DEC-6 described.

**Rejected: dropping snooze as a distinct operation.** DEC-6 named it explicitly.

**Consequences.** Snooze and reschedule perform the same physical write. They remain separate product actions, distinguished by command name, accepted input shape (bounded relative preset from `now()` versus an explicit absolute instant), validation, audit `action_type` and UI affordance — which is the shape DEC-7 requires. The word "snooze" no longer means "enters the `snoozed` state", and the CHECK member stays dormant and unretired: **2F-REMINDER-004 is untouched, and the closeout census keeps measuring zero.** The `snoozed` status is still modelled in the surface's state machine — as a state that accepts nothing and renders an honest label — so a legacy row would fail closed rather than crash a lookup.

## ADR-064 - Cross-feature object state gets one shared typed vocabulary, and the per-feature modules stay

**Date:** 2026-07-31. **Context:** UX-21 (Slice H), and the three slices that closed part of it before.

UX-21 named nine surfaces rendering a stored enum as a label. Slices G3, G4 and G5 closed Memories, History and Reminders by giving each **feature** its own typed `copy.ts`, which is the shape `docs/ENGINEERING_STANDARDS.md` names as canonical. Eleven sites survived all three, and re-auditing them found they share a property the earlier fixes did not address: every one is **one feature rendering another feature's state** — a task's status on a person's page, a memory's kind beside a person, an attachment's status on Files. A vocabulary rendered by four features has no feature that owns it, which is exactly why no feature-scoped slice picked it up.

**Decision.** `src/features/vocabulary/copy.ts` holds the object-state vocabularies rendered across feature boundaries: task status, project status, context kind, memory kind, attachment status, job status, job type, entity type. It holds **only** closed sets a `check` constraint already defines, one label per member per locale, typed `Record<Union, string>`. It contains no sentences, no page copy and no behaviour. Every lookup returns `null` for a value outside its own constraint and the caller renders a sentence; the module never falls back to the stored value.

**Rejected: folding the existing feature modules into it.** `history/copy.ts` and `memories/copy.ts` ship, are covered by 72 and 40+ tests respectively, and their labels are tuned to their own register. Rewriting them to consume a shared module would put G3's and G4's green suites at risk to remove a duplication that a test can pin instead. Seventeen tests assert the overlapping labels are **identical** to the modules that already own them, so two homes cannot mean two answers, and changing a label in one place fails the build.

**Rejected: a runtime fallback to `replaceAll("_", " ")`.** That is the mechanism the finding was raised about. It produces `recurring info` and `professional context`, which are neither Portuguese nor English.

**Consequences.** One documented exception survives and is argued rather than omitted: `public.jobs.type` has **no** `check` constraint — deliberately, so a new worker can ship without a migration — so an unknown type falls back to its identifier on `/app/jobs`, a route whose own subtitle calls itself a private technical queue. A `Record<Union, string>` makes an unlabelled member a compile error, so a future enum value cannot reach a screen unlabelled the way these did.

## ADR-065 - An inherited finding is measured before it is fixed

**Date:** 2026-07-31. **Context:** Slice H's accessibility carryover, and UX-32.

Slice G3 fixed an accessible-name defect in its own memory textareas and recorded the same shape as latent in `entity-edit-form.tsx`, attributing it to React rendering a textarea's value as a child text node so that a wrapping `<label>` absorbs the value into the field's **name**. Slice H inherited it as a fix to apply.

Applying it would have been three lines. Measuring it first — in Chromium's own AX tree over CDP, in Playwright's `toHaveAccessibleName`, and in the `dom-accessibility-api` the component tests use — showed the shape computes **correctly in all three**: a label that both associates by `for`/`id` and wraps its control is the case the accname algorithm is explicit about. The stated mechanism was wrong, and the fix would have been three lines of `aria-label` justified by a comment nobody could later verify.

**Decision.** A finding inherited across a slice boundary is reproduced before it is fixed, and the measurement is recorded whether it confirms the finding or not. Where it does not reproduce, the disposition is RETAINED with the evidence, not RESOLVED with a fix.

**Consequences.** Probing the neighbouring shapes found the one that does pollute a name — a label wrapping its control **plus other content** — and found three live instances of it in Settings, including the assistant-name field Slice F1 had just added. A defect that was never looked for was found by declining to fix one that was never verified. The cost is one probe script; the alternative was a fix that would have been carried forward as settled fact.

## ADR-066 - Closeout evidence comes from an assertion run over the whole route inventory, not from review

**Date:** 2026-07-31. **Context:** Slice H's route re-audit, and UX-34.

Fifteen slices reviewed this product at four viewports in two locales. Slice H's sweep — the same invariants, expressed as assertions and run over the current route inventory rather than the audit's — found on its first pass that the notifications bell, a navigation link rendered on **every page in the product**, never marked itself as the current destination.

**Decision.** Closeout evidence for a UX initiative is an executable sweep over the route inventory read from the filesystem, asserting the invariants the initiative established: route answers, zero horizontal overflow, exactly one `<h1>`, navigation present and marking the current destination, account surface reachable, no stored enum in visible text, no inert row carrying an interactive affordance, and 44 px touch targets on phone viewports. Exceptions are named individually in the spec with the reason, not accommodated by weakening an assertion.

**Consequences.** The sweep has exactly one named exception — `/app/jobs`, which `capabilities.ts` declares `context-only` and the type system excludes from `VisibleNavigationKey`, so nothing could mark it. Reading the inventory from `src/app/[locale]/app` rather than from the original audit mattered: five routes that did not exist at audit 1 were audited here for the first time. The cost is 168 page loads against a live database per run, which is why it is a closeout instrument rather than a per-PR gate.

## ADR-067 — A phase-start guard measures governing artifacts, not filenames

- **Date:** 2026-07-31
- **Status:** Accepted; owner-authorized as a correction to the guard's proxy, explicitly **not** a relaxation of the invariant.
- **Context:** `phase-2f-documentation.test.ts`'s A13 case asserted that no file in `docs/` or `docs/reports/` may be named to match `/PHASE_2G/i`. The owner then commissioned a definition study — a document whose purpose is to decide whether a phase should exist, which declares no requirement and plans no work — and the guard reddened the `application` job.
- **Problem:** The invariant A13 protects is *"the next phase must not be started before it is authorized."* A filename is a weak proxy for that. It is simultaneously **too strict** (it forbids recording an evaluation) and **too weak** (a declared requirement family in a file called `ROADMAP_NOTES.md` would have passed). The guard's own comment already drew the correct distinction — "a mention as future work is a record; a declared requirement would be a start" — and its companion assertion enforced it; only the filename half was wrong.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Rename the study* (rejected: it hides a real document behind a name chosen to dodge a test, which this repository's review culture would flag later, and it leaves the too-weak half unfixed). *Delete or withhold the study* (rejected: the owner commissioned it and it is the record of a decision). *Delete A13* (rejected: it is the only mechanical thing preventing a silent phase start).
- **Decision:** A phase is started by an **implementation-governing artifact**, not by a filename. Four signals, any one of which fails the build: a governing artifact **by role** (`/^PHASE_2G_.*(PRD|IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN|REQUIREMENTS)/i`, whatever else it is called); a **declared** requirement family in this repository's declaration shape (`- **2G-XXXX-000:**`); an **accepted** ADR whose heading names the phase; and a migration or source file marked as that phase's implementation. Permitted explicitly: definition studies, future-work mentions, candidate labels such as `2G-1`…`2G-4`, and append-only amendments stating the phase remains unauthorized.
- **Reason:** It measures the property rather than a symptom, and it is strictly stronger in the direction that matters — the declaration scan is now content-based and file-name-independent, so an innocuous filename can no longer hide a phase start.
- **Consequences:** Eight executed mutation cases prove each signal fires and that the permitted set passes; a guard whose failure modes are asserted is the only kind worth keeping, and A13's previous form had none. The declaration shape is deliberately identical to the one `phase-2f-traceability.test.ts`'s attribution guard uses, so the intentional `2G-READINESS-001` negative-control fixture stays a non-match and does not become a false positive. No other Phase 2F closeout assertion was touched: the same file's C1-re-raise case, the parity case, the funnel cases and the cross-scope-comparison case are unchanged, and all 142 tests across the five closeout suites pass.

## ADR-068 — The post-2F initiative order, and what gates public signup

- **Date:** 2026-07-31
- **Status:** Accepted; owner decision, recorded after three definition studies.
- **Context:** Two evidence-backed recommendations competed for what follows Phase 2F — operational readiness with C1 first, and ranked product candidates. A third input arrived from real owner use (the entity graph is unreachable), and a fourth from a product-direction proposal (per-user OpenAI credentials).
- **Problem:** Sequencing by risk register and sequencing by product gap give different answers, and neither had the other's facts. Meanwhile the hosted project accepted open self-service signup while every AI path spent the owner's key.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Operational Readiness first* (rejected: its items are each keyed to a trigger — public signup, first pilot, deployment — and none had occurred; a rate limiter cannot be validated against a surface nobody can reach). *Conversational Creation first* (rejected by the owner in favour of building it against the final credential contract rather than a project-key design that BYOK would immediately replace). *Deferring the entity work* (rejected on evidence: the Person surface is not truthful without it).
- **Decision:** Entity Graph Completion → BYOK implementation → signup hardening → Phase 2G — Conversational Creation → Phase 2H — Deploy and Operate → open self-service signup. **Public signup is a gate proven by a checklist, never a scheduled step.**
- **Reason:** Entity Graph Completion is deterministic, independent, and needs zero migrations and zero privileged boundaries. BYOK establishes the credential contract before any new AI surface consumes it. Signup hardening is what actually makes signup safe — BYOK addresses **one** of its fourteen controls.
- **Consequences:** C1's spend-cap recommendation changes meaning rather than being deferred a third time: under per-user credentials the owner is not the payer, so the protective control becomes an infrastructure quota owned by signup hardening, not a USD ceiling owned by a product phase. Three prerequisites for the first non-owner account do not exist and are named rather than assumed: **account deletion**, **admin suspension**, and **terms and privacy policy**. Hosted signup was closed on 2026-07-31 and verified (G-0.5); **BYOK is explicitly not a mitigation for an open signup path**, and no initiative may be cited as one.

## ADR-069 — BYOK's pre-code gates bind to what depends on them, not to the whole initiative

- **Date:** 2026-08-01
- **Status:** Accepted; owner decision, recorded as an append-only amendment to `BYOK_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` §0 (Amendment A-1). Explicitly **not** an implementer relaxing a gate.
- **Context:** The plan's §0 declares five pre-code gates and one absolute rule: *"No slice may start until every artifact below is in the repository."* G-0.1, G-0.2 and the G-0.3 procedure were executed and committed on `codex/byok-precode`. Two artifacts remained: G-0.3's provisioned keys for four environments, and G-0.4's dedicated low-limit OpenAI validation key.
- **Problem:** Read absolutely, the rule stops everything. G-0.4's artifact is consumed by exactly one lane — BYOK.3's live credential validation — yet it would block BYOK.1's tables and BYOK.2's resolvers, neither of which reaches a provider. G-0.3's *preview and production* values are likewise consumed only by a deployment that BYOK.1 and BYOK.2 do not perform. The absolute reading therefore blocks work on the availability of an artifact that work cannot use, while the implementer's own inference to the contrary was correctly refused as unilateral: this repository does not renegotiate a governing document's invariant inside a branch.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Hold the absolute reading* (rejected by the owner: it stalls two slices of schema, crypto and RPC work on a paid external vendor commitment they never touch, and it does so to protect nothing). *Let the implementer proceed on its own reading* (rejected: the plan is a governing document, and softening it is the owner's act, not the implementer's — the implementer stopped, which was correct). *Delete or rewrite §0* (rejected: it would erase the record of what the plan originally required, and the stricter reading was not wrong, only overbroad). *Generate preview and production keys now* (rejected: it needs administrative controls the implementer cannot reach without extracting or exposing credentials, and nothing yet deploys).
- **Decision:** Each gate binds to what actually depends on its artifact. G-0.1, G-0.2 and G-0.5 gate all BYOK implementation. G-0.3's **written procedure** gates BYOK.1. G-0.3's **local/test provisioning** gates execution of crypto integration tests needing persistent environment secrets. G-0.3's **preview/production provisioning** gates deployment to those environments. **G-0.4 gates BYOK.3's live validation lane and BYOK.3's closeout** — BYOK.3 may not be accepted or merged without it, and the lane may never ship marked "passed" while unexercised.
- **Reason:** A gate exists to stop work that would be dishonest or unsafe without its artifact. Testing an AES-256-GCM envelope needs a key, not a *provisioned production* key; a resolver that returns ciphertext needs no provider at all. Binding each gate to its dependents keeps every gate's protective force exactly where the artifact is consumed, and it makes the one gate that genuinely blocks — G-0.4 — legible instead of hidden inside a blanket rule that had already been over-applied four times.
- **Consequences:** BYOK.1 and BYOK.2 are authorized and proceed on local and test keys the implementer generates (four values, pairwise distinct, 32 bytes, base64, in git-ignored files, never displayed or recorded beyond presence/validity/length/distinctness). Preview and production provisioning becomes an explicit **stop with exact owner-facing commands** at the first deployment that needs it, with both the Next.js and Edge Function runtimes required to receive the same environment-specific pair. G-0.4's key acquires named constraints that BYOK.3 must honour in code — dedicated project and key, restricted permissions, USD 2 monthly budget **alert** documented as a soft alert and not a hard cap, lowest practical rate limits, opt-in lane only, `maxRetries: 0`, short timeout, a hard application-side daily attempt ceiling, and revocation after acceptance. The implementer stops before BYOK.3 while G-0.4 is unprovisioned rather than shipping a validation lane it did not exercise.

## ADR-070 — The per-IP throttle gets a column and a third secret, and G-0.4 closes with one thing still unreachable

- **Date:** 2026-08-01
- **Status:** Accepted; owner decision, recorded as append-only Amendment P-1 in `BYOK_PRD.md` and Amendment A-2 in `BYOK_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`.
- **Context:** BYOK.1 shipped `credential_validation_attempts` with exactly the three columns `BYOK-SCHEMA-007` declares, and **raised a conflict rather than resolving it**: `BYOK-VALIDATE-004` and plan task 3.8 require throttling per user *and per IP* over that table, and BYOK.3's migration budget was zero. Separately, G-0.4 — the dedicated low-limit OpenAI validation key — was the last outstanding pre-code gate, and `ADR-069` had made it gate BYOK.3's acceptance and merge rather than only its lane.
- **Problem:** Three governing statements could not all hold: a fixed three-column shape, a per-IP throttle stored in that shape, and a zero-migration slice. Any implementer resolution would have been an implementer expanding a governing document's schema inside a branch — the move `ADR-069` exists because the *owner* is entitled to make. Meanwhile a per-IP throttle stores something the product has never stored before: a signal about where a user connects from.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Per-user ceiling only* (rejected: it leaves the T-16 control — the product as an oracle for testing stolen OpenAI keys — mitigated only for a single account, and the whole point of the control is the attacker who is not limited to one). *Throttle per IP outside the table*, at an edge or in a separate store (rejected: it adds an infrastructure dependency this repository does not have, and puts the abuse evidence somewhere the residue verifier and the retention rule cannot see it). *Store the raw IP* (rejected: it is the most identifying value the product would hold, for a purpose that needs only equality). *Derive the HMAC key from `BYOK_MASTER_KEY` or the fingerprint pepper* (rejected for the reason `BYOK-FINGERPRINT-001` already gives: one compromise must not yield two capabilities — and a rate-limit pepper is the most widely-handled of the three, so coupling it to decryption is the worst pairing available).
- **Decision:** `credential_validation_attempts` gains **one** column, `ip_hash`, in a BYOK.3 migration; the initiative budget rises from **four to five**. `ip_hash` is `HMAC-SHA256` over a **canonicalized** IP under a **third independent secret**, `BYOK_RATE_LIMIT_PEPPER` — never the master key, never the fingerprint pepper, subject to `BYOK-MASTER-001…005` independently. The raw IP is never stored, logged or returned. `ip_hash` is used only for throttling and abuse control. Retention is bounded and enforced. Indexing supports concurrency-safe daily ceilings per user and per IP and nothing more. Requirements: `BYOK-SCHEMA-010…015`. **G-0.4 is satisfied** — a dedicated project and key exist as `BYOK_VALIDATION_OPENAI_API_KEY` with a USD 2 budget, restricted models, lowest practical limits and acceptance-lane-only use — so **BYOK.3 is authorized to begin**.
- **Reason:** Equality is the only property a throttle needs from an address, and a keyed hash gives equality without giving the address. Canonicalization is part of the contract rather than an implementation detail, because an un-canonicalized hash silently mints a fresh bucket per spelling and defeats the ceiling it exists to enforce. The third secret costs one more value to provision and removes the case where a rate-limit leak assists decryption. Raising the budget by one and saying so is cheaper than a slice quietly spending a migration it was not allocated — which is exactly what BYOK.1 refused to do.
- **Consequences:** Six secrets now exist per environment rather than four; local and test values for the new pepper are generated with the same evidence rule as the others (presence, base64 validity, 32 bytes, pairwise distinctness, and nothing else recorded), and preview/production follow Amendment A-1.2's point-of-use ordering. `credential_validation_attempts` acquires the initiative's first retention obligation, which BYOK.6's residue verifier must then account for. **One gap is recorded rather than discovered later:** measured at `0b62a5b`, no repository Actions secret named `BYOK_VALIDATION_OPENAI_API_KEY` exists and the name is absent from `.env.local` and `.env.test.local`, while `ADR-059` runs the opt-in lane locally and deliberately keeps credentials out of CI. The lane can therefore be **written but not executed** until the value is readable by a local run, and `ADR-069` forbids BYOK.3 closing with it unexercised — so this gates BYOK.3's **closeout**, not its start.

## ADR-071 — BYOK.4's second migration carries a second function, and "consumes no retry" is stated narrowly

- **Date:** 2026-08-01
- **Status:** Accepted; implementer decision inside an approved budget, recorded because the *content* of an approved migration changed even though the *count* did not.
- **Context:** `BYOK_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` allocates BYOK.4 exactly two migrations and names them: the `entries.status` literal, and the `claim_next_entry_interpretation_job` `create or replace`. `BYOK-JOBS-003` separately requires that a job reaching execution with no active credential move to a **declared terminal state** and **not consume a retry**.
- **Problem:** The existing `fail_job` cannot express that. Its terminality is `attempts >= max_attempts` and nothing else, so a credential failure on attempt 1 of 5 is rescheduled for four more attempts that will fail identically — spending backoff, lease churn and the retry budget a *fixable* failure will need later. Two migrations were approved; neither, as described, contains a way to satisfy the requirement.
