Supabase Edge Functions expose no deployment history through `verify:edge-parity` beyond `updated_at`, which is why the rollback target must be **recorded before** a deploy rather than reconstructed after one. `pg_cron` liveness is only observable from inside the database, which is what `2H-DEADMAN-001` exists to surface.
- **Consequences:** `2H-DEPLOY-001`'s runbook is written against this ADR and must carry a **per-layer** deploy and rollback section rather than one generic sequence. `2H-DEPLOY-002`'s environment contract inherits the byte-for-byte BYOK constraint as a named check. `2H-DEPLOY-003` and `2H-DEPLOY-004` are the mechanisms that make the asymmetry visible, and their value is now argued from a measured incident rather than from principle. Nothing in this ADR authorizes a deployment, a provider change, or a configuration change.

## ADR-088 — The deletion executor gains a second door for the reaper, guarded by a database-issued lease rather than by a target parameter

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Status:** Accepted — slice **2H.1**, requirements `2H-RECOVER-001` and `2H-RECOVER-003`. Authorized by the owner's Phase 2H execution authorization; migration `202608070079`.
- **Context:** `delete-account/index.ts` derives the account being deleted from the Bearer token the request presents and accepts **no target parameter**. That is what makes it structurally self-only (T-01), and it is deliberate. It is also why nothing could ever re-run it: an account stranded in `deleting` has nobody holding a session, so the one caller that door admits does not exist. The 2026-08-04 stall sat for two days behind an executor that was idempotent, resumable and unreachable — `re-runnable` as a property with no mechanism. `2H-RECOVER-001` requires automatic re-invocation, and no arrangement of `pg_cron`, `pg_net` or Vault can mint a user token.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Reap by having the database perform the deletion itself* (rejected outright: it needs the Storage API and the Auth admin API, neither reachable from SQL, and deleting `storage.objects` rows leaves the objects behind — it would be a second, weaker write path to the most destructive operation in the product, which `2H-RECOVER-003` forbids by name). *Mint a session for the stranded account from the database* (rejected: it puts the service-role key inside the database and manufactures a session for a person who did not ask for one, in order to impersonate them while deleting their account). *Route the retry through the `jobs` queue and `process-jobs`* (rejected: it makes deletion recovery depend on a worker whose own deployment is a separate open decision — ADR-086 — and widens that worker's capability to account deletion). *Add a target parameter to the existing user door* (rejected: it would destroy the self-only property for every caller in order to serve one).
- **Decision:** a second entrypoint on the same function, taken **only** when the `x-deletion-reap-secret` header is present, implemented in `reap.ts`, and requiring three things at once:
  1. **a shared secret absent from this repository**, which when unset or shorter than 32 bytes **disables the door** rather than opening it — the fail-closed direction, asserted by a test whose presented value matches the configured one and is still refused;
  2. **a `claimToken`** that only `claim_stalled_account_deletions` mints, that expires, and that the database issues **only** for an account already in `deleting` and already past its backoff. `confirm_account_deletion_claim` is the check, and the verdict is the database's rather than the worker's;
  3. **`executeDeletion`'s own step 1**, unchanged, which still stops with `lifecycle_not_deleting` for anything else.
- **Why this is not the generic privileged endpoint the standing invariants forbid.** It takes one action on one account, and the account is not chosen by the caller: the caller can only present an account the database has already selected, for a deletion the account's owner already requested under re-authentication and a typed confirmation. A caller holding the secret and an arbitrary account id receives `409` — proven by an executed test, not by review. The capability itself did not move: `reap.ts` calls `executeDeletion`, never `deleteUser`, so `deletion-capability-guard.test.ts` still finds exactly one home for it, and that guard now asserts `reap.ts`'s **absence** from the allowlist as part of the claim.
- **What this ADR does not do.** It deploys nothing. The reap door is inert on the deployed function until `DELETION_REAP_SECRET` is set on it, and the tick that would call it is inert until an operator populates two Vault entries **and** schedules it with `npm run ops:deletion-reaper-schedule -- --enable`. Migration `202608070079` schedules nothing, per ADR-082, and asserts that about itself in a postcondition. Until armed, the tick still claims, still applies the ceiling and still classifies a hopeless deletion as `stalled` — so the operator read answers *is anything stuck?* from the first minute while nothing is invoked. Half-armed fails quiet, which is the intended direction.
- **Consequences:** the deployed `delete-account` build and this repository diverge the moment this merges, and closing that gap is a **separate, explicitly recorded deployment operation** in the shape ADR-086 established for `process-jobs`. The phase whose founding defect was an undeployed worker does not get to create a second one silently. `2H-DEPLOY-003`'s `verify:edge-parity` is what makes that divergence visible, and `2H-OPS` will make the stalled population visible beside it.

## ADR-089 — Operator alerting is defined and deliberately not built, because a destination is an owner decision this phase cannot make

- **Status:** accepted (2026-08-07, Phase 2H slice 2H.4).
- **Requirement:** `2H-OPS-005`.
- **Context.** Slice 2H.4 makes the operational state *readable*: queue depth and expired leases, scheduled-job liveness with the tick and the useful work in separate columns, Edge Function deployment parity, error-sink volume by classification, account lifecycle distribution and stalled deletions with their reason codes. Reading is not noticing. The 2026-08-04 stall was visible in the database for two days and the only thing that reported it was a person looking at a screen that never changed.
- **The question this ADR closes.** Should 2H.4 also *push* — email, Slack, PagerDuty, a webhook, a status page?
- **Decision: no, and the reason is the destination rather than the work.** Every alerting mechanism worth having needs three things this phase cannot supply without inventing them: **a destination** (an address, a channel, a key), **a threshold** at which a state becomes an alert, and **an owner** who has agreed to receive it. All three are owner decisions. The PRD's value sheet signs ceilings, not addresses, and `RG-DEP-4` — monitoring and incident-handling adequacy — is explicitly an owner signature that is still unsigned. Building a notifier against a guessed destination would produce a mechanism nobody reads, which is worse than no mechanism: an unread alert channel is an *argument* that someone is watching.
- **What was built instead, and why it is not a consolation.** The reads exit non-zero when something needs attention. `npm run ops:health` returns 1 for an expired lease, a stale or never-reported scheduled job, a scheduled-job finding, or a stale, missing or orphaned Edge Function deployment; `npm run ops:account-health` returns 1 when an account has gone terminal in deletion recovery. That is the *whole* interface an alerting integration needs — cron, a CI schedule, a monitoring agent's exec check, or a person, can all consume it, and none of them requires a code change here. The decision the owner still has to make is where the exit code goes, not whether the state is computable.
- **Consequences.** `2H-OPS-005` is delivered as a **bounded scope statement plus the exit-code contract**, and it is recorded here so that a later reader does not mistake the gap for an oversight — which is the failure mode `2H-SINK-005` exists to prevent one layer down. If the owner picks a destination, the work is a consumer of an existing exit code and needs no migration. Until then, nothing in this repository claims that anyone is being told.


## ADR-090 — The owner retires the green ×3 CI rule and replaces it with a one-complete-green-run policy

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, given as an explicit instruction at the start of the post-2H session. It supersedes the ×3 convention for **all future work**. It does not rewrite any historical record, and every Phase 2H acceptance document keeps the number of runs that actually happened.
- **Context.** Phases 2C through 2H required a merge SHA to be green **three times per job** before a migration could deploy. The rule was written when a flake was suspected and never measured, and it was reaffirmed rather than re-derived each phase. Phase 2H then measured its own cost directly: 2H.6's closeout merge SHA `4a7e9bd` came back green on all three jobs on its first run, the owner instructed that the single run be accepted, and the record was corrected in four documents rather than rounded up (`b1d1d94`). What the ×3 rule mostly bought was **wall-clock**: during the 2026-08-06 Actions incident, four runs on one lineage were cancelled while queued having executed zero steps, and each repetition spent queue capacity to learn nothing.
- **Decision.** Two policies, by what the PR changes.
  1. **Documentation- and planning-only PRs.** **One complete PR-head CI run, all required jobs green**, plus a clean review of the complete diff. Merge. No merge-SHA run is required, and an already-green suite is not re-run.
  2. **Code, migration, runtime or deployment-affecting PRs.** (a) one complete PR-head CI run green; (b) review the complete diff; (c) merge; (d) **one exact merge-SHA CI run green**; (e) then the deployment or hosted verification. Deployment still never precedes a green merge SHA.
- **What replaces the repetition.** *Investigation.* A failure is diagnosed and the actual cause is fixed; only what is required is re-run. **A repeated CI attempt is not an acceptance mechanism** — if a test is flaky it is diagnosed and fixed or isolated, and the flake is recorded. Re-running an already-green suite to accumulate attempts is now explicitly forbidden, because it launders an unfixed flake into an apparent pass.
- **What this does not relax, and the list is exhaustive.** RLS; grant boundaries; migration parity (local = remote, verified after every deploy); destructive-action authorization (ADR-082 — scheduling IS authorization); secret boundaries; account-deletion invariants; fail-closed rate limiting; and the fail-closed signup rollout gate. A faster gate is a change to *how many times evidence is collected*, never to *what counts as evidence*.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Keep ×3* (rejected by the owner: measured cost, unmeasured benefit, and it makes an unfixed flake survivable). *Keep ×3 only for migration PRs* (rejected: it preserves the same laundering effect exactly where the stakes are highest — a migration deploy should be gated by a green run and a **reviewed diff**, not by a retry count). *Drop the merge-SHA run as well* (rejected: the merge commit is a tree no CI run has ever seen, and Phase 2H's founding defect was a divergence nobody measured).
- **Consequences.** `docs/reports/phase-2h/PHASE_2H_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` steps 2 and 9 are amended to this policy; the runbook is a living operational document, and leaving a retired rule in it would be the resurrection this ADR exists to prevent. Handoff §39 records the change so a future session does not reinstate ×3 from a Phase 2H acceptance document, all of which correctly still say ×3 about the past. A flake, when one appears, is now a **defect with an owner** rather than a cost absorbed by repetition.

## ADR-091 — One post-2H migration corrects the fresh-database scheduling defect, and it is not charged to Phase 2H's budget

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner authorization** given at the start of the first post-2H session, for exactly one migration with an explicit **post-2H rollout hardening** identity. Migration `202608070084_post_2h_retention_schedule_correction.sql`.
- **Context — the defect, restated precisely, because its shape is the whole point.** `202608050077`'s `do $retention_schedule$` block calls `cron.schedule` for seven jobs at apply time. Five are **user-content** sweeps: `sh-prune-terminal-jobs` (04:11), `sh-prune-notifications` (04:17), `sh-prune-product-events` (04:23), `sh-prune-heartbeat-runs` (04:29), `sh-prune-undo-operations` (04:35). ADR-082 then established the opposite rule — *scheduling **is** authorization* — and those five were removed from the **hosted** project the same day by an operator act. **The migration was not, and could not be, edited afterwards.** Migrations are append-only.
- **So the hosted project is safe and the chain is not, and the difference is the finding.** Every database built from the chain — CI's `supabase db reset`, a disposable project restored for the `RG-DEP-3` drill, any new environment — schedules all five and begins deleting user content at 04:11 UTC the next morning, with nobody having authorized a purge. Phase 2H found this (F-2H.5-7) and could not fix it: unscheduling takes a **sixth** migration against a budget of five, non-transferable.
- **Decision, and the accounting matters as much as the fix.** The correction is authorized as **one post-2H rollout hardening migration**, with its own identity in governance. Phase 2H's budget remains **5 allocated · 5 spent · 0 remaining**, reconciled per slice, and this migration is **not** counted against it, **not** reconciled into it, and **not** cited by any `2H-*` requirement. The traceability generator's per-slice map is unchanged, and the migration's file name carries `post_2h_` rather than `phase_2h_` so no guard that filters on the phase can silently absorb it.
- **What the migration is bounded to.** Unschedule those five names when present. It **schedules nothing**; executes **no sweep, no prune, no DELETE**; deletes **no user data**; and **drops, alters and re-grants nothing** — all eight built destructive retention mechanisms stay *built and unscheduled*, executable by no role, which is the posture the rollout gate and the restore drill both read. It is **idempotent**: `cron.unschedule` raises on a name that does not exist, so membership is checked first, and applying the chain to a database where the five are already gone unschedules nothing and still asserts.
- **The proof travels with the DDL, in the shape `202608070083` established.** Four postconditions inside the migration's own transaction: (1) none of the five survives by name; (2) **none of the eight** is scheduled under *any* name — asserted over the **command**, because the BYOK prune taught this phase that a job *name* is an artifact of which environment wrote the catalog while the command is what the authorization was about; (3) **every pre-existing job that is not one of the five still exists**, which is what protects the two authorized attempt-ledger prunes and every ordinary operational job, and which an absence-only check would let a catalog-emptying migration pass; (4) nothing was added. The file does not manage its own transaction, so a raise rolls the unschedules back with it.
- **Regression coverage is a chain-built database, not a hosted readback, and that distinction is the requirement.** `supabase/tests/post_2h_retention_schedule_correction.sql` runs in CI's `database` job **after `supabase db reset`** — the whole chain applied from empty — which is exactly the reconstructed database the defect lives in. A hosted readback would have passed before this migration existed and would therefore have proved nothing. The suite is non-vacuous by construction: it asserts pg_cron is installed, that the catalog is non-empty, that the command detector matches real prune jobs, and that all five sweep **functions still exist** — so "no sweep is scheduled" cannot be satisfied by having deleted the capability.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Edit `202608050077`* (impossible and forbidden: it is applied to a shared environment, and rewriting it would put the chain and hosted permanently out of agreement). *Leave it and rely on the operator script* (rejected: it is the status quo, and it requires every future environment to know about a script it has no way to discover — the restore drill's check 4 would fail correctly, but only for someone who ran the drill). *Fold it into the next phase's first migration* (rejected by the owner on timing: it blocks `RG-DEP-3`, since a drill that restores into a disposable project restores the five schedules with it, and no phase is authorized). *Also drop the five sweep functions* (rejected: it is a larger, unauthorized change that would destroy a capability SH.6 built and the Privacy Policy's retention table refers to — the defect is the schedule, not the sweep).
- **A second consequence, found while checking what else referenced those five job names, and worse than the first.** `scripts/verify-signup-rollout.mjs` computes `retentionSweepsScheduled` as *"all five user-content sweeps are present in `cron.job`"*, and `RG-QUO-3` passes on it. In any database built from the chain, `202608050077` scheduled all five at apply time — **so `RG-QUO-3` would have read PASS with nobody having authorized a purge.** The gate whose entire purpose is to require an *authorized* retention activation would have been satisfied by the very defect ADR-082 was written about. The hosted project never reached that state only because an operator removed the five the same day they were created, which means the gate has been honest by accident rather than by construction. After this migration the only way `RG-QUO-3` can pass is `npm run sh6:retention-schedule -- --enable` — an operator act, which is what the gate always meant. **Hosted behaviour is unchanged** (the five are already absent, so it still reads FAIL, correctly); what changes is that a rebuilt environment can no longer read one gate greener than the truth.
- **Consequences.** `AUTHORIZED_MIGRATION_HEAD` moves to `202608070084` and `docs/SECURITY.md` names it, both in this commit. `supabase/tests/phase_2h_retention.sql` section 7's comment — which correctly recorded that the five *were* scheduled in a chain-built database and that asserting either way would have been a statement about an environment — gains a pointer to this correction rather than being rewritten. `RG-DEP-3`'s drill can now restore a chain-built copy without arming five purges. **Nothing here opens signup, arms the reaper, schedules a sweep, or executes a purge.**

## ADR-092 — The owner authorizes Phase 2I — Foundation and Findability as a planning phase, and the phase-start guard retargets to the roadmap successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **planning only**. It does **not** authorize implementation, any product code, any migration, or opening public signup.
- **Context.** Phase 2H closed 2026-08-07 (44 declared · 44 delivered). Its roadmap successor per ADR-068 was *opening public self-service signup*, not another lettered phase — and that remains an independent fail-closed track. Separately, the owner wrote `docs/initiatives/product-ux/MY_BRAIN_MOBILE_FIRST_EXPERIENCE_PRD.md`, a seven-etapa mobile-first product direction, and a planning study reviewed it against the code (`docs/initiatives/next-experience/`). The study found the parent PRD's diagnosis correct and its structural remedy **further along than it assumed**.
- **Decision.** The owner authorizes **Phase 2I — Foundation and Findability**, scoped to the parent PRD's **Etapa 0 + Etapa 1 only**. Governing pair: `docs/initiatives/phase-2i/PHASE_2I_PRD.md` and `..._IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; audit, threat model, traceability contract and gaps document under `docs/reports/phase-2i/`.
- **Why the label covers only two etapas.** A seven-etapa phase **cannot close**, and a phase that cannot close cannot be traced, budgeted or gated — which would break the one mechanism that has made every phase since 2C verifiable. The parent PRD remains the product roadmap and is **decomposed into independently closable phases**, one per etapa, in the shape Phases 2C→2H already used: one governing pair, one migration budget, one closing report, then the next authorization.
- **The signed decisions this phase is built on.** Public signup is **not** a prerequisite — development and validation proceed against the existing controlled accounts, and instrumentation must distinguish the internal cohort from a future public-signup cohort, with current-population figures never presented as public acquisition or activation evidence. **Dark mode is out**, and not partially implemented. **The command palette precedes global lexical search, and search precedes Library** — the current problem is discovery, and building Library first would group twelve items into five and then build the two tools that make grouping largely unnecessary. **Original audio is discarded** after successful transcription and confirmed capture; that decision is recorded now as an input to the *future* Etapa 2 phase and **voice is not in Phase 2I**.
- **Migration budget: MAXIMUM ONE**, allocated to slice 2I.5 (global lexical search) **only if repository investigation proves schema work is needed**, and **it may remain unspent — `1 allocated · 0 spent` is the preferred close.** No other slice owns a migration by default. A migration must not be created to justify the budget; if one is spent, every object and index in it is justified individually; a second is an owner amendment. The decision is taken at gate G-2I.2 **by measurement, before any search code**. Investigation already established the input: **no `tsvector`, no GIN index, no `pg_trgm` exists anywhere in the 84-migration chain**, so this is genuinely greenfield rather than an extension.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Label the whole parent PRD "Phase 2I"* (rejected: it cannot close). *Skip a lettered phase and treat the UX work as maintenance* (rejected: it carries a migration budget, a threat model and a traceability obligation, which is exactly what a phase is for). *Keep the parent PRD's ordering* (rejected by the owner: it is the discovery inversion above). *Start with Library because it is cheapest* (rejected: cheapest is not the same as first, and Library is better judged as an information architecture once the user can already reach everything).
- **The audit corrected the parent PRD in three places, and the corrections are load-bearing.** `capabilities.ts` already exposes `home`, `inbox`, `work` (absorbing `today`/`tasks`/`waiting`) and `chat` as primary destinations with capture as the global centre action and a five-slot mobile bar; the `chat: "Conversar"` rename is **already shipped**; and Library's six members **already share `group: "context"`** — the grouping exists as data and is simply not rendered. So Phase 2I's navigation slice is **one label (`home` → Hoje) plus one grouping**, not a redesign. A phase that rebuilt delivered work would spend its budget proving nothing.
- **A13 retargets from Phase 2I to Phase 2J in this same commit**, by the rule ADR-083 and ADR-085 established: an accepted ADR naming the authorized phase is itself a start signal, so the guard must move in the change that records the authorization and the invariant is never unenforced in between. ADR-067's invariant is unchanged — *the next phase must not be started before it is authorized.* **This ADR names no Phase 2J scope**, and inventing one would be the error the guard exists to prevent.
- **Consequences.** The `2I-*` requirement namespace becomes declarable, which is why the retarget is mandatory rather than tidy. Phase 2I adds **no write path, no model call, no RLS policy, no grant, no secret and no external service**, so its threat surface is concentrated in two places: search as an enumeration oracle (T-2I-01) and the palette as a second write path (T-2I-02). **Two owner decisions remain open and block slice 2I.5** at gate G-2I.5 — whether `private`/`highly_sensitive` records appear in global search results, and whether `attachments.extracted_text` is searchable. **ADR-055 expires 2026-10-27 and is neither satisfied nor superseded by lexical search**; `2I-SEARCH-010` forbids embeddings, vector retrieval and generated answers, and the slice must state that at close. **The signup rollout gate is untouched** by this authorization and is re-read at the phase's close.

## ADR-093 — Phase 2I's search decisions: sensitivity, extracted text, and a measured refusal to spend the migration

- **Date:** 2026-08-07
- **Status:** Accepted — **owner decisions OD-1 and OD-2**, plus the outcome of gate **G-2I.2**, which is an engineering measurement the owner's budget rules bound. Phase 2I implementation is authorized; this ADR records the three decisions that shape slice 2I.5.
- **Context.** `PHASE_2I_PRD.md` §8.2 raised two product decisions it deliberately refused to default, and the implementation plan's G-2I.2 required the migration question to be settled **by measurement before any search code**. All three are settled here.

### OD-1 — sensitivity in global search

`entries`, `memories` and `attachments` carry `sensitivity in ('normal','private','highly_sensitive')`. The owner decides:

- **`normal` — searchable by default. `private` — searchable by default.**
- **`highly_sensitive` — EXCLUDED from default global-search results**, and searchable only after an **explicit user action** enabling a sensitive scope. That scope must be **visibly active** while results are shown, and must **not** silently persist across sessions unless an existing product preference contract supports it — none does today, so it does not persist.
- **The default state must not leak the existence or the count of excluded matches.** No "3 hidden results" affordance, no different empty state when sensitive matches exist. A count is an existence oracle wearing a helpful hat.

**This is a UX and expectation decision, not an authorization boundary.** The user owns every one of those rows. Ownership continues to come **exclusively** from the authenticated query plus forced RLS, and **no service-role search path may exist** — the sensitivity filter is a predicate on top of a boundary, never a substitute for one.

### OD-2 — `attachments.extracted_text` is searchable

Search may match inside extracted document text. Consequences, all binding: results render **bounded plain-text snippets only**; extracted text is **always data, never markup**; **no model call consumes it in Phase 2I**; OD-1's sensitivity handling applies to attachments too; **no query text, filename, extracted text or snippet enters telemetry**; a file result identifies itself as a **File** result and opens the existing file surface; and **no durable duplicate of `extracted_text` is created for UI convenience**. This does not authorize semantic search.

### G-2I.2 — the migration is NOT spent, and the measurement says so

`scripts/phase-2i-search-benchmark.mjs`, executed against the live database with **RLS active** and **realistic per-owner volumes** (5 000 entries, 2 000 tasks, 1 000 memories, 300 people, 100 projects, 50 companies, 500 attachments carrying ~20 KB of extracted text each), inside a **single `DO` block that ends by raising** so every fixture is rolled back by Postgres itself:

| Shape | scale 1 | scale 2 | Budget 300 ms |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sequential — seven domains in one `UNION` round trip | 338–354 ms | 669 ms | **FAIL** |
| **Parallel — slowest single domain** | **121–191 ms** | **244 ms** | **PASS** |

**Decision: zero migrations. `1 allocated · 0 spent`.**

The parallel shape is not a convenient reading — **it is the shape the requirements already mandate.** `2I-SEARCH-006` (per-domain result bounds) and `2I-SEARCH-007` (a named per-domain partial failure) are both meaningless unless each domain is queried independently. Having required independent per-domain queries, the wall clock is the slowest domain, not the sum.

**The recorded revisit threshold**, so this is re-measurable rather than re-arguable: re-run the benchmark when any single owner passes **~10 000 entries** or **~1 000 attachments carrying `extracted_text`**. Growth is linear (338 → 669 ms across a doubling), which is a sequential scan behaving exactly as expected; the two dominant columns are `entries.original_content` and `attachments.extracted_text`.

- **Alternatives considered.** *Spend the migration on `tsvector` + GIN across seven tables* (rejected: the accepted requirement is met without it, the owner's stated preference is `0 spent`, and seven tables of permanent index surface is a real maintenance cost incurred to answer a question the measurement already answered). *Query the seven domains in one `UNION` to save round trips* (rejected: it fails the budget **and** it destroys per-domain partial-failure reporting, so it is worse on both axes). *Drop `extracted_text` from search to make the sequential shape fit* (rejected: it is OD-2, and it is what makes file search worth having). *Use `pg_trgm`* (rejected: an extension this database does not have — reaching for it is already option B).
- **Consequences.** Slice 2I.5 implements **one query per domain, issued concurrently**, each owner-scoped in the query under the authenticated client, each bounded, each independently failable. The benchmark script is committed so the threshold is measured rather than debated, and it is safe to re-run against production because its only exit is a raise. **ADR-055 is untouched:** no embeddings, no vector retrieval, no similarity, no generated answers — it remains open and expires 2026-10-27.

## ADR-094 — The owner authorizes Phase 2J — Today, Capture and Attention as a planning phase, and the phase-start guard retargets to the roadmap successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-08
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **planning only**. It does **not** authorize implementation, any product code, any migration, any deployment, or opening public signup.
- **Context.** Phase 2I closed 2026-08-07 (61 declared · 61 classified · 0 undelivered · `1 allocated · 0 spent`), delivering the parent PRD's Etapa 0 + Etapa 1. Its roadmap successor is that PRD's **Etapa 2 — Hoje, captura e atenção**. Before naming anything missing, a current-experience audit re-derived every Etapa 2 item from source: `docs/reports/phase-2j/PHASE_2J_CURRENT_EXPERIENCE_AUDIT.md`.
- **Decision.** The owner authorizes **Phase 2J — Today, Capture and Attention**, scoped to the parent PRD's **Etapa 2 only**. Governing pair: `docs/initiatives/phase-2j/PHASE_2J_PRD.md` and `..._IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; audit, threat model, traceability contract and gaps document under `docs/reports/phase-2j/`. **74 requirements across nine families, eight slices.** Etapa 3 and beyond are out, by ADR-092's decomposition rule: a phase that cannot close cannot be traced, budgeted or gated.
- **The audit corrected the parent PRD in five load-bearing places, and the corrections shrink the phase everywhere except voice.** (1) **`/app/today` is a redirect** to `/app/work?view=today`; the cockpit the PRD asks for already exists at `/app` as `HomeDashboard`, composing four projections with capture at the top — so Hoje must be made reachable under its own name, not built. (2) **The attention queue already exists**: `list_needs_attention`, `security definer`, `auth.uid()`-scoped, keyset-paginated, whose **five reasons cover four of the six** sources the PRD lists — task suggestions, ambiguities, questions and recoverable failures; what is missing is a surface and in-place actions, since every item's primary action is today a link. The two absent sources are absent for different reasons, and neither is an oversight. (3) **Memory conflicts do not exist in this schema** — there is nothing to compose, and inventing a table for them would be the duplication the PRD itself warns against. (4) **The review domain already ships** with four periods; the gap is continuity from Hoje, not the domain. (5) **`configure_ai_credential` is deliberately outside the queue** because the analytics enum validating attention reasons is enforced *in the database*, so admitting it is a migration rather than a mapper change.
- **Voice: the cost question resolves technically, and the retention decision is what makes it cheap.** A repository-wide search for transcription, `MediaRecorder` or audio returns **zero hits in code** — the only mention is the parent PRD. `user_ai_credentials.provider` is `check (provider in ('openai'))`, a single provider whose API transcribes, so **the user's existing BYOK credential already authorizes transcription**: no project-paid AI, no project-wide key, no new secret, no new environment variable, no new vendor commitment. Because the owner's signed flow **discards the original audio** after confirmed transcription, voice needs **no storage bucket, no table, no retention class, no sweep and no deletion-cascade entry** — the draft lives client-side and confirmation goes through `captureEntry`, the write path that already exists.
- **Migration budget: MAXIMUM TWO**, allocated per slice — **M1 to slice 2J.7**, extending `product_events`' event-name check constraint and re-declaring `private.validate_product_event_properties`, because the allowed event names are a **database constraint** and no application path can record an unnamed event; and **M2 to slice 2J.4**, adding a transcription value to `ai_usage_events.operation`. **M2 is explicitly avoidable** — `'other'` is an allowed value — so `2 allocated · 1 spent` is a legitimate close; it is recommended only because logging an entire new AI capability as `'other'` destroys per-operation cost attribution and makes transcription invisible in `/app/costs`. A migration must not be created to justify the budget; reconciliation is **per slice, not by count**; a third is an owner amendment. No migration is created during planning.
- **Three deliberate exclusions, each recorded with its destination rather than dropped.** ***O Brain percebeu*** is **out**: no deterministic source exists, and the alternative is an unbounded proactive-AI contract billed to the user's own credential on a cadence nobody specified — against the parent PRD's own principle that *"silence is also a result."* **Attention snooze/defer** is **out**: it is the only attention action that cannot be composed from existing state, and it would turn a zero-migration slice into a schema change for a convenience. **Weekly and monthly review** stay parent Etapa 4.
- **Phase 2I's accessibility residual is discharged as slice 2J.0, a pre-code gate.** `2I-CLOSE-002` closed **partial** — component semantics, keyboard behaviour, focus behaviour and ARIA contracts asserted by test, but no browser-level audit, no screen-reader session and no Playwright journey over the new surfaces. It becomes the phase's **first** slice, not its last, because Phase 2I deferred it to the end and then did not reach it. The repository has **no axe dependency today**, so the lane adds one; CI's `database` job already runs a local Playwright spec against a production build on desktop and Pixel 7, which is where the lane lands so it runs on every PR. The screen-reader session is recorded **as manual** or closes as an evidenced negative — never reported as automated.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Fold the accessibility residual into Phase 2J's closeout* (rejected: that is exactly the ordering that produced the partial). *Ship it as a standalone post-2I PR before Phase 2J* (rejected as the smaller-looking but not smaller option: the lane must also cover Phase 2J's surfaces as they land, so a standalone PR would be written twice — as slice zero it is written once and inherited by every later slice). *Include attention snooze and dismissal to make the surface feel complete* (rejected: both need state the schema does not have, and dismissal without source mutation would create a record saying "resolved" while the entry says otherwise). *Rank priorities with a model call* (rejected: deterministic derivation from existing due/overdue data is explainable, controllable and free, and a model call would add BYOK cost, a confidence contract and a fail-closed path to produce an ordering the user can already predict). *Rename the phase* (rejected: the working name matches the audited scope, and renaming for style is churn).
- **A13 retargets from Phase 2J to the roadmap successor in this same commit**, the fifth application of the rule ADR-083, ADR-085 and ADR-092 established: an accepted ADR naming the authorized phase is itself start signal 3, and the governing artifacts are signals 1 and 2, so the guard must move in the change that records the authorization and the invariant is never unenforced in between. ADR-067's invariant is unchanged — *the next phase must not be started before it is authorized.* **This ADR names no scope for the successor**, and inventing one would be the error the guard exists to prevent.
- **Consequences.** The `2J-*` requirement namespace becomes declarable, which is why the retarget is mandatory rather than tidy. The phase adds **no RLS policy, no grant, no secret, no external service and no second write path** — `T-2I-02` is inherited and is stressed hardest by slice 2J.3, whose gate requires the guard to **fail against a planted second path before the unified capture surface is written**. **Three owner decisions remain open:** how `highly_sensitive` behaves on Hoje, attention, capture receipts, notifications and review summaries (**OD-2J-1**, blocking slice 2J.6 and gating 2J.1/2J.2 acceptance — Home, attention and Work apply **no** sensitivity predicate today while search excludes it by default, so two surfaces of one product currently disagree); what voice does with no valid AI credential (**OD-2J-2**, blocking 2J.4); and whether daily priorities may be explicitly user-selected, which would need storage (**OD-2J-3**, shaping one requirement only). **ADR-055 expires 2026-10-27 and is neither satisfied nor superseded** — this phase adds no semantic retrieval, and `2J-CLOSE-003` requires that restated at close. **The signup rollout gate is untouched** by this authorization, still reads **25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature**, and Phase 2J must not be read as progress toward it.

## ADR-095 — The owner authorizes Phase 2J implementation, signs the three open decisions, and fixes a non-transferable two-migration budget

- **Date:** 2026-08-08
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **implementation** of Phase 2J — Today, Capture and Attention. It does **not** authorize the roadmap successor, opening public signup, a third migration, or any project-paid AI.
- **Context.** ADR-094 authorized Phase 2J for planning only and left three decisions open. The planning package (PR #135, merged `2b36e1f`) declared 74 requirements across nine families and recommended a two-migration budget. This ADR records the owner's answers and converts the recommendation into an allocation.

### OD-2J-1 — `highly_sensitive` is governed by ONE central contract, with per-surface presentation rules

`normal` and `private` are **visible normally** and their behaviour is unchanged. `highly_sensitive` is governed per surface, and **every rule below is read from a single typed policy module** — no component may invent its own behaviour or test a literal sensitivity value on its own.

- **Hoje** — excluded or masked from default attention/content presentation; an **explicit, visible user action** is required to reveal; while a reveal is active it must be **obvious**.
- **Precisa de você** — the same default posture; **no hidden count and no wording that leaks what was excluded**; explicit reveal required.
- **Review / end-of-day summaries** — hidden or masked by default, explicit reveal required, and sensitive content is **never silently summarized into ordinary review copy**.
- **Capture receipt** — the user authored the item moments ago, but a mobile surface is still shoulder-surfable. The receipt **may state that capture succeeded**; sensitive textual content is **masked by default**; an explicit reveal may show it.
- **Notifications / lock screen** — **never** place `highly_sensitive` personal content in the payload or the rendered notification: no sensitive title, no excerpt, no task/entry/memory text, no personal entity name, no filename. Generic copy only (e.g. *"My Brain has something that needs your attention."*). Opening the app may lead to the authenticated surface, where the normal contract applies.

**No global "show sensitive content forever" mode** is persisted; no accepted preference contract supports one, so the reveal is local and transient. **Search keeps ADR-093's signed Phase 2I behaviour and is not redefined here.** Structural tests must make it impossible for these surfaces to drift apart.

Consequence, stated plainly: this is a **presentation** contract. Ownership continues to come exclusively from the authenticated query plus forced RLS, and the policy module is never a substitute for that boundary.

### OD-2J-2 — Voice transcription requires the user's own credential, and there is no fallback

Transcription requires a **valid user-owned supported AI credential**. With none, **voice transcription is unavailable**: the product shows the existing configure-AI affordance, explains that transcription uses the user's configured AI provider, and leaves text capture and every existing non-AI path fully usable.

Explicitly forbidden: recording audio and retaining it for later transcription; queueing durable audio awaiting a future credential; a project-paid fallback; a project-wide provider key; and silently subsidizing transcription. **This is what preserves the signed audio-discard contract** — a "we'll transcribe it when you add a key" affordance is durable audio retention wearing a helpful hat.

### OD-2J-3 — Daily priorities are deterministic, and there is no pin model

Priorities are computed **deterministically from existing task data**. **No model call** chooses them, and **no new persistent "pin as today's priority" model is authorized.** Candidate signals are limited to what the schema already represents — overdue, due today, an existing explicit priority, status, and blockers/dependencies where already represented.

The surface shows **up to three**. If fewer honestly qualify, it shows fewer — *silence is also a result* — and **hidden scoring complexity invented merely to produce exactly three is forbidden**. Manual or persisted daily priorities remain a future product decision.

### The migration budget: two, non-transferable

- **2J.4 (voice) — MAX 1.** It should remain **UNSPENT** if the existing `ai_usage_events` contract can *truthfully* represent transcription with the already-allowed `other` operation. **A migration must not be created merely to name "transcription".** It is spent only if executed investigation proves `other` would make usage, cost or accountability semantically false, or would break an accepted invariant — and then only for the narrowest correct vocabulary change.
- **2J.7 (telemetry/closeout) — MAX 1.** Expected to be needed, because `product_events.event_name` is enforced by a database `CHECK` constraint, but repository truth is re-verified before it is written.

**Preferred close: `2 allocated · 1 spent`.** No other slice owns a migration. A migration required by 2J.0, 2J.1, 2J.2, 2J.3, 2J.5 or 2J.6 is a **stop condition** requiring an owner amendment, as is a third migration. Reconciliation is **per slice, not by count**.

- **Alternatives considered.** *A single sensitivity switch applied identically everywhere* (rejected: a notification payload and an authenticated list have genuinely different exposure, and one rule would either over-restrict Hoje or under-restrict the lock screen). *Letting each surface decide* (rejected: that is the drift the central module exists to prevent, and it is how two surfaces of one product come to disagree — which is already the case today, since Hoje applies no predicate while search excludes `highly_sensitive` by default). *Offering degraded voice that records now and transcribes later* (rejected under OD-2J-2: it is durable audio retention). *Spending the 2J.4 migration up front for a clean operation name* (rejected: `other` is already allowed, and the budget rule is that a migration is never created to justify its allocation — the same rule that produced Phase 2I's `1 allocated · 0 spent`).
- **Consequences.** Phase 2J introduces **no new write path** — `T-2I-02` is inherited and is stressed hardest by slice 2J.3, whose gate requires the no-second-write-path guard to **fail against a planted second path before the unified capture surface is written**. It introduces **no RLS policy, no grant, no secret, no external service and no new provider**: transcription rides the user's existing BYOK OpenAI credential through `resolveOwnCredential`. **No durable audio object may exist**, and the absence is proved by a guard that re-derives it from repository state rather than asserting it. **ADR-055 remains open and unchanged, expiring 2026-10-27**; this phase adds no semantic retrieval. **The signup rollout gate is untouched** by this authorization — it still reads 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature, and Phase 2J is not progress toward it.

## ADR-096 — `ai_usage_events` has no `other`, so Phase 2J's voice migration is spent — and the premise that said otherwise was mine

- **Date:** 2026-08-08
- **Status:** Accepted. It records a **correction to a premise inside ADR-095**, not a change to the owner's decision. The allocation, the per-slice ownership and the ceiling are unchanged.
- **Context.** ADR-095 allocated slice 2J.4 at most one migration and said it **should remain unspent** "if the existing `ai_usage_events` contract can truthfully represent transcription using the already-allowed `other` operation". Slice 2J.4's investigation checked that against the schema instead of trusting it.
- **The premise is false.** `ai_usage_events.operation` is constrained to exactly eight values — `capture_extraction`, `semantic_search`, `chat`, `review`, `file_analysis`, `advanced_reasoning`, `background`, `task_command` — created inline in `202607160015:58` and extended once by `202607250055`. **`other` is not among them and never has been.** The `other` quoted in the Phase 2J PRD, in the current-experience audit and in ADR-095 belongs to **`error_events.operation`**, a different table created by `202608070080:93` for the Phase 2H error sink, whose sixteen values do include it. Two tables, two closed vocabularies, one conflation — carried from the audit into the PRD into the ADR without anyone re-deriving it.
- **Decision. The 2J.4 migration is SPENT**, for the narrowest possible change: one value, `transcription`, added to `ai_usage_events_operation_check`. Phase 2J therefore closes at **`2 allocated · 2 spent`**, not the `2 · 1` ADR-095 called preferred.
- **Why no existing value can carry it.** `background` names the routing profile's background slot and is used for work the user did not initiate; transcription is foreground and user-triggered, so recording it there would misattribute spend the user is watching in `/app/costs`. `file_analysis` belongs to `process_attachment`, and voice deliberately creates **no attachment** — the audio is discarded, so there is no file to analyse. Either choice would make the ledger say something untrue about the user's own money, which is precisely the condition ADR-095 named as the trigger for spending the allocation.
- **This is not a budget amendment and does not need one.** The owner authorized *up to one* migration for 2J.4. Spending it is inside that authorization; what changed is only that the escape hatch ADR-095 hoped for does not exist. A third migration, or a migration owned by any slice other than 2J.4 and 2J.7, would still be a stop condition.
- **A named limitation, and it is smaller than it first looked.** `ai_model_pricing` contains **no audio-model row**, and its schema is per-token while transcription is billed per audio **minute**. The first draft of this ADR called that a hole in the ledger. Reading `record_ai_usage` rather than assuming corrected it: the function already selects pricing by model and writes **`cost_status = 'unpriced'`** with a null `pricing_id` when no row matches. An unpriced operation is therefore a **first-class, already-modelled state**, not an omission — the event is recorded, attributable and visibly marked as not costed. Inventing an audio-minute pricing model would need a new column and a third migration, which is out of budget and out of scope. **Destination:** a future phase that decides whether per-minute pricing belongs in `ai_model_pricing` or in a sibling table. Until then `/app/costs` shows that transcription happened, marked `unpriced`, and the closing report says exactly that rather than implying full cost coverage.
- **The constraint is not the only enforcement, and the verification block is what found that.** `record_ai_usage` **enumerates the operation vocabulary in its own body** (`202607250055:118`), raising `22023` for anything outside it. A migration that widened only the table CHECK would have deployed clean and then rejected every transcription at runtime — inside the function, through the code path `src/lib/ai/usage.ts` swallows into a console line. The `do $$` block written for this migration caught that before it shipped, which is the entire reason the block exists. So this migration re-declares the function verbatim plus one value, the repository's established convention for a `case`/`in` list Postgres cannot extend in place.
- **The migration follows `202607250055`'s fail-closed shape**, deliberately and not by imitation: `drop constraint if exists` is fail-open, so a wrong assumption about the constraint's generated name would silently leave the old eight-value constraint in place, the ADD would succeed under a free name, and **every `transcription` insert would fail at runtime — where `src/lib/ai/usage.ts` swallows the error into a console line.** The `do $$` verification block is what turns that silent failure into a migration that refuses to apply.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Leave it unspent and record transcription as `background`* (rejected: it is the misclassification the ADR-095 trigger describes, and it would be invisible precisely to the user paying for it). *Leave it unspent and record nothing* (rejected: every AI call writes to the ledger before any dependent write — an unlogged provider call is a hole in the one record that makes BYOK spend auditable). *Spend it on `transcription` **and** an audio-pricing column* (rejected: two objects for two problems in one migration, and the pricing half needs a decision nobody has taken). *Treat this as a stop condition and wait* (rejected: the allocation already covers one migration for this slice; stopping would confuse a corrected premise with a scope change).
- **Consequences.** Phase 2J's close reads `2 allocated · 2 spent`, reconciled per slice — M1 to 2J.7, M2 to 2J.4 — and the closing report states that the *preferred* close was not achieved and why. The Phase 2J PRD, the current-experience audit and ADR-095 all contain the false `other` claim; they are **not rewritten** — ADRs are append-only and the audit is a record of what was believed at the time — so this ADR is the correction and the traceability matrix cites it. **The lesson is the phase's own rule turned on itself:** the audit's standard was that every claim cite the file it was re-derived from, and this claim cited a grep across all migrations rather than the constraint on the table it was talking about.

## ADR-097 — The owner authorizes Phase 2K — Conversar as the primary interface as a planning phase, signs four scope decisions, and the phase-start guard retargets to the roadmap successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-08
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **planning only**. It does **not** authorize implementation, any product code, any migration, any deployment, opening public signup, executing rollout residuals, or running ADR-055's offline spike.
- **Context.** Phase 2J closed 2026-08-08 (74 declared · 74 classified · `2 allocated · 2 spent`), and the post-2J product-event writer correction `202608080087` is deployed with hosted parity proved local = remote. Its roadmap successor is the parent PRD's **Etapa 3 — Conversar**. Before naming anything missing, a current-experience audit re-derived every Etapa 3 item from source: `docs/reports/phase-2k/PHASE_2K_CURRENT_EXPERIENCE_AUDIT.md`.
- **Decision.** The owner authorizes **Phase 2K — Conversar as the primary interface**, scoped to the parent PRD's **Etapa 3 only**. Governing pair: `docs/initiatives/phase-2k/PHASE_2K_PRD.md` and `..._IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; audit, gaps, threat model and traceability contract under `docs/reports/phase-2k/`. **Sixty-eight requirements across eleven families and eight slices planned for execution — none executed.** Etapa 4 and beyond are out, by ADR-092's decomposition rule: a phase that cannot close cannot be traced, budgeted or gated.
- **The audit corrected the roadmap in five load-bearing places, and four of them make the phase smaller.** (1) **Semantic retrieval already ships** — `public.match_internal_knowledge` (`202607160006`) is pgvector over `entry_embeddings` and `memories`, `security invoker`, owner-scoped, called on every chat turn after a BYOK gate and a single rate-limit admission. Candidate slice 2K.7 was never "build retrieval"; it was "widen it". (2) **Sources per answer already ship** — citations are computed, stripped of fabricated ids by a `flatMap` that degrades to a missing citation rather than a `TypeError`, **persisted** to `conversation_messages.citations`, and rendered as links. 2K.4's real remainder is provenance typing, freshness and insufficiency, not plumbing. (3) **A mature preview to confirm to apply to undo pipeline already ships, for tasks only** — 66 files, a preview whose immutability is the literal type `willMutate: false`, every changed field disclosed whether it changed or not, a TOCTOU fingerprint, a server-issued single-use confirmation the caller deliberately cannot name, a 24-hour undo and restore afterwards. 2K.1/2K.2 are unification and extension of one existing grammar. (4) **Continuity is the one wholly-absent capability** — the entire card, preview and proposal state lives in a client `useActionState`, so clicking a citation and returning destroys a pending confirmation silently; and it needs **no schema**, because `session.ts` already re-derives a command from a pinned `issuedAt` and reproduces the identical fingerprint. (5) **Conversar is the only content surface with no sensitivity policy, and the only one that *persists* an excerpt** — `chat` is absent from `GOVERNED_SURFACES`, and a 220-character copy of every cited source is written into a second table where the source row's classification no longer travels with it. That is a live divergence and it makes the phase *larger* in exactly one place.
- **ADR-055 is the binding constraint, and it expires inside this phase's own estimate.** It forbids `source_type` widening, backfill, pipelines, job types and indexes until an evidence threshold is met; the funnel is **empty**; and it expires **2026-10-27**, while the roadmap estimates Phase 2K at 9–13 weeks from 2026-08-08. Independently, `phase-2i-search-guard.test.ts` fails the build if the search feature so much as references an embedding column, a vector operator, a similarity function, or generates an answer — so lexical search and chat retrieval cannot legally be merged today. **`TODO.md` already records that letting the date pass silently is the one unacceptable outcome.**
- **Four owner decisions are signed by this ADR.** **(1a) 2K.7 leaves implementation scope.** Current retrieval over entries and memories is sufficient baseline; the phase works sources, explainability and quality over it. Slice 2K.0 must produce the permanent decision resolving ADR-055's expiry. No widening, backfill, pipeline, job type, index or semantic infrastructure is authorized. ADR-055's offline spike remains permitted **by ADR-055**, but is not a 2K closing requirement, is not an implementation of 2K.7, authorizes no infrastructure, and may run only under a later or specific authorization. **(2a) Mutating cards for tasks and memories only.** Tasks reuse the mature path whole; memories gain preview, staleness witness, explicit confirmation, result and a domain-appropriate undo; people, projects, entries, organizations and files stay read-only previews with links and sources. The PRD must state explicitly that *"actionable card"* does not mean every card type may mutate. **(3a) Migration budget: maximum one**, destined for the content-free Conversar telemetry vocabulary, budgeted at planning only, ceiling not obligation — `1 allocated · 0 spent` is a legitimate close — with vocabulary derived from one canonical source, every live enforcement point audited, CHECK and validator and the real writer all covered, producer to writer to RLS consumer proved, negative controls and non-vacuity proved, zero residue proved, and no repetition of the failure `202608080087` corrected. **(4a) Continuity by reopening and server re-derivation.** Restore position and the card's visual reference; request a new server derivation; re-check authority, fingerprint and current state; never treat client state as source of truth; mark a prior confirmation expired when the object changed; show the relevant difference where that is possible without improper exposure; require a fresh confirmation; never auto-reapply. **No pending confirmation is persisted this phase** — permitted persistence is limited to identity and navigation context, and may never function as authorization, a reusable confirmation, or a mutation payload.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Keep 2K.7 and amend ADR-055 now* (rejected: the funnel is empty, so an amendment would spend the expensive commitment against the weakest evidence — the exact trade ADR-055 exists to prevent). *Keep 2K.7 as the offline spike and count it as the slice* (rejected: the spike produces evidence, not user value, and calling it an implementation of 2K.7 would make the traceability matrix claim a delivery that never reached anyone). *Widen mutating cards to people and projects* (rejected: each needs its own preview, fingerprint, undo semantics and cross-owner analysis, and neither table carries a `sensitivity` column, so the masking analysis would not transfer). *Persist pending confirmations* (rejected: it creates a durable authorization-adjacent artifact needing its own RLS policy, expiry, retention class and deletion-cascade entry, to buy a property re-derivation already provides). *Defer the sensitivity work to a later phase* (rejected: the excerpt is persisted today and diverges from its source's classification today; a phase that touches this surface and leaves that is choosing not to fix a live exposure it just read).
- **The estimate went up, and that is reported rather than smoothed.** The plan estimates **11–15 calendar weeks** against the roadmap's 9–13, *despite* removing 2K.7 — because the audit added the ADR-055 decision, a sensitivity policy for a surface that has none, and a memory undo that has never existed. Slices are independently closeable, so the phase can be stopped between them.
- **A13 retargets from Phase 2K to the roadmap successor in this same commit**, the sixth application of the rule ADR-083, ADR-085, ADR-092 and ADR-094 established: an accepted ADR naming the authorized phase is itself start signal 3, and the governing PRD and implementation plan are signals 1 and 2, so the guard must move in the change that records the authorization and the invariant is never unenforced in between. ADR-067's invariant is unchanged — *the next phase must not be started before it is authorized.* **This ADR names no scope for the successor, and its heading does not name the successor at all** — a heading that did would start the next phase in the act of authorizing this one, which is why the guard asserts that property rather than trusting it.
- **Consequences.** The `2K-*` requirement namespace becomes declarable, which is why the retarget is mandatory rather than tidy. The phase adds **no RLS policy, no grant, no secret, no external service, no second write path and no new AI operation kind** — every operation it performs is already in `ai_usage_events`' eight-value vocabulary, so the usage ledger needs no migration. **Six owner decisions remain open**, two of them blocking: what happens to the persisted citation excerpt for `highly_sensitive` sources (**OD-2K-2**, blocking `2K-PRIVACY-003/004`), and whether a conversational memory undo archives or removes (**OD-2K-3**, blocking `2K-ACT-008/009`, because archive-versus-remove changes what the undo *means*). **ADR-055 is neither satisfied nor superseded by this authorization** — `2K-AUDIT-004` makes resolving its expiry a closing requirement of slice 2K.0, and `2K-CLOSE-004` requires its status restated at close. **The signup rollout gate is untouched**, still reads **25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature**, and Phase 2K must not be read as progress toward it.
- **Correction, 2026-08-08, owner-authorized and recorded rather than made silently.** The Decision bullet above originally read "eight **executed** slices". No slice has been executed and none may be: this ADR authorizes planning only. The phrase is corrected in place to "eight slices planned for execution — none executed", and the correction is logged here because editing an accepted ADR without saying so is the drift this repository documents against. The same false claim appeared in `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/STATE.md` and `docs/reports/README.md` and was corrected in all four places, not the two first identified — a wrong claim left standing twice is still a wrong claim.

## ADR-098 — The owner signs Phase 2K's three blocking decisions: no persisted excerpt, memory undo archives, and semantic widening retires at the ADR-055 expiry

- **Date:** 2026-08-08
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It signs three decisions ADR-097 left open. It **does not** authorize implementation, product code, any migration, any deployment, merging, opening public signup, or starting any successor phase. Phase 2K remains a planning phase.
- **Context.** ADR-097 authorized Phase 2K for planning and recorded six open decisions, three of which **blocked** requirements rather than merely gating them: what happens to the persisted citation excerpt (**OD-2K-2**), whether a conversational memory undo archives or removes (**OD-2K-3**), and whether the ADR-055 expiry retires semantic retrieval or renews it (**OD-2K-6**). A blocked requirement cannot be planned to a falsifiable slice, so the package could be reviewed but not authorized for implementation until these were signed.

- **OD-2K-2 — for new messages, the excerpt is not persisted at all.** The product stores only the **structured reference** needed to find the source, and **re-reads the source at render time**. Rules, each of which is a testable property rather than a guideline: the current classification is consulted again on every render; `highly_sensitive` follows the central presentation contract and appears **masked**, never dropped; a source that was removed, is inaccessible, or falls outside its validity window appears as **unavailable**; a historical excerpt is **never** reproduced in the clear; **no new copy of content is persisted**; the excerpts already stored remain a **named residual**; **no backfill belongs to Phase 2K**; and **no additional migration is authorized by this decision**.
- **Why this option and not the other two.** Carrying the classification alongside the excerpt was rejected because it keeps two copies of one fact in sync by convention — the same shape of defect as the three product-event vocabulary copies that `202608080087` had to delete, and the reason that defect survived two phases. Refusing `highly_sensitive` sources into the answer was rejected because the central contract's own doctrine is that exclusion makes a visible count a lie and any "n hidden" affordance is an existence oracle. Re-reading at render is the only option where the classification cannot go stale, because there is no second copy to go stale.
- **What it costs, stated plainly.** A render-time read per cited source, and a thread that renders differently after the underlying record changes — which is the correct behaviour and not a regression. The historical excerpts are **not** repaired by this decision; they are named and carried, because a `jsonb` backfill is a migration and the single budgeted migration is destined for telemetry.

- **OD-2K-3 — the conversational undo of a confirmed memory ARCHIVES it. It does not physically remove it.** Rules: provenance is preserved; the memory leaves the active context; authorization and owner scope are unchanged; the result recorded is the **true** one; and the UI says **explicitly** that the memory was archived or withdrawn from use, and **never** says it was deleted. An undo-of-the-undo exists only if the current domain can prove it safely.
- **Why.** It matches what the product already decided and already built: `memories` has no delete path by product decision — "archiving sets `valid_until` and leaves the row intact, because a memory's provenance is the reason the detail page exists and a physical delete would destroy it" — and `authenticated` **does** hold `delete`, so this is a decision rather than a limit. It also needs **no migration**: the audited lifecycle transition already exists and is already the product's signed answer to "this stopped being true". The one thing this decision forbids is the comfortable lie — a control labelled "undo" that leaves a row in place while implying removal.
- **Consequence for the card grammar.** Memory and task undo are now **honestly different** and must render as such: the task card keeps its 24-hour window and its restore-afterwards disclosure; the memory card states archival in its own words and claims no window it does not have. `2K-CARD-009` already requires a card to declare reversibility from its own value rather than inherit a sibling's; this is the first case that exercises it.

- **OD-2K-6 — at the ADR-055 expiry, the semantic-retrieval WIDENING retires from the active roadmap.** This **does not remove, disable or degrade the retrieval that exists today** over entries and memories, which continues unchanged. What retires is precisely the expansion that would require `source_type` widening, backfill, a pipeline, a job type, a new index, or new semantic infrastructure. Any future resumption requires a **new measurable demand signal, a new audit, a new ADR, its own budget and explicit authorization**.
- **The instruction that came with it, and it is the substantive half.** ADR-055 is **not** to be renewed artificially merely to keep a possibility alive on the roadmap. A permanently pending gate blocks nothing and decides nothing — which is the reason ADR-055 gave itself an expiry in the first place. Retiring the widening is therefore the outcome ADR-055 already committed to when its thresholds went unmet, not a new refusal: the funnel is empty, the spike tier was never executed, and the planning tier's statistics were never available at one user.
- **Consequences.** `2K-AUDIT-004` and `2K-AUDIT-005` are unblocked and their content is now determined: slice 2K.0 records the retirement, and `2K-CLOSE-004` restates it at close. Every 2K.7 candidate requirement still closes `not-built-by-rule`, now naming ADR-055 **and** this ADR. `docs/TODO.md`'s dated ADR-055 entry stops being an open commitment awaiting a decision and becomes an entry with a decided outcome and an execution date.

- **Consequences across the package.** `2K-PRIVACY-003/004`, `2K-ACT-008/009` and `2K-AUDIT-004/005` move from **blocked** to **specified**, so no Phase 2K requirement is blocked on an unsigned decision. Three decisions remain and none of them blocks: **OD-2K-1** (the editable-parameter set per action), **OD-2K-4** (which state may produce a suggestion), and **OD-2K-5** (whether task-command confirmations expire independently of object change, which `2K-AUDIT-002` measures in slice 2K.0 rather than assuming). **The migration ceiling is unchanged at one**, and all three decisions were chosen partly because none of them spends it. **Implementation remains unauthorized**, hosted parity remains `202608080087`, and the signup rollout gate is untouched at **25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature**.

## ADR-099 — The semantic-retrieval widening is retired from the active roadmap; ADR-055 expires unmet and is not renewed

- **Date:** 2026-08-08
- **Status:** Accepted — executes the decision **already signed** as OD-2K-6 in ADR-098. This ADR **records** the retirement; it does not re-decide it. Recorded during Phase 2K slice **2K.0**, the only slice the owner has authorized for implementation.
- **Context.** ADR-055 (2026-07-29) created a two-tier evidence standard for semantic retrieval and gave itself a **90-day expiry**, dated by ADR-060 from the Slice 2F.5 merge to **2026-10-27**. ADR-055's own reasoning for the expiry was that "a permanently pending gate blocks nothing and decides nothing", and it committed in advance to the consequence: "at 90 days without a met threshold, an ADR removes semantic retrieval from the active roadmap until a new demand signal appears." This is that ADR.
- **The thresholds were never met, and the record is unambiguous.** The spike tier needed 50 qualifying commands over 10 distinct active days in a 14-day window; the planning tier needed 150 commands, 20 active days, 30 days and **≥2 distinct real users**, with a `no_match` rate ≥20% or a no-match-to-creation rate ≥15%. **The funnel is empty.** Phase 2G closed measuring it and found nothing; Phases 2H, 2I and 2J each added no retrieval and fed it nothing, and each restated the expiry as neither satisfied nor superseded. At one real user the planning tier authorizes nothing beyond the spike by ADR-055's own terms, and the spike was never executed.
- **Decision.** At the ADR-055 expiry the **widening** of semantic retrieval leaves the active roadmap. Specifically retired: `source_type` widening beyond `entry` and `memory`; embedding backfill; any retrieval pipeline; any new job type; any new index; and any new semantic infrastructure.
- **What is explicitly NOT retired, and this is the load-bearing half.** The semantic retrieval that **ships today** is untouched. `public.match_internal_knowledge` (`202607160006`) continues to serve every grounded chat answer over `entry_embeddings` and `memories`. It is **not removed, not disabled, not degraded, and not deprecated.** Embedding generation on capture and on memory edit continues. The `semantic_search` operation in `ai_usage_events` continues. A future reader must not mistake "the widening retired" for "retrieval was switched off".
- **No renewal date is written, deliberately.** Creating a new date to keep the possibility alive on the roadmap would rebuild the permanently-pending gate ADR-055 existed to avoid, and OD-2K-6 forbids it by name. Resumption requires **all five**: a new measurable demand signal, a new current-experience audit, a new accepted ADR, its own migration budget, and explicit owner authorization. Nothing about this ADR pre-authorizes any of them.
- **The spike's status, stated because it is the likeliest misreading.** ADR-055's time-boxed offline replay spike remains permitted **by ADR-055, for as long as ADR-055 stands**. It is not a Phase 2K deliverable, not an implementation of the removed slice 2K.7, authorizes no infrastructure, and is not a route back into the retired scope. It requires its own authorization.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Renew with a new 90-day window* (rejected: it is the artificial renewal OD-2K-6 forbids, and nothing has changed that would make the funnel fill in the next 90 days that did not apply to the last 90). *Let the date pass without an ADR* (rejected: `TODO.md` records that letting the date pass silently is the one unacceptable outcome, and an expiry nobody executes is the pending gate again). *Retire the current retrieval too, for consistency* (rejected as actively harmful: it ships, it works, it is owner-scoped and RLS-backed, and removing a working capability to tidy a roadmap would be the most expensive possible reading of "retire").
- **Consequences.** `2K-AUDIT-004` and `2K-AUDIT-005` close on this record. Every Phase 2K slice-2K.7 candidate requirement closes `not-built-by-rule`, naming ADR-055, OD-2K-A and this ADR. `docs/TODO.md`'s dated entry stops being an open commitment and becomes a decided outcome. **Slice 2K.0 spends no migration and this ADR authorizes none.** The boundary the retirement leaves behind is now covered by a test rather than only by prose: `supabase/tests/phase_2k_knowledge_retrieval_ownership.sql` asserts that the surviving retrieval is `security invoker`, owner-scoped in both arms, refused to `anon`, and non-vacuously so — the gap the Phase 2K audit named at §5.4.
- **Correction, 2026-08-08.** This ADR's Consequences originally said the suite asserts the retrieval is "refused to `anon`". CI proved that reading wrong: `anon` **holds** `EXECUTE` through `PUBLIC`, because revoking from `anon` does not remove a `PUBLIC` grant. The boundary that actually refuses `anon` is the **table** grant — the hosted probe said so directly, returning `42501 permission denied for table entry_embeddings`. The suite now asserts that, and asserts the function-level privilege in neither direction. The sentence above is left standing as the point-in-time record it is; this bullet is the correction.

## ADR-100 — Conversation continuity re-derives with a new clock and always re-asks; the continuity payload can never be an authorization

- **Date:** 2026-08-08
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, taken on direct review of the slice 2K.0 branch before merge. It records a **planning contract**; it authorizes no implementation. Slices 2K.1–2K.8 remain unauthorized.
- **Context.** Slice 2K.0's measurement M1 established that task-command confirmations have **no TTL** and become inapplicable on facts, and — fact 4 — that `issuedAt` reaches the request fingerprint through `observedBefore`, so a derivation minted with a new clock cannot consume an earlier confirmation. That measurement is correct and is not disturbed by this ADR.
- **Problem.** The **consequence** drawn from that measurement was not. The first draft of `2K-CONT-006` and of the 2K.0 acceptance report said continuity *"must carry the original pinned `issuedAt` … or state plainly that a fresh confirmation is required"*. That contradicted the same package's own `2K-CONT-003` — a closed identifier-only payload carrying no confirmation id, operation key, fingerprint, patch or mutation payload — and OD-2K-D's rule that permitted persistence "may never function as authorization, a reusable confirmation, or a mutation payload". **Transporting `issuedAt` *in order to keep an earlier confirmation consumable* is transporting an authorization**, whatever it is called: the clock is a hashed fingerprint input, so carrying it is carrying the thing that makes the old confirmation match. And offering the implementer a *choice* between the two behaviours was the worse half of the defect — a contract with two readings is not a contract.
- **Decision.** **Returning to a conversation always re-derives on the server, always with a new `issuedAt`, and always requires a fresh confirmation.** There is no branch in which a prior confirmation is reused. The continuity payload is restricted to the identifiers needed to locate the conversation, locate the message, locate the object and restore the visual position, and carries **none** of: confirmation id, operation key, `issuedAt`, `observedBefore`, request fingerprint, patch, computed preview, mutation payload, authorization, reusable confirmation. Object changed and provable from the authorized identifiers → the `expired` presentation state, only the safely re-derivable difference, a new confirmation. Object unchanged → the re-derived preview and a new confirmation request, rendered as **normal rather than as an error**, never claiming the earlier confirmation still holds. A safe difference not reconstructible without forbidden state → **do not invent one**; say generically that the earlier preview no longer applies, show the new one, re-ask.
- **Reason.** The property required is not that the payload is hard to forge but that it is **incapable** of authorizing — the same reasoning ADR-047 used when it refused to let a caller name a confirmation token, because "a token a caller can name is a token a caller can guess at". A new clock on every return makes the earlier confirmation unusable **by construction**, which converts a rule someone must remember into a mechanism that cannot be bypassed. The cost is one extra confirmation per return, which is the correct price and is also the honest UX: the user is being asked about a preview that was genuinely recomputed.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Carry `issuedAt` so an unchanged object needs no second confirmation* (rejected: this is the defect — it is a reusable authorization in a payload the same package declares closed, and it optimises away the one act that makes a destructive command deliberate). *Give the confirmation a TTL so staleness is time-bounded* (rejected: ADR-047 refused an expiry on three grounds, the decisive one being that `2E-UX-001`'s outcome vocabulary is closed and has no member for it; and 2K.0 is explicitly forbidden from creating a TTL). *Persist the pending confirmation so it can be restored intact* (rejected by OD-2K-D: a durable authorization-adjacent artifact needing its own RLS policy, expiry, retention class and deletion-cascade entry, to buy a property re-derivation already provides). *Leave the choice to the implementer* (rejected: that was the second half of the original defect).
- **Consequences.** `2K-CONT-003`, `2K-CONT-005`, `2K-CONT-006` and `2K-ACT-005` now have a single interpretation, and slice 2K.3's tests must assert the payload against a **planted instance of each** forbidden field rather than against one representative. **A differing fingerprint after a return is the expected result, not a failure** — 2K.3's stopping condition was inverted accordingly, because the previous one would have stopped the slice on correct behaviour.
