The traceability contract gains **R17**, refusing any plan or implementation that transports those fields *and* any text offering a choice between transporting a clock and re-asking. **An earlier confirmation row may persist as history** — there is no TTL and none is created — but continuity does not transport what would consume it, the product never reuses it, and it grants the new card no authority; whether such rows should ever be cleaned is a **separate residual** outside slice 2K.0. **`TASK_COMMAND_OUTCOMES` is not widened, no TTL is created, no migration is created, and no new persistence for pending confirmations is introduced.** M1's measured facts are unchanged: no `expires_at`, no TTL, no age check, `55P03` / `2E_CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED` / `2E_IDEMPOTENCY_MISMATCH`, no `expired` member, and a later failure in the transaction does not consume the confirmation.

## ADR-101 — The owner authorizes Phase 2K implementation through closeout, signs OD-2K-1 and OD-2K-4, and authorizes deploying the single telemetry migration if one is created

- **Date:** 2026-08-09
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, given as a standing authorization for the remainder of Phase 2K. It authorizes slices **2K.1 through 2K.8**, the final traceability matrix and closing report, and — **only** within the one-migration ceiling and **only** for the content-free Conversar telemetry vocabulary of slice 2K.8 — creating, merging and **deploying** that migration. It authorizes nothing else: no successor phase, no public signup, no SMTP, no restore drill, no retention scheduling, no rollout residual, no external integration, no semantic widening, no backfill, no ADR-055 spike, and no second migration.
- **Context.** ADR-097 authorized Phase 2K for **planning only** and stated that implementation "requires a separate, explicit owner decision recorded as an accepted ADR". ADR-098 signed the three decisions that **blocked** requirements; ADR-099 recorded the ADR-055 retirement; slice 2K.0 executed the three measurements and merged as PR #146 at `9d93263`, CI green on the exact merge SHA, hosted parity unchanged at `202608080087` across 87 migrations. Gate **G5** — "explicit owner approval after review of this package" — is what this ADR discharges. The two decisions that merely **gated** rather than blocked, OD-2K-1 and OD-2K-4, are signed below so no slice starts on an unsigned decision.

- **OD-2K-1 — editable parameters are a closed, per-action set, and an edit always re-derives.** Gates `2K-ACT-003/004`. The rules, each a testable property rather than a guideline: an arbitrary patch is **never** accepted; a field name supplied by the caller is **never** accepted; free-form JSON is **never** accepted; no field the current command schema does not already recognise is editable; for tasks, each action exposes **only** parameters that already belong to that action's typed command; any parameter whose authority and validation are not already proved is **excluded by default**; an edit **re-derives the command** and produces a **new fingerprint**, which invalidates any earlier confirmation; destructive changes still require a server-issued confirmation; a memory create permits editing **only the content the user proposed**, and `kind`, confidence, provenance, ownership, lifecycle state and memory metadata are **not** editable from the card; and nothing about a person, project, entry, organization or file is editable at all. Where a parameter is arguable, it is **omitted rather than widened**.
- **Why this shape.** The alternative — free-text patch editing — was refused in the PRD before this ADR existed, and for the reason ADR-047 gave about confirmation tokens: the property required is not that a bad edit is unlikely but that the surface is **incapable** of expressing one. A closed per-action set derived from the existing typed command achieves that without adding authority, and the re-derivation rule means an edited preview can never be applied under the fingerprint of the preview the user actually read — which is `2K-CONT-005`'s mechanism reused rather than a second one invented.

- **OD-2K-4 — suggestions are deterministic, contextual, and at most three.** Gates `2K-SUGG-001`. Permitted sources: the current route; the current conversation; the user's current state; the already-authorized Hoje / Trabalho / Precisa-de-você projections; people and projects the user can read **under RLS**; and context explicitly present in this interaction. The rules: no long fixed list; **retrieved text is never a command**; a suggestion never carries a mutation payload; a retrieved source may never propose an action; a suggestion may name a person or project **only** when the user has current read access **and** the sensitivity policy permits it, with the most protective level following the central masking contract; a removed, foreign or inaccessible object yields `unavailable` with **no differentiable existence**; suggestion **text** never enters telemetry; a person or project **name** never enters telemetry; telemetry carries a closed category only; **at most three**; **zero** when none is honestly appropriate; and **no additional model call** is made merely to produce suggestions. Where a suggestion is arguable, it is **omitted**.
- **Why this shape.** T-2K-10 records that "contextual suggestions derived from current state" invites a model call per page load, which would spend the user's own BYOK credential on something they did not ask for — the same trade ADR-094 refused when it rejected ranking priorities with a model call. Determinism removes the cost, the rate-limit interaction and the fail-closed path together. The naming rule is the one place this decision is *permissive*, and deliberately so: a suggestion that cannot say "Marina" is not contextual. What it may not do is put that name into an **event**, which is T-2K-06's boundary and is enforced by the payload's shape rather than by a promise.

- **What the authorization does not relax.** Every signed decision stands unchanged: 2K.7 is not implemented; retrieval over entries and memories is preserved exactly; no widening, backfill, pipeline, job type, index or semantic infrastructure; mutating cards for tasks and memories only; the migration ceiling is **one**, and `1 allocated · 0 spent` remains a legitimate close; continuity always re-derives, always with a new `issuedAt`, and always re-asks (ADR-100); no payload transports reusable authorization; new messages persist no excerpt; the source is re-read at render; memory undo **archives** and never claims deletion; and R17 refuses any reintroduction of the unsafe alternative.
- **The stop conditions are part of the authorization, not advice.** Work stops and returns to the owner if any of these becomes necessary: a second migration; a backfill; a change to semantic retrieval; a new job type or pipeline; service-role on a product path; a product decision covered by neither OD-2K-1/OD-2K-4 nor an existing ADR; an unresolvable exposure of the most protective sensitivity level; a destructive change; an unplanned RLS or grant change; an inability to satisfy OD-2K-2, ADR-100, or memory undo by archival; a need to persist a confirmation or an authorization; persistently red required CI; divergent hosted parity; an unremovable fixture; branch protection requiring a bypass; a material concurrent change on `main`; real cost against the owner's credential without authorization; or any need to open signup or execute a rollout residual. Difficulty, an in-scope defect, a red PR of one's own making, slow CI, a failed first approach, or having to re-run gates after a fix are **not** stop conditions.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Authorize slice by slice* (rejected by the owner: the package was reviewed whole, the slices are independently closeable anyway, and a per-slice authorization would add a round trip without adding a decision). *Authorize implementation but not the telemetry deployment* (rejected: a telemetry vocabulary that never reaches the deployed writer is exactly the SH.6 failure `2K-METRICS-007` exists to prevent, so the deploy is part of the requirement rather than an extra). *Leave OD-2K-1 and OD-2K-4 to the implementer* (rejected: both are product decisions with a security edge — an editable-parameter set is an authority surface and a suggestion source is a disclosure surface — and ADR-100 is the recent precedent for what happens when a contract is left with two readings).
- **Consequences.** Slices 2K.1–2K.8 may execute, each on its own branch and PR, with CI green on the PR head **and** on the exact merge SHA before the next begins (ADR-090: green ×1). `2K-ACT-003/004` and `2K-SUGG-001` are unblocked and their content is determined rather than open, so **no Phase 2K requirement is gated on an unsigned decision**. `docs/reports/README.md`'s "PLANNING ONLY" line for `phase-2k/` stops being true on the first merged implementation slice and is updated with it. The signup rollout gate is **untouched** at 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature, and Phase 2K is not progress toward it. **A13 continues to target the roadmap successor, which this ADR does not authorize, does not scope and does not name in its heading.**

## ADR-102 — The owner authorizes Phase 2L — Work and execution as a planning phase, and the phase-start guard retargets to the roadmap successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-09
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **planning only**. It does **not** authorize implementation, any product code, any component or page, any migration, any database, RLS, grant, policy or Auth change, any new RPC, any deployment, opening public signup, executing rollout residuals, spending a BYOK credential, calling any provider, merging or marking ready the planning PR, or planning or starting any successor phase.
- **Context.** Phase 2K concluded 2026-08-09 after an extraordinary post-phase correction: **79 declared · 79 classified — 67 built, 9 baseline, 3 partial**, budget `1 allocated · 1 spent` and **not** retroactively reclassified, with `202608090089` charged to **no phase**. `main` is `044f541`, clean and synchronized; **89 migrations, hosted parity `202608090089`, local = remote**; CI green on the exact merge SHAs `eaf98b6` and `044f541`; signup closed and the rollout gate re-read live at **25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature**. The roadmap successor by `PHASE_2K_2O_ROADMAP_DESIGN.md` §4 is the parent PRD's **Etapa 4, slices 4.1–4.3**. Before naming anything missing, a current-experience audit re-derived every candidate item from source: `docs/reports/phase-2l/PHASE_2L_CURRENT_EXPERIENCE_AUDIT.md`.
- **Decision.** The owner authorizes **Phase 2L — Work and execution**, scoped to the parent PRD's **Etapa 4 slices 4.1–4.3 only**. Governing pair: `docs/initiatives/phase-2l/PHASE_2L_PRD.md` and `..._IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; audit, gaps, threat model and traceability contract under `docs/reports/phase-2l/`. **Seventy-six requirements across ten families and six slices planned for execution — none executed.** Etapa 4 slices 4.4–4.7 and beyond stay out, by ADR-092's decomposition rule: a phase that cannot close cannot be traced, budgeted or gated.
- **The audit corrected the roadmap in five load-bearing places, and four of them make the phase smaller.** (1) **The domain is strong and the surface is thin** — fifteen policied verbs with eligibility, destructiveness, confirmation, reversibility and undo strategy all declared as data in `taxonomy.ts`, a twelve-column staleness gate, a server-issued single-use confirmation and a 24-hour audited undo all ship; the Work list exposes four buttons and a link. Quick edit, bulk and undo are **surfacing existing authority**, not building it. (2) **The undo ships and is unreachable from any operating surface** — the only rendered Undo control in the product is in the command console; `work-item-actions.tsx` and the task-detail controls offer none. The roadmap lists "bounded undo" as though it needed building. (3) **The product's own Back control discards the user's place** — `task-detail-view.tsx:113` hardcodes `/${locale}/app/work`, so `parseWorkView(undefined)` resolves to `today` and `parsePage(undefined)` to page 1; a user on page 3 of *Todas* lands on page 1 of *Hoje*. The fix is a query string, not a store. (4) **Kanban, timeline, saved filters and adjustable density are rejected or narrowed** — drag needs a user-ordering model `dynamic_priority` does not provide, the calendar surface belongs to the successor, and a persisted saved view is a table with an RLS policy, a retention class and a deletion-cascade entry bought to replace a URL. (5) **What grows the phase is two absences that are not polish** — `work` is **not** in `GOVERNED_SURFACES` and `tasks` carries **no** classification column, so Work is the last ungoverned content surface and its posture is an owner decision rather than a task; and Work, the task detail and the recovery surface are **absent from the automated accessibility lane** while Conversar has ten entries in it.
- **The binding structural constraints, measured rather than assumed.** `public.apply_task_command` takes one `p_task_id`, and **ADR-057** records that PostgreSQL cannot extend an argument list with `create or replace` — so a set-valued bulk RPC is a new function, a migration, and a second copy of eligibility, fingerprint, audit and undo. **Bulk is therefore iteration over the existing single-item authority**, which is the same trade `2J-ATTN-010` already took for the attention queue. Separately, `private.validate_product_event_properties` pins `workView` to `array['today','all','waiting']` — present since `202607170024:224` and carried forward through `202607220044:1816` — so **a fourth canonical Work view that is reported to telemetry costs a migration**, and this is the only reason the phase has a ceiling at all.
- **Migration budget: maximum ONE**, allocated to **slice 2L.3 only**, non-transferable, reconciled per slice, ceiling and not obligation — **`1 allocated · 0 spent` is the preferred close** and is legitimate. Explicitly refused whatever implementation discovers: a set-valued or batch RPC; a saved-view or saved-filter table; a sensitivity column on `tasks`; a second product event beyond what one migration carries; a new `undo_operation` handler; a new index. A second migration is a **stop condition**, not a decision the implementer makes.
- **Five owner decisions remain open, two blocking and three gating, and each is presented as a signable proposal with a recommendation.** **OD-2L-1 — Work's sensitivity posture** (blocking 2L.1's acceptance and 2L.5): evidenced negative *(recommended)*, derive from the source entry, or classify tasks — the third is outside this budget and would be a separate authorization. **OD-2L-2 — the canonical view taxonomy and whether the migration is spent** (blocking 2L.3): three views with richer URL filters *(recommended, zero migrations)*, widen the reported taxonomy *(one migration)*, or widen without reporting *(rejected: a view nobody can measure is the producer-with-no-consumer failure this repository has paid for twice)*. **OD-2L-3 — which operations may be bulk-applied** (gating 2L.2): non-destructive bounded-value operations only *(recommended)*, or additionally bulk cancel with per-item confirmation. **OD-2L-4 — the selection ceiling** (gating 2L.2): 50, matching `WORK_PAGE_SIZE` *(recommended)*. **OD-2L-5 — gesture policy** (gating 2L.4): no gesture *(recommended)*, or swipe strictly as an accelerator for a visible non-destructive control. **None of the five blocks the planning package's own consistency**, which is why they are recorded here as proposals rather than as a stop.
- **Phase 2K's three partials are dispositioned rather than transported.** `2K-AUDIT-002` (zero-source provider prose) **does not belong to 2L** — it needs a credentialed provider call and is a Conversar answer property; it stays an independent residual. `2K-EXPL-007` (interpretation correction has no domain) **does not belong to 2L** — its destination was written as "the roadmap successor's own audit", and this *is* that audit: its subject is *interpretation* correction on entries and interpretations, while correcting a **task** is already a first-class operation through `rename_task`, `append_note` and the twelve field verbs. Its recommended destination is the phase that owns entries, memory and provenance. `2K-A11Y-007` (a real-device mobile session) **is inherited as a stated limitation only** — `2L-ACCESS-008` restates it with the same standing as `G-2J.4b` and it cannot be closed without owner-run hardware.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Build bulk on a new set-valued RPC* (rejected: ADR-057 forbids growing the signature, so it is a new function — a migration plus a second implementation of every guarantee the single-item path already proves, for an operation the existing path performs correctly *n* times). *Persist saved views and filters* (rejected: a durable per-user artifact needing its own RLS policy, retention class and deletion-cascade entry, bought to replace a query string the product already parses). *Classify tasks now* (rejected **for this budget**, not on the merits: it is the most complete answer to OD-2L-1 and it would make a UX phase a schema phase; it must reach the owner as a budget amendment rather than as a mid-slice discovery). *Include kanban* (rejected: drag is the least accessible primary verb in the product and needs keyboard and touch equivalents plus an ordering model that does not exist; it is a phase, not a slice). *Defer accessibility to closeout* (rejected: that is exactly what produced Phase 2I's partial, and Phase 2J's lane found a real Phase 2I defect on its first execution — so Work enters the lane in the slice that builds each surface). *Carry `2K-EXPL-007` into 2L because its destination said "the successor"* (rejected: the destination named the successor's **audit**, and the audit's answer is that the remainder is an entries-domain record; transporting it would put an entries-domain deliverable inside a Work slice).
- **The estimate is 5–8 calendar weeks against the roadmap's 5–7, and the increase is reported rather than smoothed.** Four of the roadmap's items shrank, but the audit added two the roadmap did not anticipate — a sensitivity decision for a surface that has none, and Work's entry into an accessibility lane it has never been in. Slices are independently closeable, so the phase can be stopped between any two of them.
- **A13 retargets from Phase 2L to the roadmap successor in this same commit**, the seventh application of the rule ADR-083, ADR-085, ADR-092, ADR-094 and ADR-097 established: an accepted ADR naming the authorized phase is itself start signal 3, and the governing PRD and implementation plan are signals 1 and 2, so the guard must move in the change that records the authorization and the invariant is never unenforced in between. ADR-067's invariant is unchanged — *the next phase must not be started before it is authorized.* **This ADR names no scope for the successor, and its heading does not name the successor at all** — a heading that did would start the next phase in the act of authorizing this one, which is why the guard asserts that property across the whole series rather than trusting it. The heading check is added for this ADR and the earlier series checks are preserved rather than replaced.
- **The accessibility family is named `2L-ACCESS` rather than the shape Phase 2K used, and that is a governance fix rather than a style choice.** `2K-A11Y` does not match `2L-[A-Z]+-\d{3}` — the shape both the A13 detector and the traceability generator use — because its family name contains digits. That is why Phase 2K's seven accessibility requirements were invisible to every prose count and why five documents stated 68 where 79 were declared. `R-14` in the traceability contract asserts the property directly rather than trusting the naming.
- **Amendment A-1, 2026-08-09 — appended, not rewritten.** The Decision bullet above says *"Seventy-six requirements"*, which was true of the package this ADR authorized. **ADR-103 then signed the five open decisions, and the count moved to 82**: OD-2L-1 was signed as option **B**, whose contract the four option-agnostic `2L-PRIVACY` requirements could not carry, so that family went 4 → 8; and `2L-MOBILE` gained two requirements — zoom/reflow and IME composition — named in the owner's signed slice contract. **No id was reused, renamed or renumbered, and none was removed.** The original sentence stands because it is a true statement about what this ADR authorized; editing it silently is the drift this repository documents against, and `R-15` in the traceability contract is satisfied by this amendment rather than by a rewrite.
- **Consequences.** The `2L-*` requirement namespace becomes declarable, which is why the retarget is mandatory rather than tidy. `docs/initiatives/phase-2l/` and `docs/reports/phase-2l/` are created with their governing artifacts and `docs/reports/README.md` marks `phase-2l/` as the active initiative, **planning only**. The phase adds **no RLS policy, no grant, no role, no secret, no external service, no second write path, no new AI operation kind and no service-role client** — and performs **no provider call at all**, so the usage ledger is untouched. **ADR-055's expiry of 2026-10-27 is unchanged by this authorization** — Phase 2L builds no retrieval and feeds no funnel — and `2L-CLOSE-006` requires it restated at close as neither satisfied nor superseded. `2E-COMMAND-012` remains deferred behind ADR-057's unexecuted reopening gate and is **not** pulled into 2L. **The signup rollout gate is untouched**, still reads **25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature**, and Phase 2L must not be read as progress toward it. **Per-slice acceptance records, the traceability matrix, the closing report and any deployment record are forbidden until their gates**, and this planning pass creates none of them.

## ADR-103 — The owner authorizes Phase 2L implementation through closeout, signs all five open decisions, and fixes the derived-sensitivity contract

- **Date:** 2026-08-09
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, taken after reviewing the complete Phase 2L planning package. It **discharges gates G3 and G5** and authorizes slices **2L.0–2L.5**, the per-slice acceptance records, the traceability matrix, the closing report, and the 2L.6 successor re-audit. It authorizes **no migration**, no deployment, no database/RLS/grant/policy/Auth change, no new RPC, no second write path, no service-role on a product path, no BYOK spend, no provider call, no signup change, no rollout residual, and **no successor phase**.
- **Context.** The planning package was delivered as draft PR #157 from baseline `main` `044f541`, with CI green on the exact head `5cc6a26` across all three jobs. It presented five open decisions — two blocking, three gating — each as a signable proposal with a recommendation, and stated that none of the five blocked the package's own consistency. The owner reviewed it and **changed one recommendation**.
- **Decision 1 — OD-2L-1 is signed as option B: derive sensitivity from the source entry.** The recommendation was option A (an evidenced negative, zero cost); the owner chose **coverage over cheapness**. The contract, signed clause by clause and reproduced at `PHASE_2L_PRD.md` §2.2: when a task carries `source_entry_id`, presentation consults the **current** classification of that source and applies the **central** sensitive-presentation policy when it is `highly_sensitive`; the classification is **re-read, never durably copied onto the task**; **no sensitivity column on `tasks`, no backfill, no migration**; manually created tasks are **never artificially classified** and keep **no derivable classification**, and that **partial coverage is presented and documented honestly**; the absence of a source entry is **never** inferred to mean `normal`; a source that is removed, inaccessible or outside the caller's authority produces a **safe state, never exposure**; no read may widen authority or bypass RLS; **the list and the detail converge on one contract**, which bulk, preview, result, undo, accessibility and telemetry all respect; **no new copy of a title, a description or sensitive content is persisted**; and **no sensitive information enters telemetry**. Option **C** (classify tasks) stays **out of this phase entirely** and would need its own authorization.
- **Decision 2 — OD-2L-2 is signed as option A: three canonical views, richer URL filters, zero migrations.** `today`, `all` and `waiting` remain the only **reported** views; *upcoming*, *completed*, per-project and per-context are delivered as **filters within** them. `workView` is **not widened**, so `private.validate_product_event_properties` is untouched and **the single allocation lapses unspent**. Option C — adding views without reporting them — stays rejected: a Work view nobody can measure is the producer-with-no-consumer failure this repository has paid for twice.
- **Decision 3 — OD-2L-3 is signed as option A.** Bulk covers status change within active statuses, priority, due date, planned date and authorized relation assignment. **`cancel_task` is excluded, by name and by test.** The one destructive verb stays a per-task, per-confirmation operation, which also means **no bulk confirmation is ever minted** and `T-2L-04`'s surface is empty by construction rather than by care.
- **Decision 4 — OD-2L-4 is signed at 50, matching `WORK_PAGE_SIZE`.** The set is exactly what the user can see. `2L-BULK-003` requires the bound to be **stated before it is reached** and **refused rather than silently truncated**. The ceiling is recorded as a **security control rather than a performance setting**: it is what holds off the pressure toward a set-valued RPC, which ADR-057 makes a migration and a second copy of every guarantee.
- **Decision 5 — OD-2L-5 is signed as option A: no gesture.** No touch, pointer, drag or swipe handler ships on any Work surface this phase, **including one added "in preparation"** for a later one, and `2L-MOBILE-004` makes the absence a guard proved against a planted handler rather than a promise. Every action is reachable through a visible, labelled control.
- **The requirement count moved from 76 to 82, by declaration and never by renumbering.** OD-2L-1 option B's contract has obligations the four option-agnostic `2L-PRIVACY` requirements could not carry without becoming paragraphs, so that family is **4 → 8**; and `2L-MOBILE` gains **two** the owner's signed slice contract names and no existing requirement expresses — **zoom/reflow** and **IME composition**. **No id was reused, renamed or removed.** ADR-102 keeps its original "seventy-six" sentence and carries an appended amendment rather than a silent edit, because that ADR truthfully describes the package it authorized.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Sign option A for OD-2L-1 as recommended* (rejected by the owner: a task extracted from a `highly_sensitive` entry would keep rendering in the clear on the surface a phone opens first, and the product would go on disagreeing with itself). *Sign option C and amend the budget* (rejected: it makes a UX phase a schema phase, and B delivers most of the value with no schema at all). *Fold option B's contract into the existing four privacy requirements* (rejected: four requirements carrying thirteen clauses is how a contract becomes prose nobody can classify, and `R-12b` needs each obligation to have a row it can refuse). *Leave the requirement count at 76 by trimming* (rejected: that is the renumbering the traceability contract's `R-15` exists to prevent). *Authorize slice by slice* (rejected: the slices are independently closeable anyway and a per-slice authorization would add round trips without adding a decision — the same reasoning ADR-101 recorded).
- **The stop conditions are part of the authorization, not advice.** Work stops and returns to the owner if any of these becomes necessary: **any migration**; widening the budget; changing a signed decision; a conflict with a standing ADR; any RLS, policy, grant, Auth or signup change; a new write path or a set-valued RPC; service-role on a product path; use of the owner's BYOK credential; any provider cost; a concurrent branch or PR; data loss; a product decision covered by none of the five decisions or an existing ADR; an external service still unavailable after investigation; or a next slice that cannot be reconciled without widening scope. **Difficulty, an in-scope defect, a red PR of one's own making, slow CI, a failed first approach, a wrong count, a bad fixture or a broken probe are not stop conditions** — they are investigated, fixed and continued.
- **Consequences.** Slices 2L.0–2L.5 may execute, **each on its own branch and its own PR**, with CI green on the PR head **and** on the exact merge SHA before the next begins (ADR-090: green ×1), and with the next slice re-reviewed against the *updated product* rather than against the old plan between each pair. `docs/reports/README.md`'s "PLANNING ONLY" line for `phase-2l/` stops being true on the first merged implementation slice and is updated with it. **The expected and required close is `1 allocated · 0 spent`**, and there is therefore **nothing to deploy**: hosted parity stays `202608090089`, and inventing a deployment record for a phase that changed no schema would be a fabricated evidence claim. **A13 continues to target the roadmap successor, which this ADR does not authorize, does not scope and does not name in its heading.**

## ADR-104 — The owner authorizes Phase 2M — Calendar, daily planning and notifications as a planning phase, and the phase-start guard retargets to the roadmap successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-09
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **planning only**. It does **not** authorize implementation, any product code, any component or page, any migration, any database, RLS, grant, policy or Auth change, any new RPC, any deployment, sending any notification, registering or modifying a service worker, requesting any permission, creating any telemetry event, calling any provider, spending a BYOK credential, opening public signup, executing rollout residuals, creating any acceptance report, final matrix or closing report, merging or marking ready the planning PR, or planning or starting any successor phase.
- **Context.** Phase 2L completed 2026-08-09 and its closeout was corrected the same day: **82 declared · 82 classified — 73 built, 5 baseline, 3 partial, 1 not-built-by-rule, 0 undelivered**, budget `1 allocated · 0 spent`. `main` is `5e6174b`, clean and synchronized with `origin/main`; PR #164 merged with **CI green on that exact merge SHA**; no open PR and no competing branch; **89 migrations, hosted parity `202608090089` confirmed by a live read-only `supabase migration list --linked` on 2026-08-09** — 89 rows, local equal to remote on every one, zero local-only, zero remote-only; signup closed and the rollout gate green and fail-closed. The roadmap successor by `PHASE_2K_2O_ROADMAP_DESIGN.md` §5 is the parent PRD's **Etapa 4, slices 4.4–4.7**. Before naming anything missing, a current-experience audit re-derived every candidate item from source: `docs/reports/phase-2m/PHASE_2M_CURRENT_EXPERIENCE_AUDIT.md`.
- **Decision.** The owner authorizes **Phase 2M — Calendar, daily planning and notifications**, scoped to the parent PRD's **Etapa 4 slices 4.4–4.7 only**. Governing pair: `docs/initiatives/phase-2m/PHASE_2M_PRD.md` and `..._IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; audit, gaps, threat model and traceability contract under `docs/reports/phase-2m/`. **94 requirements across thirteen families and six slices planned for execution — none executed.** Etapa 5 and beyond stay out, by ADR-092's decomposition rule: a phase that cannot close cannot be traced, budgeted or gated.
- **The audit corrected the inherited picture in seven load-bearing places, and three of them change a decision.** (1) **`planned_at` is write-then-display.** It is stored, audited, editable by command and rendered on two surfaces, and a search over every PostgREST predicate and ordering position finds **no read of it anywhere** — not a view, not a filter, not an ordering, not Hoje, not the heartbeat. There is also no `clear_planned` counterpart to `clear_due`. The product shows the word "Planejado" and does nothing with it. (2) **A service worker exists and is registered in production.** `public/sw.js` is registered by `layout.tsx:3,21`, calls `skipWaiting()` and `clients.claim()`, and caches static assets only. The Phase 2L successor re-audit's *"no service worker"* is **wrong**, and the push-absence guard's scope — `src/features/pwa`, `src/features/agent`, `src/app`, `.ts`/`.tsx` only — **cannot see that file**. Any push work would modify a worker already installed on every production client. (3) **Five review and planning schedule preferences are inert.** `daily_review_time`, `weekly_review_day`, `weekly_review_time`, `planning_day` and `planning_time` are read by the settings surface and by nothing else; reviews are on demand only and the reviews page says so in both locales. A control with no consumer is the failure this repository has already recorded in the other direction. (4) **There is no event entity, and slice 4.4 does not need one** — the committed-versus-suggested distinction it asks for is already representable from five existing sources: `tasks.due_at`, `tasks.planned_at`, `reminders.remind_at`, `summaries` period ranges, and unconfirmed extracted dates. (5) **Reminder sensitivity is derivable and nobody has derived it** — `reminders.entry_id` is the same relationship `task-derivation.ts` consumes for tasks, so a calendar lane rendering reminder titles beside masked task titles would reproduce, one entity over, exactly the divergence slice 2L.1 found on Hoje. (6) **Telemetry has five enforcement points and they are now enumerable by name** — the event-name CHECK, `private.validate_product_event_properties`, `productEventNames`, the surface CHECK and `productSurfaces`; the writer holds no copy of either vocabulary since `202608080087` and `202608090089` deleted them. (7) **Two independent implementations of "the user's local day" exist** — TypeScript `localDayBounds` and PL/pgSQL inside `run_user_heartbeat` — and `localDayBounds` returns `start + 24h`, which is not a 23-hour or 25-hour DST day's end. They agree today only because they are asked different questions.
- **Migration budget: ceiling TWO, obligation ZERO.** One migration is allocated to the **telemetry vocabulary** and is required only if the phase declares a product event or a new surface; it must widen the event-name CHECK, the property validator and — if a `calendar` surface is declared — the surface CHECK **in a single change**, and it must land **before any producer exists**. A second is allocated to **notification consent and delivery** and is required **only** if OD-2M-4 signs outbound delivery. **If OD-2M-4 is not signed for delivery the ceiling is ONE; if the phase declares no event the ceiling is ZERO**, and every dependent requirement closes `not-built-by-rule` against the budget rather than as a partial with an invented remainder. Everything else in the phase is specified to need **no schema change at all**, which is the reason the calendar is built over sources that already exist. A third migration is a **stop condition**, not a decision the implementer makes.
- **Seven owner decisions remain open, and each is presented as a signable proposal with options, a recommendation and impact.** **OD-2M-1 — what the calendar renders and how it is protected** (blocking 2M.1): tasks and reminders both through derived sensitivity *(recommended)*, tasks only with anonymous reminder markers, or titles rendered directly *(refused — the convergence guard fails it)*. **OD-2M-2 — the migration and the event vocabulary** (blocking 2M.5, gating 2M.1): one migration widening every enumerated enforcement point at once, spent before any producer, with non-vacuous negative controls; and an explicit choice whether a `calendar` surface is declared. **OD-2M-3 — the semantics of `planned_at`** (blocking 2M.1 and 2M.2): intention *(recommended)*, commitment *(refused: it silently reclassifies data users already entered under the other meaning)*, or a separate event entity *(deferred, with its trigger named — an item that is neither a task nor a reminder, having a duration, participants or a location)*. **OD-2M-4 — whether anything leaves the application** (blocking 2M.4): no outbound delivery this phase, governance only *(recommended)*; opt-in push with a content-free payload *(available as a separately signed conditional slice with its own migration, security review and real-device gate)*; push with governed content *(refused — `notificationCopy` takes no content parameter by construction)*; or email *(refused — a second egress boundary for no gain)*. **OD-2M-5 — real-device verification** (blocking closeout): the hardware-dependent requirements named before implementation, split into blocks-slice and blocks-closeout, with iOS Safari and Android Chrome checklists and an executed/not-executed record; **the owner runs these**, and no emulated run may satisfy one. **OD-2M-6 — gesture policy on the calendar** (gating 2M.1): visible controls only *(recommended)*, drag strictly as an accelerator for an action that is also a visible control, or drag as the primary interaction *(refused — it makes the primary path unreachable by keyboard)*. **OD-2M-7 — recurrence** (gating 2M.0): **explicit separation, recommended** — recurrence is a domain with its own series, occurrence, exception, edit-scope, DST-expansion and undo semantics, not a slice; the product already refuses it deterministically through `recurrence_requested` with copy in both locales, and Phase 2M declares it out of scope by rule with a guard against a recurrence field appearing "in preparation". **None of the seven blocks the planning package's own consistency**, which is why they are recorded here as proposals rather than as a stop.
- **The initial recommendation for `planned_at` was option C, and the audit changed it.** The authorization brief proposed keeping `planned_at` as intention **and** creating a separate event concept, conditional on the audit confirming that a calendar needs to distinguish intention from commitment. The audit's answer is that a calendar needs to distinguish **committed from suggested**, which is not the same question — and that distinction is already carried by five existing sources with no new entity. An appointment entity would also be one users would immediately ask to repeat, which pulls OD-2M-7 straight back in. So the recommendation is **A with C deferred and its trigger named**, and the change of recommendation is reported here rather than smoothed.
- **Phase 2L's residuals are dispositioned rather than transported.** `2L-MOBILE-008` and `2L-ACCESS-008` **belong to this phase by dependency** — its own scope needs the same owner-run hardware — and are carried by `2M-DEVICE-005`. `2L-METRICS-005` **belongs conditionally**: the same migration serves it if OD-2M-2 spends one, and otherwise it stays open with its destination unchanged. `2K-AUDIT-002` and `2K-EXPL-007` **do not belong** — both are Conversar and entries properties. `2E-COMMAND-012` **does not belong** — still behind ADR-057's unexecuted reopening gate.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Create an event or appointment entity now* (rejected: a new domain — table, composite-FK ownership, RLS, grants, triggers, audit, undo handler, telemetry and conflict rules — for a distinction the existing sources already carry, and it makes the phase unclosable by pulling recurrence in). *Redefine `planned_at` as a commitment* (rejected: it silently reclassifies data users entered under the other meaning, which is the silent move this phase forbids everywhere else). *Include recurrence* (rejected: a domain, not a slice; and a partial recurrence is worse than none). *Ship push in the phase body* (rejected as the default: it is the first egress of user-linked data in this product, it needs its own migration and security review, and it blocks closeout on owner-run hardware — so it is available as a separately signed conditional slice with a declared close-out path if it is not signed). *Ship drag-to-reschedule* (rejected for this phase: it adds a second hardware-dependent verification to a phase that must stay closable, and reverses a no-gesture posture signed one phase earlier on an adjacent surface; pre-specified so a later authorization is cheap). *Generate the daily plan with the model* (rejected: a provider call whose cost, consent and provenance are a separate decision, and it collides with "nothing moves by itself"). *External calendar integration* (rejected: the roadmap's own boundary, needing separate authorization, and not requested). *Leave the five inert preferences alone* (rejected: they compound, and a control the user configured deliberately that changes nothing is worse than one they never saw).
- **No estimate in weeks is offered by this ADR.** The roadmap's 7–10 weeks stands as the roadmap's number. The phase differs from Phase 2L in two ways that both add time and are stated rather than smoothed: it may spend a migration, which brings back parity verification and a deployment record; and it depends on **owner-run hardware**, which an implementer cannot schedule.
- **A13 retargets from Phase 2M to the roadmap successor in this same commit**, the eighth application of the rule ADR-083, ADR-085, ADR-092, ADR-094, ADR-097 and ADR-102 established: an accepted ADR naming the authorized phase is itself start signal 3, and the governing PRD and implementation plan are signals 1 and 2, so the guard must move in the change that records the authorization and the invariant is never unenforced in between. ADR-067's invariant is unchanged — *the next phase must not be started before it is authorized.* **This ADR names no scope for the successor, and its heading does not name the successor at all** — a heading that did would start the next phase in the act of authorizing this one, which is why the guard asserts that property across the whole series rather than trusting it. The heading check moves to this ADR and the earlier series checks are preserved rather than replaced.
- **The accessibility family is named `2M-ACCESS`**, for the same governance reason ADR-102 recorded: a family name containing digits does not match `2M-[A-Z]+-\d{3}` — the shape both the phase-start detector and the traceability generator use — which is how Phase 2K's seven accessibility requirements became invisible to every prose count.
- **Consequences.** The `2M-*` requirement namespace becomes declarable, which is why the retarget is mandatory rather than tidy. `docs/initiatives/phase-2m/` and `docs/reports/phase-2m/` are created with their governing artifacts and `docs/reports/README.md` marks `phase-2m/` as the active initiative, **planning only**. The phase adds **no RLS policy, no grant, no role, no secret, no external service, no second write path, no new AI operation kind and no service-role client**, and performs **no provider call at all**, so the usage ledger is untouched. **ADR-055's expiry of 2026-10-27 is unchanged by this authorization** — Phase 2M builds no retrieval and feeds no funnel — and `2M-CLOSE-005` requires it restated at close as neither satisfied nor superseded; ADR-099 already retired the semantic-retrieval widening from the active roadmap. `2E-COMMAND-012` remains deferred behind ADR-057's unexecuted reopening gate and is **not** pulled into 2M. **The signup rollout gate is untouched** and Phase 2M must not be read as progress toward it. **Per-slice acceptance records, the traceability matrix, the closing report and any deployment record are forbidden until their gates**, and this planning pass creates none of them.

## ADR-105 — The owner authorizes Phase 2M implementation through closeout, signs all seven open decisions, and fixes a non-transferable two-migration budget

- **Date:** 2026-08-11
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, taken after reviewing the complete Phase 2M planning package. It **discharges the seven decision gates** and authorizes slices **2M.0–2M.5**, the per-slice acceptance records, the two named migrations, the deployments that follow their merges, the hosted proofs, the owner hardware checkpoint, the traceability matrix, the closing report, and the 2M.6 successor re-audit. It authorizes **no third migration**, no schema outside the two named destinations, no change to a signed decision, no RLS/grant/policy beyond the approved model, no unplanned external service, no provider call, no BYOK spend, no signup change, no public rollout, and **no successor phase**.
- **Context.** The planning package was delivered as draft PR #165. **The baseline moved during review:** PR #166 (*"perf: make authenticated navigation responsive"*) merged at `62753ce`, with CI green on that exact merge SHA. The package was rebased onto it and **every load-bearing negative was re-executed rather than carried forward** — `planned_at` still has no predicate, ordering or filter anywhere; `public/sw.js` is unchanged and still carries no push handler; no gesture handler exists on any Work surface, including the file PR #166 edited; 89 migrations; hosted parity `202608090089`, local equal to remote. The re-execution is recorded as `PHASE_2M_CURRENT_EXPERIENCE_AUDIT.md` §0.
- **Decision 1 — OD-2M-1 is signed as option A: the calendar renders tasks AND reminders, both protected.** Both go through derived sensitivity and `ProtectedContent`; reminder sensitivity is derived through `reminders.entry_id` by the same mechanism `task-derivation.ts` uses for tasks, with the same three outcomes and the same fail-closed absence rule. `calendar` joins `GOVERNED_SURFACES` in the same change that ships its first consumer. **No classification is persisted anywhere**, manual tasks stay `undetermined`, and no surface renders a title directly. Option B — tasks only, with anonymous reminder markers — is refused: the audit found reminder sensitivity was derivable and simply had not been derived, so B would ship a surface that *could* have protected content and chose not to.
- **Decision 2 — OD-2M-2 is signed: one vocabulary migration before any producer, and the calendar gets its own `calendar` surface.** Migration 1 widens the event-name CHECK, `private.validate_product_event_properties` and the surface CHECK **in one change**, declaring `calendar` rather than folding calendar events into an existing surface. It preserves every existing event and surface, introduces **no hardcoded list in the writer**, proves every value through the real write path, carries **non-vacuous** negative controls, and admits **no free-form properties**. It **precedes every producer**, and only events with a declared question and a real consumer are declared at all.
- **Decision 3 — OD-2M-3 is signed as option A: `planned_at` is an intention.** It means *"I mean to work on this that day"* — **not a commitment and not a reserved time**. Option B is refused because it would silently reclassify data users already entered under the other meaning, which is the silent move this phase forbids everywhere else. Option C — a separate event/appointment entity — is refused for this phase: the committed-versus-suggested distinction the parent PRD asks for is already representable from `tasks.due_at`, `tasks.planned_at`, `reminders.remind_at`, `summaries` period ranges and unconfirmed extracted dates, and an appointment entity would pull recurrence back in. **No event entity is authorized.** The recommendation the package carried was A, having moved from the brief's initial C, and the owner signed A.
- **Decision 4 — OD-2M-4 is signed as option B: push, opt-in, with a generic content-free payload.** This is the **first egress of user-linked data in this product**, and it is authorized as a slice of its own, 2M.4b, behind governance shipped first in 2M.4a. The payload carries `notificationCopy(locale)` and a destination and **nothing else** — no title, description, name, record content or user-supplied text — and the prohibition is enforced **by construction**, because the payload type has nowhere to put content. Delivery uses **VAPID** with the private key held only in the server environment and never exposed to a client; quiet hours, the daily cap and the cooldown are applied **on the server before sending**; revocation is honoured immediately; an expired subscription is retired rather than retried; no delivery is duplicated; only content-free metadata is recorded; and **no `service_role` client appears on a product path**. The browser permission prompt may be raised **only after an explicit user action**, never on first load or sign-in, and only after the benefit has been explained on that same surface. Option C — push with governed content — stays refused: `notificationCopy` takes no content parameter by construction, and `omit` exists precisely because a payload leaves the application's control. **Email stays refused.**
- **Decision 5 — OD-2M-5 is signed: the owner executes the real-device proof, and push delivery and real compatibility block closeout.** The phase names its hardware-dependent requirements before implementation, splits them into blocks-slice and blocks-closeout, and publishes an iOS Safari checklist (including the installed PWA where required) and an Android Chrome checklist with OS and browser versions, steps and expected observations. Each claim is recorded as **executed** — device, OS, browser, date, observation — or as **not executed**. **No emulated run may satisfy one**, and the traceability contract refuses a close that claims otherwise. After 2M.4b is implemented, merged, deployed and green, the loop **stops at the checkpoint** and hands the checklist to the owner; the phase is recorded as awaiting the owner's proof rather than as blocked.
- **Decision 6 — OD-2M-6 is signed as option A: visible controls only.** No drag, swipe, pointer or touch gesture ships on any calendar, planner, review or notification surface this phase, **including one added "in preparation"**, and the no-gesture guard is extended to name the new surfaces. Every action is reachable through a visible, labelled control and by the keyboard. This keeps the posture OD-2L-5 signed one phase earlier consistent across the adjacent surface, and it removes a second hardware-dependent verification from a phase that already carries one.
- **Decision 7 — OD-2M-7 is signed: recurrence is out of Phase 2M and gets its own initiative.** No recurrence field, no series table, no occurrence table, no exception model, no repeating rule, no preparatory UI and no preparatory event. The existing deterministic refusal — `recurrence_requested`, with copy in both locales and an instruction in the extraction contract — remains correct and is re-asserted by test. The family closes **`not-built-by-rule`** against this signature, with a **named destination**, and never as a partial.
- **Migration budget: `2 allocated · 0 spent`, and the two are NON-TRANSFERABLE.** **Migration 1** — slice 2M.1 — carries the telemetry vocabulary and the `calendar` surface, and nothing else. **Migration 2** — slice 2M.4b — models consent, subscription, delivery state, revocation, the timestamps those need, ownership, RLS, minimum retention, deduplication and content-free audit, and nothing else; it **may not store** a task title, a description, names, record content, user-supplied notification text or any free-form payload column. Unspent capacity in one does not become capacity in the other, and **a third migration is a stop condition** rather than a decision the implementer makes. `2 allocated · 2 spent` is the expected close **only if both were genuinely necessary and applied**; a migration created to use up an allocation fails the close.
- **The requirement count stays at 94, and no id was reused, renamed, renumbered or removed.** Three requirements were **restated to the signed reality** rather than added to: `2M-NOTIFY-011` was the clause describing how the family would close if push were *not* authorized, and now carries the push delivery contract; `2M-METRICS-006` was the clause describing a budget that lapsed, and now requires that every event carry a question, a producer, a writer, a consumer, a test, a negative control and a proof of writability; `2M-MOBILE-003` was decision-agnostic and now states OD-2M-6 option A directly. `2M-PRIVACY-005`, `2M-METRICS-002`, `2M-METRICS-004`, `2M-NOTIFY-003`, `2M-NOTIFY-010`, `2M-DEVICE-002` and `2M-RECUR-004` were likewise narrowed from "whatever the owner signs" to what the owner signed. **Narrowing an unsigned requirement to its signature is not a scope change**; inventing a new id for the same obligation would have been.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Sign OD-2M-4 as option A (no egress this phase)*, which was the package's recommendation (rejected by the owner: the value of the phase is a user being told the few things that matter **on their phone**, and an in-app-only notification cannot do that; the content-free payload and the owner-run hardware gate are what make the risk acceptable). *Sign OD-2M-3 as option C and build the event entity* (rejected: it makes a UX phase a schema phase, needs far more than one migration, and pulls recurrence in — the phase would not close). *Fold calendar telemetry into an existing surface to avoid touching the surface CHECK* (rejected: `202608090089` was bought by exactly that kind of quiet reuse, and a surface nobody declared is a vocabulary nobody can enumerate). *Ship push and governance in one slice* (rejected: governance is provable in CI and delivery is not, so merging them would make the whole family wait on hardware). *Allow the two migrations to be fungible* (rejected: a transferable budget is not a budget, and the phase's own history shows unspent capacity becoming scope). *Re-plan against the new baseline from scratch after PR #166* (rejected: PR #166 touches no calendar, planning, review, notification, timezone, sensitivity or telemetry contract, and re-executing the four load-bearing negatives is the proportionate check — but it **was** re-executed, not assumed).
- **The stop conditions are part of the authorization, not advice.** Work stops and returns to the owner if any of these becomes necessary: a **third migration**; schema beyond the two named destinations; changing a signed decision; RLS, grant or policy beyond the approved model; an unplanned external service; a spend or external contract; opening signup; a risk of data loss; a concurrent branch or PR; **the real hardware checkpoint**; or the successor phase needing to start. **Difficulty, an in-scope defect, a red PR of one's own making, slow CI, a failed first approach, a wrong count, a bad fixture or a broken probe are not stop conditions** — they are investigated, fixed and continued.
- **Consequences.** Slices 2M.0–2M.5 may execute, **each on its own branch and its own PR**, with CI green on the PR head **and** on the exact merge SHA before the next begins (ADR-090: green ×1), and with the next slice re-reviewed against the *updated product* rather than against the old plan between each pair. Deployment happens **only** for a slice whose merged content includes a migration, and each deployment is followed by a live read-only parity reading and a hosted proof that carries **non-vacuous negative controls** and leaves **zero residue**, proved owner-scoped rather than by a global count. An Edge Function change is expected for the sender in 2M.4b, so **edge parity is verified explicitly** rather than assumed. **ADR-055's expiry of 2026-10-27 is unchanged** — Phase 2M builds no retrieval and feeds no funnel — and ADR-099 already retired the semantic-retrieval widening. `2E-COMMAND-012` remains deferred behind ADR-057's unexecuted reopening gate. **The signup rollout gate is untouched** and Phase 2M must not be read as progress toward it. **A13 continues to target the roadmap successor, which this ADR does not authorize, does not scope and does not name in its heading.**

## ADR-106 — The owner authorizes a third Phase 2M migration, exclusively for `clear_planned`, and restates the budget as three allocated and non-transferable

- **Date:** 2026-08-11
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, taken on the stop condition slice 2M.1's closing record raised before a line of slice 2M.2 was written. It authorizes **one** additional migration and **nothing else**.
- **Context.** `2M-PLAN-002` requires that "a planned day can be **removed** through the same validated command path that sets it; the asymmetry with `clear_due` is closed". The implementation baseline made that impossible without schema: `public.apply_task_command` carries **its own action allowlist and its own per-action patch rules in SQL** (`202607270060:525-679`, `:892-903`), and `set_planned` *requires* `plannedAt` to be a string matching `iso_instant_pattern` — **a JSON null is refused** — so `set_planned` cannot be made to clear. Adding the verb means replacing the function, which is a migration. The blocker was found, proved and reported **before** 2M.2 was started rather than discovered mid-slice: `AUTONOMOUS_LOOP_HANDOFF.md` §49 and `docs/reports/phase-2m/PHASE_2M_ONLINE_CALENDAR_EXECUTION.md` record it, with three honest resolutions and the explicit statement that **an implementer may not pick between them alone**. The owner picked (b).
- **Decision.** A **third migration is authorized for Phase 2M**, allocated to slice **2M.2**, whose contents are **`clear_planned` and nothing else**: the action's admission to `public.apply_task_command`'s allowlist, its patch rule, its delta, its write, its recorded applied state, and the assertions that prove all of it. It may not carry telemetry vocabulary, notification schema, any other verb, any new table, column, policy, grant or role, or any change to a signed decision. **This authorization does not substitute for, reallocate or reduce migration 2**, which stays reserved for notification consent, subscription and delivery in slice 2M.4b. It **does not widen Phase 2M's functional scope**, **does not authorize the roadmap successor**, and **does not retarget A13**.
- **Migration budget: `3 allocated · 1 spent`, all three NON-TRANSFERABLE.** **Migration 1** — telemetry vocabulary and the `calendar` surface, slice 2M.1 — **spent** as `202608110090`, applied and deployed, hosted parity `202608110090`. **Migration 2** — notification consent, subscription and delivery, slice 2M.4b — **unspent, reserved**. **Migration 3** — `clear_planned`, slice 2M.2 — **unspent at the time of this decision, reserved**. Unspent capacity in one is still not capacity in another. **A fourth migration remains a stop condition**, and so does any use of migration 3 for anything other than `clear_planned`.
- **Why (b) rather than (a) or (c).** *(a) Ship 2M.2 without `clear_planned` and classify `2M-PLAN-002` blocked* was rejected by the owner: the requirement is what makes `planned_at` a field a user can take back, and the deployed telemetry vocabulary **already admits `operation: 'cleared'`** for `day_planned` (`202608110090:485`) — a funnel that could never observe a removal would report planning as monotonic when it is not, so (a) would have shipped a vocabulary with an unreachable value, which is the shape `202608080087` and `202608090089` were both bought by. *(c) Reallocate migration 2* was rejected because it defunds OD-2M-4's signed push, ends slice 2M.4b, and converts a signed decision into an implementation side effect — exactly what a non-transferable budget exists to prevent.
- **What this does not become.** The precedent is **not** "a migration may be added when the work is hard". It is that a **signed requirement proved impossible without schema, reported before the slice started, with its cause located in a named line of a named migration, and with the three resolutions and their costs stated**, is an owner decision — and the owner may fund it. A migration discovered mid-implementation, or one whose necessity is argued rather than proved, remains a stop condition.
- **Consequences.** Slice 2M.2 spends migration 3 and closes `2M-PLAN-002` **built in the repository**. **It is not deployed by that slice.** The owner's execution order places the deployment, the `supabase db lint`, the live read-only parity reading and the hosted proof at the phase's deployment step alongside migration 2, so slice 2M.2 merges with the migration in the tree and hosted parity **unchanged at `202608110090`** — and every artifact says so rather than implying the schema moved. G8 is discharged there, to the same standard migration 1 was held to, and until then `2M-PLAN-002` is **built and not yet deployed**, which is a different claim from **built**. `TASK_COMMAND_POLICY_VERSION` is bumped in the same change, because a sixteenth action changes what a sentence may be classified as and therefore invalidates every stored fingerprint and every unexpired confirmation minted under the previous version; `policy-lock.test.ts` is what forces that bump to be deliberate. `public.apply_task_command` is re-declared **whole** rather than patched, which is this repository's established convention for a function whose `case` arms cannot be extended in place. **The signup rollout gate is untouched.** ADR-055's expiry of 2026-10-27 is unchanged. `2E-COMMAND-012` remains deferred behind ADR-057's unexecuted reopening gate and is **not** reopened by this migration. **A13 continues to target the roadmap successor, which this ADR does not authorize, does not scope and does not name in its heading.**

## ADR-107 — The owner defers real-device validation of push and amends the OD-2M-5 closeout gate, without asserting that push works

- **Date:** 2026-08-12
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, taken after two hardware runs on a real iPhone. It changes **the governance of Phase 2M's closure** and nothing about the product's behaviour, and it makes **no claim that push delivery works**.
- **What OD-2M-5 said, preserved verbatim in effect.** ADR-105 signed OD-2M-5 as: *the owner runs the hardware proof; **push delivery and real-device compatibility block closeout***. `2M-DEVICE-002` restates it. That text stands as the record of what was decided on 2026-08-11 and is **not** rewritten. This ADR is an **amendment dated 2026-08-12** layered on top of it.
- **The amendment.** Real delivery on iOS Safari and real validation on Android Chrome **no longer block the start, the execution or the closure of slice 2M.5, nor the closure of Phase 2M**. They remain **open, named residuals** with a destination, and they may not be deleted, reclassified as passing, or discharged by any offline test.
- **Why this is governance and not a success claim.** Push is **implemented and hosted** — 92 migrations at parity `202608120092`, the `send-push` Edge Function deployed, 47 of 47 hosted claims passed — and it **fails on the owner's real iPhone with HTTP 403 from Apple Web Push**. It has **never been executed on Android**. Keeping the phase open changes neither fact; it only prevents everything else the phase proved from being recorded. The owner's judgement is that the remaining work is an investigation of a third-party service's refusal, which is not the same body of work as the phase it happened to surface in.
- **The evidence this amendment rests on, stated so it cannot be read as a diagnosis.** The deployed sender's configuration self-check answers `subject: "operational"`, `publicKey: "p256_point"`, `privateKey: "p256_scalar"`, `pair: "consistent"`. The single retest after it answered `delivered=0 · failed=1 · diagnostics:[{category:"unauthorized", status:403}]`, and no notification arrived. **A consistent VAPID pair eliminates a key mismatch. It does not explain the 403, and no root cause is asserted here.** The application's public key was independently proved byte-identical to the Edge Function's by digest, so a cross-environment mismatch is eliminated too. What remains unexplained is why Apple refuses an authentication whose halves agree.
- **What is recorded, and how.** `H-5` (real push on a real iPhone) is **FAILED**, never `pass`, with the date, the device state and the sanitised response. Android is **NOT EXECUTED**, never `pass`. `2M-DEVICE-004` is **partial** and `2M-DEVICE-005` is **partial**, each with a concrete remainder and a named destination; `2M-ACCESS-007` stays **partial** for the same reason. **An emulated run may never be recorded as satisfying any of them**, which `2M-DEVICE-004` already requires and this amendment does not relax.
- **The destination, which is a real place and not a sentence.** `docs/initiatives/push-hardware-validation/` — created by the same change as this ADR — carries the whole residual: the Apple 403 investigation after a consistent self-check, iOS re-validation after any repair, Android Chrome and PWA validation, foreground/background/lock screen, tap destination and deep link, revocation, quiet hours, cap and cooldown observed on a device, and VoiceOver and TalkBack. It is a **backlog**, not an authorized phase: it declares no requirement identifiers, allocates no migration, and authorizes no implementation.
- **What this does not do.** It does not authorize a new migration — Phase 2M closes at **`3 allocated · 3 spent`** and a fourth remains a stop condition. It does not remove, weaken or roll back any shipped behaviour, and no migration is reverted. It does not open signup, and the rollout gate is untouched at **25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature**. It does not authorize further push attempts, key rotation or any further change to VAPID configuration in this phase. It does not authorize the roadmap successor, does not scope it, does not name it in this heading, and does not retarget **A13**. ADR-055's expiry of **2026-10-27** is neither satisfied nor superseded.
- **The guard that had to move, and why that is not a weakening.** `src/lib/closeout/phase-2m-declarations.test.ts` forbade `PHASE_2M_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md` and `PHASE_2M_REPORT.md` from existing at all, which was correct while the phase was mid-flight and is exactly wrong at closeout. It is **inverted rather than deleted**: both artifacts are now **required**, and the matrix must be **byte-identical to what the generator produces from the slice records**, so it cannot be typed, edited or drifted. That is a stronger contract than the one it replaces, and it changes in this same unit rather than silently.
- **Consequences.** Slice 2M.5 executes and Phase 2M closes with the honest classification: **push is implemented and hosted, it fails on a real iPhone with HTTP 403, and it has not been validated on Android.** The two Phase 2L residuals `2L-MOBILE-008` and `2L-ACCESS-008` travel to the same destination rather than being absorbed. The successor is **re-audited and not started**.

## ADR-108 — The owner authorizes Phase 2N — People, projects, memory, files and relations as a planning phase, and the phase-start guard retargets to the roadmap successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-12
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **planning only**. It does **not** authorize implementation, any product code, any component or page, any migration, any schema, RLS, grant, policy or RPC change, any Edge Function, any deployment, any secret, any external integration, resuming the push investigation, opening public signup, altering the rollout, creating any acceptance record, traceability matrix or closing report, merging or marking ready the planning PR, or planning or starting any successor phase.
- **Context.** Phase 2M completed 2026-08-12: **94 declared · 94 classified — 89 built, 4 partial, 1 not-built-by-rule, 0 undelivered**, budget `3 allocated · 3 spent`, all non-transferable. `main` is `eab6d08`, clean and synchronized with `origin/main`; PR #189 merged at `71c258d` and PR #190 at `eab6d08`, each with **CI green 3/3 on its exact merge SHA**; no open PR and no competing branch. **92 migrations, hosted parity `202608120092` confirmed by a live read-only `supabase migration list --linked` on 2026-08-12** — local equal to remote on every row, zero pending. Signup is closed (`[auth].enable_signup = false`) and the rollout gate reads **25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature**, fail-closed. The roadmap successor by `PHASE_2K_2O_ROADMAP_DESIGN.md` §6 is *people, projects, memory and files*. Before naming anything missing, a current-experience audit re-derived every candidate item from source: `docs/reports/phase-2n/PHASE_2N_CURRENT_EXPERIENCE_AUDIT.md`.
- **Decision.** The owner authorizes **Phase 2N — People, projects, memory, files and relations**, scoped to the roadmap's §6 candidate slices only. Governing pair: `docs/initiatives/phase-2n/PHASE_2N_PRD.md` and `..._IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; audit, gaps, threat model and traceability contract under `docs/reports/phase-2n/`. **The requirements are planned for execution — none is executed.** The roadmap's next entry stays out, by ADR-092's decomposition rule: a phase that cannot close cannot be traced, budgeted or gated.
- **The audit corrected the inherited picture in eight load-bearing places, and four of them change a decision.** (1) **The contextual person page already exists and is substantially built.** `/app/people/[personId]` renders identity, an edit form, an owner-relationship panel with create/update/end, context and project associations with roles, linked tasks, memories and an entry timeline. The roadmap's 2N.1 is therefore mostly a *hardening and provenance* slice, not a construction slice. The same is true one domain over for `/app/projects/[projectId]`. (2) **The contextual pages are outside the sensitivity contract.** `GOVERNED_SURFACES` names eight surfaces — `hoje`, `attention`, `capture_receipt`, `review_summary`, `notification`, `chat`, `work`, `calendar` — and **none of them is a person, project, memory or file page**; `src/features/entities/**` appears in no sensitivity reader. The person page renders raw `entries.original_content`, task titles and memory bodies with **no classification applied at all**, which is the divergence `2J-PRIVACY-001` was created to end, surviving one domain over. (3) **Removal does not leave retrieval; it leaves citation.** `public.match_internal_knowledge` filters **neither validity nor sensitivity**: it unions entries and memories, orders by similarity and applies `limit least(...,20)` **before** anything reads `valid_until`. `isMemoryInForce` is applied afterwards in TypeScript by `chat/actions.ts` and `resolve-sources.ts`. So an archived memory is still retrieved, still consumes one of the twenty slots, and is dropped at presentation — meaning archiving a memory silently shrinks the usable evidence set rather than freeing a slot. (4) **There is no delete path for any domain object.** A repository-wide search finds **zero `.delete()` calls in `src/`**. People, projects, memories, organizations, contexts, tags and attachments can be created and updated and can never be removed by the product; the only deletion that exists is whole-account deletion through `auth.users` cascade. "Deletion propagation" therefore has nothing to propagate *from* yet, and the phase must decide whether to create that authority at all. (5) **`entity_aliases` is a table with neither a reader nor a writer.** It carries `alias`, `normalized_alias`, `entity_type`, `entity_id` and a validity window, has its own explicit RLS policies and grants, and **nothing in the application touches it** — the only occurrence of the name outside SQL is the generated types file. The mechanism duplicate recognition would be built on already exists and has never been switched on. (6) **There is no merge, split or canonical-identity anything.** None of the 89 RPCs merges entities; `people` and `projects` carry no canonical pointer, no merged-into column and no tombstone. A person is a **row with a case-insensitively unique name per user** (`people_user_name_idx`), and that uniqueness is the only identity the product has. (7) **Relations carry confidence and validity but no source.** `person_relationships`, `person_projects` and `person_contexts` each have `confidence` and a validity window and **no `source_entry_id`, no interpretation link and no origin column** — so a relation cannot answer *"where did this come from"*, while `entry_entities` (which does carry `interpretation_id`, `mention` and `confidence`) can. Any surface that shows a relation as a fact today is showing an unsourced claim. (8) **The timezone defect is larger than the four exemptions record.** `2M-TIME-007`'s guard names four carried-past-close files, and its corpus is **eight directories** — none of which is `src/features/entities`, `src/app/[locale]/app/people`, `.../projects`, `.../memories` or `.../files`. Outside that corpus the same defect appears in **13 zone-less formatter call sites across 12 files**, including `people/[personId]` (two), `projects/[projectId]`, `memories/[memoryId]`, `inbox/[entryId]`, `files` and `home-dashboard` (where it computes *today's* label). The four are not the population; they are the part a guard happened to be pointed at.
- **The timezone decision is taken here rather than deferred, and it is not a repair.** Phase 2N surfaces would inherit the defect by extending the exact files that carry it, so `2N-TIME` makes routing dated rendering through `src/lib/time/local-day.ts` a **precondition of the first contextual slice**, and extends the `2M-TIME-007` corpus to the 2N surfaces so a new call site cannot be added zone-less. **The four carried-past-close exemptions are not repaired by this phase** — they belong to `daily-cycle`, they have their own destination, and folding somebody else's four files into this phase's first slice is how a phase acquires scope it did not plan. They are re-stated as residuals with their destination unchanged.
- **Migration budget: ceiling FOUR, obligation ZERO, and no migration is created by this authorization.** The final number is a **proposal in the implementation plan**, made only after the schema census, the authority-path census and the without-migration alternatives, and every candidate carries a named exclusive destination, the alternative that was considered, and why it was refused. Each is **non-transferable**: unspent capacity in one is not capacity in another, and a fifth is a stop condition rather than a decision the implementer makes. **No migration may be created during planning**, and a requirement whose migration is not authorized closes `not-built-by-rule` against the budget rather than as a partial with an invented remainder.
- **Push is a parallel track and not a precondition.** Phase 2N may not create a mandatory dependency on push while real delivery is unproven. Apple's `HTTP 403` after a `pair: "consistent"` self-check is unexplained, Android is **NOT EXECUTED**, and both stay at `docs/initiatives/push-hardware-validation/`. The absence of Android is **re-assessed before the closeout of the roadmap's next phase**, not this one. No requirement in this phase may be discharged by a push delivery, and the traceability contract refuses a close that treats either as approved.
- **The open decisions are presented as signable proposals and none is signed here.** Canonical person identity; canonical project identity; merge authority; merge reversibility; memory-correction authority; the difference between suppress, archive and remove; conflict representation; whether an inferred relation may be persisted at all; the file library's scope; the graph's role; deletion propagation; sensitivity on contextual pages; the destination of the timezone defects; the final migration budget; telemetry; whether any requirement needs hardware proof; and the relationship to ADR-055. Each carries two or three real options, a recommendation where the audit supports one, the user impact, the scope impact, the migrations implied, the risks, and an explicit marker for whether it blocks final planning, implementation, or only closeout. **No answer is assumed, and no artifact in this package presupposes one.**
- **Phase 2M's residuals are dispositioned rather than transported.** `2M-DEVICE-004`, `2M-DEVICE-005` and `2M-ACCESS-007` **do not belong** to this phase — they are push and hardware properties with their own destination. The four `daily-cycle` timezone exemptions **do not belong** — same defect family, different surfaces, named destination. `2L-MOBILE-008` and `2L-ACCESS-008` **remain open and are re-stated rather than absorbed**, because this phase's own mobile and accessibility families do not cover the Work surfaces they name. `2E-COMMAND-012` **does not belong** — still behind ADR-057's unexecuted reopening gate.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Treat 2N.1 and 2N.2 as construction slices, as the roadmap describes them* (rejected: the pages exist, and planning to build what is already built is how an audit over-states a phase — the 2I closeout recorded that failure three times). *Add a `sensitivity` column to `people` and `projects`* (rejected at the planning stage: OD-2L-1 B refused a persisted classification for tasks and the same reasoning applies — a person's sensitivity is a property of what is *said about* them, which the source entry already carries; a column would create a second answer). *Build merge before deciding identity* (rejected: merge is the irreversible operation, and building it against a name-uniqueness identity would make the first merge unreversible in principle rather than merely unimplemented). *Make the graph a primary navigation model* (rejected: the roadmap's own boundary, and a graph without source-linked relations is a picture of inferences presented as structure). *Repair the four `daily-cycle` timezone files inside this phase's first slice* (rejected: they belong to another surface family with its own destination, and absorbing them converts a foundation slice into a cross-cutting repair). *Fund an event/appointment or a persisted-inference model* (rejected: neither is requested by §6 and both would make the phase unclosable). *Plan the roadmap's next phase at the same time* (rejected: forbidden by this authorization and by ADR-092's decomposition rule).
- **No estimate in weeks is offered by this ADR.** The roadmap's 13–18 weeks stands as the roadmap's number. The implementation plan re-estimates against the audited reality, states the critical path, and says plainly that an estimate is not a promise.
- **A13 retargets from Phase 2N to the roadmap successor in this same commit**, the ninth application of the rule ADR-083, ADR-085, ADR-092, ADR-094, ADR-097, ADR-102 and ADR-104 established: an accepted ADR naming the authorized phase is itself start signal 3, and the governing PRD and implementation plan are signals 1 and 2, so the guard must move in the change that records the authorization and the invariant is never unenforced in between. ADR-067's invariant is unchanged — *the next phase must not be started before it is authorized.* **This ADR names no scope for the successor, and its heading does not name the successor at all** — a heading that did would start the next phase in the act of authorizing this one, which is why the guard asserts that property across the whole series rather than trusting it.
