- **Decision 4 — the planning-only refusals invert; they are not deleted.** `R-2O-7` forbade a closing artifact and `R-2O-8` forbade product code, and both were correct while the authorization was planning-only. Under this ADR the first **inverts** — an acceptance record per slice is now required, and its absence is what would be wrong — and the second is **discharged**, because product code is the thing being authorized. The assertions move with the facts and the superseded form stays quoted, which is the pattern this phase has already applied to `R-2O-5` and to `2O-AICONFIG-004`. **A deleted refusal records nothing, and the next reader cannot tell a rule that was satisfied from a rule that was removed.**
- **Decision 5 — the backlog line states this authorization and overstates nothing.** `TODO.md`'s active-milestone line drops **PLANNING ONLY** and gains **IMPLEMENTATION AUTHORIZED**, citing ADR-115, ADR-116, ADR-117 and this ADR. `phase-2f-documentation.test.ts`'s assertion inverts with it, for the eighth application of one rule: *the line cites every authorization the phase has received and overstates none of them.* Requiring "planning only" after implementation is authorized would force the line to **understate**, which is the mirror-image error and one this repository has made before.
- **Decision 6 — A13 is not touched by this ADR, and may not be touched by the closeout.** It guards the roadmap successor, was retargeted by ADR-115's own commit, and `R-2O-24` puts any further retarget in the next authorization's commit rather than in this phase's. **This ADR does not name the successor in its heading, does not scope it and does not start it.**
- **Decision 7 — what stays forbidden for the whole phase.** Opening signup or altering the rollout gate; closing `RG-DEP-1`, `RG-DEP-3`, `RG-QUO-3`, `RG-LEG-4` or `RG-DEP-4` by writing a file; changing `config.toml` or a secret; changing the CSP; a new `SECURITY DEFINER` function or any new authority for `authenticated`; scheduling a retention sweep from a migration; a model call to render a surface; a forecast price; a telemetry event with no real consumer; a control for a preference with no consumer; an export that cannot be complete; removing, altering, renaming, re-defaulting or migrating `embedding_model`; and absorbing `2N-RELATION-TRIGGER`, `2N-IDENTITY-EXTRACTION`, `2N-FILES-WRITER`, `2N-PRIVACY-FREETEXT`, `2N-RELATION-END-ANNOUNCEMENT`, the push HTTP 403 / Android track, the retention sweeps, or any resolution of ADR-055.
- **Decision 8 — `2O-ACCESS-006` keeps the evidence rule `OD-2O-12` B bought.** The screen-reader session is **executed and recorded with device, software and version**, or it is **recorded as not executed** and closes `partial` with a named remainder and a destination. It may **never** be promoted to a pass by documentation, by an emulator, by an automated accessibility scan, or by inference from one. There is no third outcome, and this authorization does not create one.
- **Decision 9 — the stop conditions that return the work to the owner.** A third migration, or any spend of M2; a new authority path, or a service-role read on an authenticated path; a change to signup or the rollout gate; a change to `embedding_model`; telemetry without a real producer and consumer; an export needing a job, storage or a migration; onboarding needing persistence; an administrative session or device list; a material privacy change; the need to implement a declined remainder; or a requirement that contradicts another in a way that changes the product.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Authorize one slice at a time* (rejected: nine authorizations for one signed plan is ceremony, and the plan already re-audits before each slice — the control that matters is the re-audit, not the signature count). *Authorize implementation and let the guards be repaired as they fail* (rejected: a guard edited under pressure to make a build pass stops being evidence; the inversions belong in the same commit as the authorization they follow from, which is where ADR-105 and ADR-112 put theirs). *Delete the planning-only refusals now that they are spent* (rejected by Decision 4). *Correct `ai_provider`'s hardcoded write while cataloguing it* (rejected: the finding is real and is recorded, and it belongs to `2O-AICONFIG`'s slice — repairing it here would be slice 2O.0 widening itself on a finding rather than on a requirement).
- **Consequences.** Slice 2O.0 lands the activation contract, the load-bearing capability registry and its two-directional guard. `docs/reports/phase-2o/` gains its first acceptance record, which `R-2O-7` now requires rather than forbids. **Migrations stay at 94 and hosted parity at `202608140094`**; slice 2O.0 creates none. ADR-108 through ADR-117 are **not rewritten**. Phase 2N stays **COMPLETE** and unclaimed. Signup stays **closed**, the rollout gate stays **25 · 3 · 2**, ADR-055 stays neither satisfied nor superseded and expires **2026-10-27**, push stays a parallel residual failing with HTTP 403 on a real iPhone and never executed on Android, and **the roadmap successor stays unauthorized, unscoped and unnamed by this heading**.

## ADR-119 — The owner decides that technical events get no page, and that the export is the sufficient view of them

- **Date:** 2026-08-16
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, taken on the single remainder slice 2O.5 classified `partial`. It settles `2O-PRIVACY-001` and authorizes **no page, no migration, no schema change, no telemetry change, no retention change and no new authority**. It alters none of the twelve signatures, adds no requirement, and does not reopen slice 2O.5.
- **Context.** Slice 2O.5's privacy centre answers *"what is stored about me"* over twelve categories. Eleven link to the surface that shows them. The twelfth — `usage`, which is `product_events` — has **no page anywhere in the product**, so the surface says *"no page of its own; it appears in the export"* rather than linking to a page that does not show it. That was the honest answer and it was classified `partial`, with the remainder routed to the owner as *"one page or a decision that the export suffices"*. This ADR is that decision.
- **Decision 1 — no dedicated page for `product_events`, now or as a remainder.** Technical events do not get a navigable surface of their own. A screen of raw telemetry rows presents engineering exhaust as an everyday feature; it would be opened once and never again, and building it would be the privacy centre widening itself to close a classification rather than to serve a reader.
- **Decision 2 — the privacy centre states plainly that this data exists.** The category stays **named** in the census, with its count, exactly as the other eleven are. **Not hiding the category is the half of this decision that carries the obligation** — an absent page is defensible only while the existence of the data is not.
- **Decision 3 — the full export is the sufficient view of this category, and the surface says so.** The reader who wants to inspect these rows gets every one of them, complete, through the export that already exists. The sentence pointing there is the category's answer and is not a link to a page.
- **Decision 4 — `product_events` is removed from neither the export nor the census.** The export is what makes "no page" acceptable, so an export that dropped it would convert a considered decision into a disappearance. This is the clause that must not be relaxed later on convenience grounds.
- **Decision 5 — nothing about retention, telemetry or the schema changes because of this.** No retention window is set, shortened or scheduled; no event is added, removed or renamed; `product_events` keeps its columns, its RPC-only write path and its append-only guarantee. This decision is about **one link on one surface** and reaches nothing else.
- **Decision 6 — the absence becomes a signed decision in the code, not a comment.** `PrivacyCategory` gains an optional `noSurface` record naming this ADR and its reason, **required** whenever `surface` is null and **forbidden** otherwise, with a guard in both directions. Slice 2O.5 shipped the reasoning as a comment and classified the requirement `partial` for exactly this reason: nothing distinguished *"the owner decided the export is the sufficient view"* from *"nobody has built the page yet"*, and the second becomes the first by sitting there long enough.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Build the page* (rejected by the owner, per Decision 1). *Drop the category from the census so twelve becomes eleven and every category links* (rejected, and it is the dangerous one: it closes the classification by making the data invisible, which is the opposite of what `2O-PRIVACY-001` is for). *Link `usage` to `/app/costs`, which is the nearest thing* (rejected: `/app/costs` renders `ai_usage_events`, a different table — a link to a page that does not show the category is the precise claim the requirement exists to prevent). *Leave it `partial` and carry it to the closeout* (rejected: the owner has decided, and carrying a decided item as an open remainder misreports the phase).
- **Consequences.** `2O-PRIVACY-001` is **re-evaluated in slice 2O.6 and closes `built`** — on the strength of the decision plus the guard, not on new UI. **Slice 2O.5's acceptance record is untouched**, and its `partial` classification stands as the honest record of what was true when it was written; the promotion and its reason are recorded in slice 2O.6's record instead. `2O-PREF-002` is re-examined **only** through this decision and is unaffected: its remainder closed in slice 2O.5 when Ajustes reached the account's own acceptance history, and that is a different surface from this one. **Migrations stay at 94, parity `202608140094`, none created by this phase.** Signup stays **closed**, the rollout gate stays **25 · 3 · 2**, the CSP is unchanged, `embedding_model` is untouched, **M2 stays without a destination and unspendable**, A13 is not retargeted, and the push HTTP 403 / Android track stays declined and unresumed.

## ADR-120 — The owner decides that no notification override exists, and that the requirement asserting one is the artifact that was wrong

- **Date:** 2026-08-18
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, taken during slice 2O.8 on the third bound `2O-NOTIFY-005` names. It authorizes **no override, no new priority, no migration, no schema change, no vocabulary change and no change to the delivery engine**. It alters none of the twelve signatures, adds no requirement, renumbers nothing, and does not reopen slice 2O.6.
- **Context — the requirement described a capability the product has never had.** `2O-NOTIFY-005` asks that *"quiet hours, the daily cap and the **important-reminder override**"* be stated where consent is given. Slice 2O.6 went looking for the third bound and found no object: `decideDelivery` in `src/features/notifications/governance.ts` refuses inside quiet hours with **no exemption for type, priority or urgency**, and the words *important*, *priority* and *urgent* appear nowhere in the governance module. The slice declared the absence on the surface rather than writing the requirement's sentence as though the override existed, classified the requirement `built` on that reading, and routed the discrepancy to the owner. This ADR is the answer.
- **Decision 1 — there is no override, and there will be none here.** Quiet hours always wins. The daily cap continues to apply. **No type, no priority and no urgency ignores either bound.** This is not a gap to be filled later in this phase; it is the product's actual delivery rule, and it is now the signed one.
- **Decision 2 — the surface must state the absence explicitly, and already does.** `boundsNoOverride` renders above the consent control in both locales: *"Não existe exceção. Nenhum aviso — de nenhum tipo, com nenhuma urgência — passa por cima do período silencioso ou do máximo diário."* / *"There is no exception. No alert — of any type, at any urgency — overrides the quiet period or the daily maximum."* **A bounded consent is bounded by what the reader is told, so the absence carries the same obligation the presence would have.**
- **Decision 3 — the requirement text is corrected in place, and the historical text is preserved.** `2O-NOTIFY-005` keeps its identifier and its position. Its wording changes from asserting an override to asserting that none exists and that the surface says so. **The superseded sentence is quoted beneath it and is not deleted** — this repository's standing pattern, applied to `R-2O-5`, `R-2O-7`, `2O-AICONFIG-004` and `2O-ACCESS-006` before it. **Renumbering is forbidden:** the identifier is cited by the PRD, the plan, the threat model, the traceability contract and two acceptance records, and moving it would be the renumbering `R-2O-1` exists to prevent.
- **Decision 4 — nothing is built, and the list of what is not built is the decision.** No override mechanism, no priority field, no exemption path, no `SECURITY DEFINER` function, no migration, no widening of the suppression-reason vocabulary, and no edit to `decideDelivery`'s refusal order. **The correct response to a requirement that describes a capability the product does not have is to correct the requirement, not to build the capability to match the sentence.**
- **Decision 5 — the classification is re-derived from the corrected rule and the real evidence.** `2O-NOTIFY-005` closes **`built`**, unchanged from slice 2O.6 — but now `built` against a requirement that asks for what the product actually does, rather than `built` against one whose third clause had no object. **The classification is the same and its meaning is not**, which is exactly why the correction is worth making at closeout instead of leaving the discrepancy in a footnote.
- **Alternatives considered.** *Build an important-reminder override* (rejected by the owner, per Decision 1, and it would be a delivery-engine change inside a closeout slice — precisely the scope creep ADR-118 Decision 9 makes a stop condition). *Leave the requirement's text alone and carry the discrepancy as a permanent footnote* (rejected: a requirement that misdescribes the product is a trap for every later reader, and the footnote lives in one acceptance record while the requirement lives in five documents). *Reclassify it `partial` with the override as the remainder* (rejected: `partial` means something is still owed, and the owner has decided nothing is owed — a remainder nobody intends to deliver is an `undelivered` requirement with softer wording). *Delete the third clause outright* (rejected: a deleted clause records nothing, and the next reader could not tell a bound that was considered and refused from one nobody thought of).
- **Consequences.** `2O-NOTIFY-005` closes **`built`** in the Phase 2O matrix. `PHASE_2O_PRD.md`, `PHASE_2O_THREAT_MODEL.md` and `PHASE_2O_TRACEABILITY_CONTRACT.md` restate the requirement with the superseded text quoted; **slices 2O.6's and 2O.7's acceptance records are untouched**, and 2O.6's reading stands as the honest record of what was true when it was written — the pattern ADR-119 set for `2O-PRIVACY-001`. **Migrations stay at 97 and hosted parity at `202608160097`; this phase creates none.** Signup stays **closed**, the rollout gate stays **25 · 3 · 2**, the CSP is unchanged, `embedding_model` is untouched, **M1 and M2 both close unspent**, A13 is not retargeted, and push stays a declined residual failing with HTTP 403 on a real iPhone and never executed on Android.

## ADR-121 — The owner authorizes Phase 2P planning, signs its product direction, and retargets the phase-start guard to the roadmap successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-18
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes the Phase 2P planning package and signs `OD-2P-1` … `OD-2P-12`. **It authorizes no implementation, migration, deploy, merge, signup change, rollout change or successor phase.**
- **Context.** Phase 2O is complete. The owner reviewed the current product and named twelve concrete everyday failures: text-only capture on Today; modality tabs on Capture; a confirmation queue that does not clear; excessive confirmation; broken and hidden Conversation; nested person-company editing; thin memory creation; list-first Relations; visually weak Calendar and Reminders; notification preferences mixed with history; and a single scrolling Settings page. A read-only audit confirmed every shape and found that the desired editable voice-transcription lifecycle already exists, while the known Needs You correction exists only on an unmerged branch.
- **Decision 1 — the phase is Phase 2P: Trustworthy capture and everyday UX.** Its governing pair is `docs/initiatives/phase-2p/PHASE_2P_PRD.md` and `PHASE_2P_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; evidence lives under `docs/reports/phase-2p/`.
- **Decision 2 — the owner signs all twelve product decisions.** Text is default; file and microphone live in one composer shared by Today and Capture; voice becomes editable text before explicit send and audio is not retained; Needs You contains only real unresolved decisions; automation uses calibrated type-specific policy rather than a raw 90% score; automatic writes are visible and undoable; Conversation is repaired before promotion and then becomes first-class on mobile; notification preferences move to Settings while history keeps its route; Settings gains stable sections; Relations opens on Drawing with a complete text tab; reminder creation uses a modal/sheet and preserves context; real iPhone and VoiceOver evidence are required for the claims that depend on them.
- **Decision 3 — implementation remains a separate authorization.** The package declares 87 requirements across fourteen families and nine slices, but no acceptance record, traceability matrix, closing report, product code or Phase 2P migration may exist until a later ADR authorizes implementation. *(Superseded wording, preserved: "The package declares 86 requirements across fourteen families and nine slices" — the count moved to 87 when the owner authorized appending `2P-SETTINGS-008` on 2026-08-18; see the amendment below.)*
- **Decision 4 — migrations are not pre-authorized.** The existing branch's migration 098 is a candidate to re-audit in 2P.0, not an allocation. Any migration needed by implementation is an owner checkpoint and a stop condition until a later decision names its purpose and ceiling.
- **Decision 5 — inherited residuals remain inherited.** Push HTTP 403/Android, retention scheduling, SMTP, restore drill, legal/monitoring signatures, `2N-FILES-WRITER` and other declined residuals are not absorbed. `2N-IDENTITY-EXTRACTION` and `2N-RELATION-TRIGGER` are explicit boundaries on automation, not work the phase may silently claim.
- **Decision 6 — A13 retargets in this same change.** Phase 2P's accepted ADR and governing artifacts are legitimate start signals only because this decision exists. The guard now protects the next lettered successor, unnamed by this heading, and retains planted positive and negative controls.
- **Alternatives considered.** Implement all twelve observations directly without a package (rejected: two are functional defects and one changes mutation authority, so visual work would land over an unstable lifecycle). Treat 90% as a universal threshold (rejected: raw confidence is not calibrated across tasks, identities, memories and relations). Merge the existing Needs You branch immediately (rejected: it predates the current baseline and requires re-audit). Fold push and every inherited residual into the phase (rejected: proximity is not authorization and would destroy the sequence the owner approved).
- **Consequences.** Six planning documents and one declaration guard are created; A13 is retargeted. The baseline remains `27f9f77`; migrations remain 97 local = 97 hosted at `202608160097`; no product code, migration or deploy is created. Signup stays closed and rollout stays 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature. The next action is owner review of the package, followed by a separate implementation authorization if approved.
- **Amendment (2026-08-18) — the owner authorizes appending `2P-SETTINGS-008`.** The independent package review found that `OD-2P-8`'s second half — Notifications becomes the history/inbox surface — was carried by plan prose, threat T-15 and the Settings-section requirement but by no dedicated requirement ID, so the closeout matrix could never see it. The owner authorized appending **one** requirement to the **end** of the `2P-SETTINGS` family: the Notifications page becomes a focused history/inbox surface, its extensive controls (consent, types, frequency, quiet hours, caps) move to the Settings notifications section, at most one discreet contextual entry to the preferences remains, empty/loading/error and read/unread states plus navigation to each item's destination are covered on desktop and mobile, deep links, keyboard focus and screen-reader access are preserved, and no existing setting is lost or changes semantics through the reorganization alone. **The count is now 87; the fourteen families and nine slices are unchanged; no ID was renumbered, reused or removed; this amendment signs no new product direction** — it instantiates `OD-2P-8`, already signed above — **and it authorizes no implementation, migration, deploy or successor.**

- **Amendment (2026-08-19) — the owner corrects `2P-PERSON-001`'s wording and signs where a role lives.** Slice 2P.6's re-audit found that the requirement's phrase *"company and role"* names a field the product does not have: `people` has columns `id, user_id, organization_id, name, notes, created_at, updated_at` and **no `role`**, measured live against the deployed schema. Building the requirement as written would mean adding one, which is a third Phase 2P migration and therefore a stop condition. The owner reviewed that and **refuses the column**: *(Superseded wording, preserved: "Company and role are editable from their displayed section with one explicit action.")* The corrected declaration is **`2P-PERSON-001`: the associated company is editable directly in the person's main section with one explicit action, and each role is edited in the context of the relationship that carries it.** Signed with it, and binding: **the company/organization associated with a person is editable directly on the person's page**; **there is no global role for a person**; **a role on a project belongs to the `person_projects` relation**; **a role on a task belongs to the `task_people` relation**; **each role is edited in the context of its own relation**; **those roles are not duplicated onto `people`**; **no global title is synthesized from the several roles**; **the absence of a role produces no inference**; and **no column, table, RPC or migration is created for any of this.** **The count stays 87, the fourteen families and nine slices are unchanged, and no ID is renumbered, reused or removed.** This amendment signs no new product direction — it corrects the expression of the everyday failure this ADR's Context already named, *"nested person-company editing"* — and it authorizes no migration, deploy or successor.

- **Amendment (2026-08-19) — the owner confirms `2P-CALENDAR-001` means a real month view, and corrects `2P-REMINDER-002` to drop recurrence.** Slice 2P.6's re-audit of the next slice found two requirements naming things the product does not have, and put both to the owner rather than resolving them. Both answers are recorded here.

  **`2P-CALENDAR-001` — the text is unchanged and the scope is confirmed.** `CALENDAR_ORIENTATIONS` is `["day","week","agenda"]`, and the question was whether "month" could be read as the agenda. **It cannot**, and Agenda must not be interpreted or renamed as Mês. Slice 2P.7 delivers **Dia, Semana and Mês**; Agenda may remain as an additional view while it stays useful. The month view must show the real grid of the month; mark today perceptibly **without relying on colour alone**; distinguish days outside the current month; show tasks, reminders and reviews **boundedly**; keep cells from growing without limit; offer access to the overflow; open a day or an item **preserving context**; work on mobile without an illegible grid; carry an accessible text alternative; preserve timezone and local-date semantics; and **create no new write path**. Where a legible grid needs a different presentation on a phone, a compact or contextual agenda is allowed **while the real month remains the selected period** — a plain list may not be declared a month view.

  **`2P-REMINDER-002` — corrected by amendment, with the prior text preserved.** *(Superseded wording, preserved: "The modal/sheet groups content, schedule, recurrence/importance and optional links in that order.")* `reminders` has **no recurrence column of any kind**, so the requirement as written needed a third Phase 2P migration. **The owner refuses it.** The corrected declaration groups **content; date and time; importance; an optional link; and the save and cancel actions**. **Recurrence is not offered in this phase, because the product has no persistent model for it**, and it becomes the explicit traceable remainder **`2P-REMINDER-RECURRENCE`** — never classified `built`, `baseline` or `partial` for this implementation. Binding while it stands: no recurrence control that does not work; no recurrence encoded in free text; no duplicated reminders simulating a repeat; no reuse of an existing column for it; **no column, table, RPC or migration**; and no change to the meaning of the current scheduling.

  **The count stays 87, the fourteen families and nine slices are unchanged, and no ID is renumbered, reused or removed.** This amendment signs no new product direction and authorizes no migration, deploy or successor.

## ADR-122 — The owner authorizes Phase 2P implementation, slices 2P.0 through 2P.8

- **Date:** 2026-08-18
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes implementation of the package ADR-121 accepted, over the twelve product decisions ADR-121 signed. **It authorizes no migration beyond the single conditional allocation named below, no signup change, no rollout change and no successor phase.**
- **Context.** ADR-121 authorized planning only and explicitly deferred implementation to a separate decision. The owner reviewed the package and recorded that decision, authorizing the nine slices in order, branch-and-PR per slice, merge only on green CI at the exact head and again at the exact merge SHA, and closeout blocked behind real iPhone and VoiceOver evidence.
- **Decision 1 — slices 2P.0 … 2P.8 are authorized in sequence.** Each slice re-audits its own subject against the `main` the previous slice produced, before editing (`2P-FOUNDATION-007`). 2P.3 and 2P.4 must never run in parallel. 2P.5, 2P.6 and 2P.7 may be parallelized only in genuinely isolated worktrees with no overlapping files, each rebased and re-audited against the `main` the other produced, and with no CI or test claim shared between them.
- **Decision 2 — implementation artifacts become legal, one slice at a time.** A slice acceptance record under `docs/reports/phase-2p/` is authorized from this decision onward. The traceability matrix and the closing report remain **forbidden until slice 2P.8**, and the matrix must be generated from the sources rather than typed. `phase-2p-declarations.test.ts` moves from asserting the planning-only absences to asserting this posture.
- **Decision 3 — migrations remain unfunded except one conditional allocation.** The owner authorizes applying the existing candidate `202608170098_confirm_entry_interpretation.sql` **only if** slice 2P.0's re-audit proves it still correct **and** slice 2P.1 proves its necessity and safety. Any other schema, RPC, policy or deployed-vocabulary need discovered by any slice is a **stop condition** requiring a separate owner authorization. Convenience is not a reason.
- **Decision 4 — slice 2P.0's re-audit rejected the candidate.** Recorded here because Decision 3's condition has been evaluated and failed. `202608170098` accepts only `awaiting_review` (leaving `partially_processed` entries stuck), registers no undo against the `undo_operation` registry, forces a terminal status instead of re-deriving it, leaves `entity_id` null in its audit row, and uses `reason` as an idempotency marker. It is **not applied and not copied forward**. The lifecycle defect it aimed at is real and is proved in `PHASE_2P_SLICE_00_ACCEPTANCE.md` §3; repairing it requires deployed SQL, which is the stop condition slice 2P.0 raises to the owner.
- **Decision 5 — 2P.4 has two additional hard stops.** Calibrated automation must not be enabled from a raw model confidence score, and must not be enabled at all without a reference set the owner has actually reviewed. If calibration requires new persistent authority or a migration, the slice stops rather than improvising an authorization.
- **Decision 6 — hardware evidence gates closeout.** Phase 2P does not close, and the roadmap successor is neither started nor planned, until the owner executes the real iPhone and real VoiceOver procedure. Viewport emulation, an automated scan and an inference from adjacent coverage are each explicitly insufficient, continuing ADR-118 Decision 8.
- **Decision 7 — inherited residuals stay inherited.** Push HTTP 403 and Android, retention scheduling, SMTP, the restore drill, the legal and monitoring signatures, the four touch-target exceptions, the forty-nine unstyled elements, `2N-FILES-WRITER`, `2N-IDENTITY-EXTRACTION` and `2N-RELATION-TRIGGER` are not absorbed, claimed or re-measured by this phase.
- **Alternatives considered.** Merge the candidate branch first and correct it afterwards (rejected: Decision 3's condition exists precisely to prevent work being preserved because it exists). Widen the allocation to "whatever 2P.1 needs" (rejected: an open-ended migration budget is indistinguishable from no budget, and the owner's checkpoint list names an unauthorized migration first). Defer the whole lifecycle repair to a later phase (rejected: it is one of the two P0 defects the phase exists to fix).
- **Consequences.** The baseline is `main` `6a7bf21` — the package's `27f9f77` plus the package's own merge, with zero product surfaces changed between them; the governing pair's baseline lines are corrected accordingly. Migrations stay 97 local = 97 hosted at `202608160097` until an allocation is authorized and spent. Signup stays closed and rollout stays 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature. A13 continues to protect the unnamed roadmap successor.

- **Amendment (2026-08-18) — the owner replaces Decision 3's failed allocation with one migration for slice 2P.1, over twelve functions.** Decision 4 recorded that the conditional allocation's condition failed, leaving the lifecycle repair unfunded. The owner has now authorized **exactly one** migration for slice 2P.1, **written from nothing**. It may carry the central re-derivation contract, the repair of the resolution functions, a true undo, the necessary audit, and the guards and compatibility that same contract needs. **Any second migration for the slice is a stop condition.** **The owner expressly corrects the earlier reference to "nine functions": the count is twelve**, as §93 measured live from `pg_proc` — `confirm_entry_task_candidates` and `_v2`…`_v6`, `confirm_entry_tasks`, `record_entry_task_candidate_confirmation`, `resolve_pending_question_v1`/`_v2`/`_v3`, and `resolve_entry_person_candidates`. All twelve must obey the same contract, **including the superseded versions still executable by `authenticated`**; a route may not keep the old behaviour merely because the current application does not call it. The rule must not be copied twelve times: the lifecycle decision is centralized and every applicable function delegates to it or consumes one unambiguous definition. **`202608170098_confirm_entry_interpretation.sql` remains rejected, obsolete, never applied, not reused, not copied and not counted as delivery.** Grants, RLS, retention and authority may not change silently: if correcting the behaviour required changing who can execute an existing function, the slice stops and presents the change first. This amendment authorizes no signup change, no rollout change, no BYOK spend and no successor phase.

- **Amendment (2026-08-20) — the owner interprets `2P-ACCESS-003` as the navigation the product actually uses, and does not reverse the five decisions that produced it.** `2P-ACCESS-003` reads *"Tabs implement keyboard selection and expose the active panel"*, and slice 2P.8's re-audit found that it names a construct the product deliberately does not have: `work-modes`, `brain-lenses`, `relation-view-controls`, `settings-section-nav` and `data-ai-tabs` each navigate **between separate documents by link**, and each already documents that it uses `aria-current="page"` and never `role="tablist"`. The owner confirms the requirement **must represent the navigation the product really uses**. It is therefore discharged by: semantic links; `aria-current="page"` on the active destination; a visible focus indicator; keyboard operation, Enter, back and forward; and the new document's title and context announced adequately. It is expressly **not** discharged by `role="tablist"`, `role="tab"` or `role="tabpanel"`, by roving `tabindex`, or by converting cross-document links into false ARIA tabs — **the five prior decisions are not reversed.** The requirement may be classified `baseline` **only after every relevant surface is proved**, not by inheritance from an adjacent surface. This amendment preserves the requirement's original wording in the PRD and adds the interpretation beside it; it authorizes no migration, no signup change, no rollout change and no successor phase.

- **Amendment (2026-08-20) — the owner refuses a migration and a vocabulary widening for `2P-CALENDAR-MONTH-TELEMETRY`, and records it as an explicitly unfunded remainder.** Slice 2P.7 put the remainder forward for signature (§101). The owner **does not authorize** a third Phase 2P migration, and does not authorize widening the deployed `private.validate_product_event_properties` orientation enum, for this purpose. Accordingly: the month view **remains without an event of its own in this phase**; `calendar_viewed` **stays valid** for the three deployed orientations and its producer and consumer are untouched; **the absence of an event for the month is not a broken event**; no existing literal may be reused with false semantics (a month must never be recorded as `agenda`); `month` must not be sent to a validator that refuses it; no parallel telemetry channel may be created; the item is recorded as a **remainder/backlog item explicitly not funded**; and this **does not block the calendar's functional closure**. Binding on the closeout guard: the traceability contract's **refusal 9** — *"a product event with no deployed vocabulary, producer or consumer"* — **must not fire on this**, because what is absent is an event for one orientation, deliberately, not an event that exists and is broken.

- **Amendment (2026-08-20) — the owner authorizes repairing `2P-REMINDER-REVALIDATE-HANG` inside slice 2P.8, without a migration.** Slice 2P.7 recorded the remainder and put it forward for signature. The owner signs it as a **real UX defect**: an action can write and leave the interface stuck, or leave it not reflecting the result. Before editing, the defect must be **reproduced against the current `main`**, and cold start/warm-up, revalidation, transition, an unmounted dialog and the Server Action's own response must be **told apart** rather than conflated — proving whether the write occurred, whether the response arrived, and when `pending` ends. **A defect proved to predate the new code is not to be attributed to it.** The repair must: never leave a permanent *"Salvando…"*; reflect save and undo without a manual reload; never close a dialog during its own transition; preserve focus and context; not duplicate a write; never turn a timeout into a success; show a true error when the response fails; work after a cold start and when warm; be tested on desktop and mobile; **require no migration**; and **not silently refactor the remaining `revalidatePath` call sites**. If a safe repair were to require a migration, a change of authority, or a global rewrite, the slice **stops and reports** instead.

- **Amendment (2026-08-20) — the owner confirms the requirement count correction: 72 of 87 before slice 2P.8, and there is no eighty-eighth requirement.** Slice 2P.7 measured that the recorded cumulative chain diverges at exactly one step — slice 2P.5 counted the closing of `2P-CHAT-004`'s remainder as an additional requirement, when `2P-CHAT-004` is one of the seven CHAT requirements slice 2P.2 had already counted. The owner confirms the correction. **The historical records are preserved as written**; the arithmetic is corrected **forward, by amendment**, never rewritten backwards over a signature. **No eighty-eighth requirement is invented**, and the traceability generator required by `2P-CLOSE-001` **must find exactly 87 identifiers** and classify each exactly once. This amendment authorizes no migration, no signup change, no rollout change and no successor phase.

## ADR-123 — The owner authorizes a second Phase 2P migration, for slice 2P.4 only, and signs the calibration contract that governs it

- **Date:** 2026-08-19
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **exactly one** further migration, for slice 2P.4 and no other purpose. **A third Phase 2P migration is a stop condition.** It authorizes no signup change, no rollout change, no BYOK spend, no push work and no successor phase.
- **Context.** ADR-122 Decision 3 left migrations unfunded, its amendment funded exactly one for slice 2P.1, and that one is now spent and deployed (`202608180098`, parity `202608180098`). Slice 2P.4's re-audit against `4d04f593` found three of its ten requirements unbuildable without persisted authority: `2P-AUTONOMY-001` (policy per category), `-003` (calibration against an owner-reviewed reference set) and `-010` (disable one category without disabling the others). `agent_preferences.autonomy_level` is a single global `text`, NOT NULL, defaulting to `'autonomous'`, with no check constraint and **no behavioural consumer** — `trust-builders.ts` passes `autonomyAllowed: true` as a literal rather than reading it. One scalar cannot express six policies, and its default is a value nobody chose.
- **Decision 1 — one migration, for six named categories.** It may create the persisted storage strictly necessary for: per-category automation policy; per-category enablement state; calibration from examples the owner actually reviewed; the minimum evidence needed to decide whether a category may execute automatically; the link between an automatic decision, its audit and its undo; fail-closed controls; owner isolation; and retention and lifecycle coherent with the contracts already deployed. The categories are **tasks, people, projects, companies, memories and relations**.
- **Decision 2 — no generic confidence rule.** A "confidence above 90%" threshold is refused. Each category carries its own policy and its own states, at minimum **disabled**, **suggest only**, and **automatic only when eligible**.
- **Decision 3 — automation begins fail-closed, and the fail-closed set is enumerated.** No category is automatic by default. `agent_preferences.autonomy_level` authorizes no write, and its current value `'autonomous'` may not be read retroactively as consent. Absence of a specific policy means do not automate; absence of calibration means do not automate; an invalid or unknown policy means do not automate. Any conflict, ambiguity or identity collision goes to Needs You.
- **Decision 4 — calibration uses outcomes the owner really reviewed.** No dataset, approval or threshold may be fabricated. Storage is content-minimal: no duplication of a record's full text where an owner-scoped reference suffices, no sensitive content in telemetry, no other user's data as reference, and absence of review is never approval. Correction, rejection and undo count as **distinct** signals. Each category is measured separately and inherits no confidence from another.
- **Decision 5 — what must be proved before enabling any category.** Minimum number of reviewed examples; hit rate; behaviour on errors; duplication risk; blocking rules; the category's specific threshold and its justification; and the ability to switch it off immediately. Where the repository defines no minimum size or calibration criterion, the slice **proposes a measurable contract and stops at the owner's checkpoint before the first category is enabled**.
- **Decision 6 — every automatic write, if one is ever authorized, must be** attributable to the agent, announced to the owner, briefly explained, content-minimally audited, genuinely and boundedly undoable, idempotent, replay- and concurrency-safe, owner-scoped under RLS, duplicate-checked before creating, never a declared relation from co-occurrence alone, never a new identity where a plausible match exists, never a durable memory from a one-off event, and never executed under conflict or insufficient information.
- **Decision 7 — the migration may not create schema nobody uses.** Slice 2P.4 itself must deliver a real consumer of the policy, a real producer of calibration evidence, an owner-scoped read, a visible per-category control, a safe state while calibration is insufficient, a history of the automatic decision, an accessible undo, content-free telemetry, and clear messages when automation is blocked for lack of evidence. The definitive visual organization of Settings belongs to slice 2P.5; 2P.4 must ship a functional, accessible surface that 2P.5 can reorganize **without changing its semantics**.
- **Decision 8 — nothing changes silently.** No grant, RLS policy, retention rule or authority may move without being presented first. Push HTTP 403 is not resumed, signup stays closed, rollout stays 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature, and no audio is persisted.
- **Decision 9 — the named remainders stay open and unabsorbed.** `2P-ATTENTION-008`'s browser half, `2P-CHAT-004-MOBILE` (slice 2P.5), `2P-CHAT-007-JOURNEY` (slice 2P.8) and `RG-DEP-3` are not absorbed by this slice.
- **Alternatives considered.** Reuse `agent_preferences.privacy_preferences` as the policy store (rejected: one vocabulary serving two authorities is the defect this repository has already paid for, and the reason `element_trust` and `element_policy` had to be normalized in slice 2P.1). Extend `element_trust` to cover domain writes (rejected: it decides whether an *element of an interpretation* may be applied, and overloading it would make one score govern both, which is the generic rule Decision 2 refuses). Read authority out of `autonomy_level` (rejected: a NOT NULL default is a value nobody chose, and reading it as consent would convert `create table` into authorization for six categories of automatic write). Defer 2P.4 entirely and proceed to 2P.5 (rejected by the owner: the slice's structure is buildable now, and stopping at the calibration checkpoint is the honest outcome rather than a reason not to start).
- **Consequences.** Migrations become 99 local once the slice merges, and 99 hosted once it is applied through the seven gates in order. **This allocation is spent by that one migration; a third is a stop condition requiring a new owner authorization.** The slice is expected to reach `CHECKPOINT DO DONO — CALIBRAÇÃO REAL NECESSÁRIA` rather than to enable anything: the hosted reference set measured at this baseline is 2 reviewed task candidates, 3 reviewed person candidates and zero for the other four categories. A13 continues to protect the unnamed roadmap successor.

- **Amendment (2026-08-19) — the owner SIGNS the calibration thresholds, and the proposal becomes the contract.** Decision 5 required the slice to *"propose a measurable contract and stop at the owner's checkpoint before the first category is enabled"*. Slice 2P.4 proposed one and stopped. The owner has now signed it, and from this amendment the numbers below are the **definitive contract** rather than a proposal — the word `PROPOSAL` is removed from `automation-policy.ts` in the same change that records this, so the code and this decision cannot disagree about their status.

  | Category | Minimum reviewed | Minimum accuracy |
  |---|---:|---:|
  | tasks | 50 | 0.90 |
  | projects | 60 | 0.95 |
  | companies | 60 | 0.95 |
  | people | 80 | 0.97 |
  | memories | 80 | 0.97 |
  | relations | 100 | 0.98 |

  Signed with them, and binding on every category alike: **at least 10 reviewed examples inside the last 90 days**; **the newest reviewed example no older than 30 days**; **any undo among the 20 most recent observations blocks eligibility**; **eligibility requires the owner's arming and sufficient measurement simultaneously**, neither being sufficient alone; **absence of a policy continues to mean `suggest_only`**; **no category is enabled now**; **no historical data predating the producer is retroactively turned into evidence**; **`dismissed` counts as neither an error nor a hit**; and **raw model confidence authorizes no write**. These are the values `CALIBRATION_THRESHOLDS` and `CALIBRATION_FRESHNESS` already carry and `private.automation_category_decision` already enforces; this amendment changes no number, and `automation-policy-parity.test.ts` continues to fail the build if the two ever diverge. **Signing a threshold is not enabling a category** — the measurement is still zero for all six, and the fail-closed posture is untouched.

- **Amendment (2026-08-19) — the four categories with no review flow are recorded as named remainders, not as calibration that time will complete.** `project`, `organization`, `memory` and `relation` have **no producer at all**, because the product has no owner review flow for them. That is a measured fact about the product and it is different in kind from "not enough evidence yet": waiting accumulates nothing. Slice 2P.5 must therefore, for each of the four, **carry an explicit traceable remainder** — `2P-AUTONOMY-FLOW-PROJECT`, `2P-AUTONOMY-FLOW-ORGANIZATION`, `2P-AUTONOMY-FLOW-MEMORY`, `2P-AUTONOMY-FLOW-RELATION` — keep the category in `suggest_only`, state on the surface that no review flow exists yet rather than showing a zero that reads as a shortfall, fabricate no observation, and **not** absorb the missing flows into documentation as though delivered. They are routed to the successor's re-audit at Phase 2P's closeout; **this amendment neither starts nor plans the roadmap successor.** `task` and `person` may accumulate evidence through their existing producers and remain in `suggest_only` until the signed minimums are genuinely reached.

- **Amendment (2026-08-19) — slice 2P.5 needs no migration, and the settings reorganization may not change what a setting means.** ADR-122 Decision 1 already authorizes slice 2P.5 in sequence; this records the two limits the owner restated when signing the thresholds. **Zero migrations: a third Phase 2P migration remains a stop condition.** No grant, RLS policy, retention rule or authority moves. Reorganizing Settings into stable sections may not change the semantics of any existing preference, may not lose one, may not re-expose a stored BYOK key, and may not offer a control that promises to enable a category that is not eligible. The `revalidatePath`-under-`[locale]` defect is repaired **only at the call sites the slice's own surfaces need**; the remaining call sites are recorded as separate debt rather than rewritten silently. Signup stays closed, rollout stays 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature, push HTTP 403 is not resumed, no BYOK credit is spent and no audio is persisted.

- **Amendment (2026-08-19) — slice 2P.7 needs no migration either, and the recurrence column it might have wanted is expressly refused.** ADR-122 Decision 1 already authorizes slice 2P.7 in sequence; this records the limits the owner restated when answering its two re-audit questions. **Zero migrations: a third Phase 2P migration remains a stop condition, and a recurrence column on `reminders` in particular is refused by name** — see ADR-121's amendment of the same date for the corrected `2P-REMINDER-002` and for the month view `2P-CALENDAR-001` really means. The month view is built from the reads and the write paths that already exist: `CALENDAR_ORIENTATIONS`, `calendar-query.ts`, `calendar-projection.ts` and the task-detail command path that `calendar-reschedule.tsx` already routes through. No grant, RLS policy, retention rule or authority moves. **No automation is enabled**, no inference becomes a stored fact, signup stays closed, rollout stays 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature, push HTTP 403 is not resumed, no BYOK credit is spent, and no audio is persisted.

- **Amendment (2026-08-19) — slice 2P.6 needs no migration, and the one column it might have wanted is expressly refused.** ADR-122 Decision 1 already authorizes slice 2P.6 in sequence; this records the limits the owner restated when correcting `2P-PERSON-001`. **Zero migrations: a third Phase 2P migration remains a stop condition, and `people.role` in particular is refused by name** — see ADR-121's amendment of the same date for where a role actually lives. Nothing in the twelve requirements needs deployed SQL: `people.organization_id`, `person_projects.role`, `task_people.role` (primary key `(task_id, person_id, role)`, `CHECK (role in ('requester','involved','assignee','waiting_on'))`) and the whole memory lifecycle already exist, and `authenticated` already holds the grants each one needs. No grant, RLS policy, retention rule or authority moves. **No relation is persisted to improve a drawing**, and no inference becomes a stored fact — `2N-IDENTITY-EXTRACTION` and `2N-RELATION-TRIGGER` stay the boundaries they were. **No automation is enabled**: the six categories stay fail-closed and the four named flow remainders stay open. Signup stays closed, rollout stays 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature, push HTTP 403 is not resumed, no BYOK credit is spent and no audio is persisted.

## ADR-124 — The owner amends OD-2J-1 for a review's own page, and records review-to-record links as a successor requirement

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, given at the Revisões checkpoint. It amends **one clause** of OD-2J-1 and authorizes **no migration**, no successor phase, and no change to any other governed surface.
- **Context.** The redesigned `/app/reviews/[reviewId]` opened **masked**, behind a "Mostrar resumo" control. The mask did not come from the sensitivity rule: `review_summary` resolves `{ normal: SHOW, private: SHOW, highly_sensitive: MASK }`, exactly as `hoje`, `attention` and `chat` do. It came from `review-body.tsx` **hard-coding `highly_sensitive` as the level of every summary**, because `summaries` carries no classification column to read — a choice OD-2J-1 made deliberately, and whose stated cost was that an owner with nothing sensitive still taps to read their own review. The owner has now judged that cost too high on this surface.
- **Decision 1 — a review's own page shows its content.** A person who has signed in and deliberately opened a review **that belongs to their own account** sees it without a second click. The disclosure control is removed from that page and `review-body.tsx` is deleted.
- **Decision 2 — the sensitivity rules are unchanged.** `GOVERNED_SURFACES`, the `RULES` table and `review_summary`'s entry are untouched. What is retired is **the fabrication of a level for a row that has none**, and `sensitivity-convergence.test.ts` now asserts that positively: no reviews surface may pass `resolveContent` or the literal `highly_sensitive`.
- **Decision 3 — the listing is not amended.** `/app/reviews` still carries **no review content at all**: `loadReviewListProjection` does not select the column and `toReviewSummaryView` has no such field. "Visible by default" reaches exactly one place — an authenticated owner's own review, on its own page.
- **Decision 4 — access control is unchanged.** The read stays `eq("user_id", user.id)` under RLS, and removed, foreign and never-existed remain one `notFound()` arm, so the page cannot be used to learn whether another account's review id is real.
- **Decision 5 — the tab strip may answer a tap, and may not become a source of truth.** Measured against the production build, the control took **1 476 ms (desktop), 1 427 ms (Pixel 7), 1 643 ms (iPhone WebKit)** to look selected. `useLinkStatus` now renders a pending indicator — `aria-hidden`, presentational, read by nothing. `aria-current` stays derived on the server from `searchParams`, no content is rendered optimistically, and the URL remains the only source of truth.
- **Decision 6 — links from a review to the records it was written from are a SUCCESSOR requirement, not a 2P deliverable.** `2P-REVIEW-CITATIONS`, high priority, specified in `docs/reports/phase-2p/PHASE_2P_REVIEW_CITATIONS_REQUIREMENT.md`. A link may be born **only** from a canonical, owner-scoped identifier, **never** from a name inferred out of the Markdown. It costs **one migration** — `summaries.citations jsonb`, the shape `conversation_messages.citations` already ships — and **that migration is not created here**. The presence of a "Fontes" section does not count as delivery.
- **Decision 7 — this remainder does not block Phase 2P from closing.** It is an extension beyond what the phase was authorized to deliver, it is explicit, and it is traceable from `TODO.md`, `STATE.md` and the page's own copy, which states the limit to the owner rather than hiding it behind an empty list.
- **Decision 8 — VoiceOver remains dispensed and NOT EXECUTED.** It is recorded as not executed, never as approved.

## ADR-125 — The owner closes Phase 2P, waives VoiceOver on the record, and authorizes no successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**, given after approving the final two-item checkpoint. It closes the phase and authorizes **nothing else**: no migration, no new scope, and **no successor phase**.
- **Context.** ADR-122 Decision 6 made two things the closing gate: a real iPhone and a real VoiceOver session. The owner ran four device passes (rounds one, two and three, then the Revisões checkpoint) and a final two-item checkpoint on tab latency and content visibility. Everything not explicitly rejected at each pass was recorded as approved rather than left ambiguous.
- **Decision 1 — Phase 2P is CLOSED.** All **87** requirements are classified exactly once by the generated matrix: **66 built, 12 baseline, 5 not-built-by-rule, 4 partial, 0 undelivered.** Each `partial` and `not-built-by-rule` names a concrete remainder and a destination, which `2P-CLOSE-002` enforces.
- **Decision 2 — VoiceOver is WAIVED, not passed.** `2P-ACCESS-005` is discharged by the owner's waiver and remains recorded as **NOT EXECUTED**. It must never be reported as approved, tested or passing. Any future statement about this product's screen-reader behaviour has to be earned by a session that actually happened.
- **Decision 3 — the phase's migration budget closes UNSPENT at its ceiling.** Two migrations were authorized and both were spent (`202608180098`, `202608190099`); parity is `202608190099`, 99 local = 99 hosted. **No migration was created after that**, including the two identified and refused during the Revisões work and the one `2P-REVIEW-CITATIONS` will need.
- **Decision 4 — `2P-REVIEW-CITATIONS` is explicitly NOT DELIVERED, and stays a priority pendency.** A review must link to the real records it was written from. It is specified in `docs/reports/phase-2p/PHASE_2P_REVIEW_CITATIONS_REQUIREMENT.md`, costs **one migration** that was deliberately not created, and is **high priority for the roadmap successor**. **The "Fontes" section that ships today is not delivery of it** — that section states what the row can prove and admits, to the owner, that it cannot name the individual records. Closing the phase does not absorb, downgrade or discharge this.
- **Decision 5 — the other remainders stay open and unabsorbed**, exactly as the matrix names them: `2P-ATTENTION-008`'s refresh-and-back half, `2P-CHAT-007`'s remaining journey, `2P-AUTONOMY-003`'s reference set, `2P-MOBILE-002`'s remainder, the four `2P-AUTONOMY` requirements blocked fail-closed by ADR-123 Decision 3, `A11Y-WEBKIT-DARK-CONTRAST`, and `RG-DEP-3`.
- **Decision 6 — no successor phase is started or planned.** This ADR authorizes no planning, no PRD, no implementation plan and no requirement enumeration for the roadmap successor. The phase-start guard stays where ADR-121 pointed it, and a start signal appearing in this repository is a defect until the owner authorizes one.

## ADR-126 — The owner authorizes Phase 2Q planning, supersedes ADR-125 Decision 6 alone, and retargets the phase-start guard to the roadmap successor

- **Date:** 2026-08-21
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **planning only**: a PRD, an implementation plan, an audit, a gaps report, a threat model, a traceability contract and the documentary guards that keep them fail-closed. **It authorizes no implementation, no migration, no deployment, no hosted data change, no signup change, no rollout change, no resumption of the push HTTP 403 investigation, and no successor phase.**
- **Context.** ADR-125 closed Phase 2P and its Decision 6 authorized *"no planning, no PRD, no implementation plan and no requirement enumeration for the roadmap successor"*. The owner has now given that authorization. **ADR-125 is not edited**: an accepted ADR in this series is never rewritten into agreement with a later one, so Decision 6 stands as it was signed and is superseded here, by name and in one clause only. Every other decision in ADR-125 — the closure itself, the VoiceOver waiver, the spent migration ceiling, and the list of unabsorbed remainders — is untouched and remains in force.
- **Decision 1 — Phase 2Q is authorized for PLANNING.** Its subject is the one place the product composes prose the reader cannot check: a generated review. The governing pair is `docs/initiatives/phase-2q/PHASE_2Q_PRD.md` and `PHASE_2Q_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; the evidence lives in `docs/reports/phase-2q/`. **39 requirements across six families and six slices, none implemented and none classified.**
- **Decision 2 — ADR-125 Decision 6 is superseded, and nothing else in ADR-125 is.** In particular **`2P-REVIEW-CITATIONS` is not discharged by being planned.** ADR-125 Decision 4 recorded it as NOT DELIVERED; it stays not delivered until a review on the owner's own device offers a working link to a real record. Planning it is not delivering it, and a "Fontes" section without canonical links in it is still not delivery.
- **Decision 3 — the migration budget is PROPOSED, not signed.** The package proposes **exactly one** migration, `summaries.citations jsonb`, whose necessity is proved in the audit: `summaries` has fourteen columns, `Relationships: []`, and nothing that can hold a citation. **This ADR allocates none.** The allocation is `OD-2Q-7` and requires the owner's signature in a separate implementation decision. **A second migration of any kind is a stop condition.**
- **Decision 4 — the eight open decisions are OPEN.** `OD-2Q-1` … `OD-2Q-8` are declared in PRD §2 with measured context, mutually exclusive options, a recommendation, a consequence and an effect on budget and schedule. **A recommendation is not a signature.** Requirements depending on an unsigned decision are not buildable, and the pull request carrying this package stays in **draft** until they are answered.
- **Decision 5 — the phase-start guard retargets to the roadmap successor.** `src/lib/closeout/phase-2f-documentation.test.ts`'s A13 detector moves off Phase 2Q — which this ADR authorizes — and onto the next unauthorized lettered phase, in this same commit, so the invariant is never unenforced in between. `phase-2o-declarations.test.ts`'s literal pin on the detector's target moves with it, deliberately, so that every future retarget must consciously pass through that guard. **This ADR names no successor**, here or in its heading, because an authorizing ADR that named the phase it was handing the guard to would start that phase in the act of authorizing this one.
- **Decision 6 — the inherited remainders are classified, not absorbed.** The audit classifies every open item from Phase 2P and earlier with a destination. `RG-DEP-3` stays a rollout matter and **cannot be closed by writing a file**; push HTTP 403 stays external and hardware; `2P-CHAT-007-JOURNEY` stays **unspendable rather than declined**, no AI credential existing in this environment; `2P-AUTONOMY-005`/`-006`/`-007`/`-008` stay `not-built-by-rule` under ADR-123 Decision 3; `2P-REMINDER-RECURRENCE` and `2P-CALENDAR-MONTH-TELEMETRY` stay refused by the decisions that refused them; `2P-MOBILE-002`'s keyboard and IME half stays owner hardware. **`2P-ACCESS-005` stays WAIVED, NOT PASSED**, and nothing in Phase 2Q may be reported as screen-reader evidence.
- **Decision 7 — the four missing automation review flows are not silently inherited.** ADR-123's amendment routed `project`, `organization`, `memory` and `relation` to this re-audit as one group. Measured, they are not one group, and the audit says so: two are medium, one changes the AI extraction contract and the Deno worker with it, and one is blocked by `2N-RELATION-TRIGGER`. Whether any of them enters this phase is `OD-2Q-8`, and the recommendation is a separate initiative.
- **Decision 8 — nothing changes silently.** Signup stays closed, the rollout gate stays 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature, all six automation categories stay `suggest_only` with no automatic writer, no grant, RLS policy, retention rule or authority moves, no BYOK credit is spent, no audio is persisted, and no hosted data is written.
- **Alternatives considered.** Amend ADR-125 Decision 6 in place (rejected: this series has never edited an accepted ADR into agreement with a later one, and ADR-113 and ADR-117 both set the precedent of amending by appending). Plan the successor from `PHASE_2K_2O_ROADMAP_DESIGN.md` (rejected: that document ends at Phase 2O and names nothing beyond it, so there is no roadmap definition to inherit — the objective is derived from the product measured in the audit). Adopt `PHASE_2P_REVIEW_CITATIONS_REQUIREMENT.md` as the plan (rejected: re-auditing it found its minimal model incomplete in a way that would have shipped a feature which silently produced no task links at all, and §11 of the roadmap design forbids treating a prior phase's specification as current truth). Absorb the accessibility defect, the automation flows and the rollout gates into one phase (rejected: the owner's instruction is explicit that the successor must not become a container for the project's debt).
- **Consequences.** The repository gains a governing artifact for Phase 2Q and the A13 detector moves one letter on. **Migrations stay 99 local = 99 hosted, parity `202608190099`**, because this package creates none. The estimate is **5.5 / 10.5 / 16.5 working days** with a nine-day critical path, and it is blocked at slice 2Q.1 until `OD-2Q-1`, `OD-2Q-3` and `OD-2Q-7` are signed. A start signal for the *new* successor appearing in this repository is a defect until the owner authorizes one.

## ADR-127 — The owner signs all eight Phase 2Q decisions, chooses the content-free source list over the recommendation, and forbids a reveal control on that path

- **Date:** 2026-08-21
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It signs the eight decisions ADR-126 left open and adds eight requirements to `OD-2Q-5`. **It authorizes no implementation, no deployment, no hosted data change, no signup change and no rollout change.** Phase 2Q remains **planning only**: implementation still needs a separate owner decision, and PR #276 stays a draft.
- **Context.** ADR-126 authorized Phase 2Q for planning and declared `OD-2Q-1` … `OD-2Q-8` open, each with measured context, mutually exclusive options and a recommendation. The owner has now answered all eight. **Seven follow the recommendation; `OD-2Q-5` does not**, and the divergence is the most consequential decision in the phase.

- **Decision 1 — `OD-2Q-1`, option A: one shared vocabulary, `entry | memory | task`.** The citation type set widens rather than forking. `ChatSource.type`, `CitedSourceType`, `ANSWER_REACH` and `referenceSchema.type` move together, and `resolve-sources.ts` gains a `task` branch resolving against `tasks`. **This costs no migration** — the pinning is TypeScript, and the deployed column is `citations jsonb not null default '[]'::jsonb` with no check constraint, no trigger and no validator. Chat's own citation tests are the control that its behaviour did not move.
- **Decision 2 — `OD-2Q-2`, option A: entries and tasks only.** A review may cite exactly what `generateReview` already retrieves. People, projects, organizations and memories are **not** citable in this phase, because the generator never puts them in the source set and a link to one could only be born by matching a name — which Decision 5 forbids. Widening retrieval stays a separate decision with its own cost.
- **Decision 3 — `OD-2Q-3`, option A: no backfill.** A review written before the producer existed carries the `unknown` evidence state, which means *"nobody recorded whether the Brain found anything"* — the only honest thing that can be said about it. No retrieval is re-run over past windows, because the rows in those windows have changed and the result would be references the original review was not written from.
- **Decision 4 — `OD-2Q-4`, option A: no telemetry event, and therefore no second migration.** `product_events` validates event names inside a deployed function, so a citation event would require a `create or replace` of deployed SQL. It is not funded, consistent with this phase's own budget and with ADR-123's refusal of `2P-CALENDAR-MONTH-TELEMETRY`.

- **Decision 5 — `OD-2Q-5`, option C: the source list identifies and links, and shows no content.** The owner chose **against** the recommendation, which was option A. The sources area on a review page shows **identification and a canonical link to the real object, and does not duplicate a preview of the content inside the review.** Opening the link is what shows the object, and **the destination page's own rules govern what is shown there.** **No reveal control is added on this path.** Eight requirements come with it:
  1. a review shows a **clear** sources area;
  2. a cited task appears **as a task**, never as a memory;
  3. every source points at its **canonical** object;
  4. no link is created by text matching;
  5. no duplicated preview of the content is shown;
  6. a **removed**, **foreign** or **unreadable** item degrades **without revealing which of those states occurred**;
  7. a historical review with no citations **says honestly that its sources were not recorded**;
  8. **the existence of a "Fontes" section without working links does not count as delivery.**

- **Decision 5.1 — what this choice does that the recommendation would not have.** Recorded because it is the reason the divergence is an improvement rather than a preference. `sensitivity-convergence.test.ts` already asserts that **no reviews surface may contain `resolveContent(` or the literal `highly_sensitive`**, over three named files including `reviews/[reviewId]/page.tsx` — an invariant ADR-124 established and left standing. **Option A would have broken it**, because rendering a cited record's preview means resolving that record's classification on a reviews surface. **Option C preserves it**: a source list carrying no governed content never calls `resolveContent` at all. The same choice also resolves a conflict the recommendation would have created — the shared `MASK` presentation carries `revealable: true`, so *"no reveal control"* is satisfiable only where nothing maskable is rendered.
- **Decision 5.2 — the identification is content-free, and it is uniform.** Following from 5.1: the row identifies its object by **kind and date**, not by its title or any excerpt, and it looks the **same for every citation regardless of classification**. Two reasons, both binding. First, a title is content and would put governed text back on the surface Decision 5.1 keeps clear. Second, a list that showed titles for ordinary objects and withheld them for sensitive ones would **disclose the classification by its shape**, which is a leak the product does not have today. **The review's own prose already names what it is talking about**; the sources area exists to point at the object, not to repeat the words above it — which is what *"não duplicar"* means.
- **Decision 6 — `OD-2Q-6`, option A: the WebKit dark-contrast defect is in scope.** `A11Y-WEBKIT-DARK-CONTRAST` is fixed as one small slice, and **only then** does the CI accessibility step widen to cover the lane that could not see it. The order is the requirement: widening CI first would land a red pipeline.
- **Decision 7 — `OD-2Q-7`, option A: exactly one migration.** It exists **solely** to persist `summaries.citations`. **This allocation is spent by that one migration, and any second need of any kind is a stop condition** requiring a new owner authorization. The allocation is now made rather than proposed.
- **Decision 8 — `OD-2Q-8`, option A: the four missing automation review flows are a separate initiative.** `project`, `organization`, `memory` and `relation` do not enter Phase 2Q. The measurement stands: the calibration producer is reachable only with the literals `'task'` and `'person'`, so those four can never accumulate evidence — and they are four different problems, not one.

- **Decision 9 — nothing else moves.** Phase 2Q remains **planning only** and PR #276 remains a **draft**. No slice is started, no migration is created, nothing is deployed. Signup stays closed, the rollout gate stays 25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature, all six automation categories stay `suggest_only`, push HTTP 403 is not resumed, no BYOK credit is spent, no audio is persisted, and `2P-ACCESS-005` stays **WAIVED, NOT PASSED**. **`2P-REVIEW-CITATIONS` stays NOT DELIVERED**: signing the decisions that would deliver it is not delivering it.
- **Consequences.** The requirement count rises from **39 to 42**: `2Q-LINK-008` and `-009` and `2Q-TRUST-009` are appended to the ends of their families to carry Decision 5's fifth, second and *no-reveal* requirements, which nothing in the 39 expressed. **No identifier is renumbered, reused or removed.** The migration budget becomes **1 allocated · 0 spent**. The estimate falls slightly — **5 / 9.5 / 15 working days**, eight-day critical path — because option C removes the cited-preview rendering, the classification resolution and the reveal control from slice 2Q.3. Slice 2Q.1 is **no longer blocked**: `OD-2Q-1`, `-3` and `-7` are signed. **The phase-start guard stays where ADR-126 pointed it**; this decision authorizes no successor and moves nothing.

## ADR-128 — The owner authorizes the implementation of Phase 2Q, from slice 2Q.0 through closeout, and spends the one allocated migration

- **Date:** 2026-08-21
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It authorizes **implementation**: slices `2Q.0` … `2Q.5`, branches, code, tests, pull requests, the merge of each after fully green CI, **exactly one migration**, its application to the hosted project after every gate, deployment of the surfaces the hosted proof needs, owner-scoped synthetic fixtures, and the technical closeout of the phase — **conditioned on a final owner checkpoint.** It authorizes nothing else.
- **Context.** ADR-126 authorized Phase 2Q for planning; ADR-127 signed its eight decisions; PR #276 merged the package at `0dc9d1a` and PR #277 recorded the integration at `a0295a2`. Both ADRs said, by name, that they authorized no implementation, and the implementation plan §4 named *"record an implementation authorization ADR"* as **the only remaining blocker on the whole phase**. The owner has now recorded it. **Neither earlier ADR is edited** — the rule since ADR-108 — and nothing they decided is reopened.

- **Decision 1 — the six slices are authorized, in order.** `2Q.0 → 2Q.1 → 2Q.2 → 2Q.3 → 2Q.4 → 2Q.5`. Each closes on its own pull request with **CI green on the exact merge SHA**, and **the next slice is re-audited against the `main` the previous one produced** before it begins. Slice 2Q.4 is the only one that may run alongside another, and only where its dependencies are genuinely satisfied; serial execution is preferred where parallelism would create merge, documentation or CI risk.
- **Decision 2 — the eight signed decisions are not reopened.** `OD-2Q-1` A · `OD-2Q-2` A · `OD-2Q-3` A · `OD-2Q-4` A · **`OD-2Q-5` C** · `OD-2Q-6` A · `OD-2Q-7` A · `OD-2Q-8` A. Implementation follows the option **signed**, not the option recommended. `OD-2Q-5` stays **literal**: no content of a cited record in the sources area, no preview, no excerpt, no sensitive title, no reveal control, no link born from text matching, uniform identification by kind, and a canonical link whose destination page governs what is shown there.
- **Decision 3 — exactly one migration, and a second is a stop condition.** `OD-2Q-7`'s allocation is **spent** by `summaries.citations`, in slice 2Q.1, alongside the writer that fills it. **A second migration of any kind halts the phase** and returns to the owner. `OD-2Q-4` stays signed as option A, so no pending decision can fund one.
- **Decision 4 — the migration reaches the hosted project only through every gate.** Migration and pgTAP; database lint; the whole chain applied to an empty database by CI; positive and negative controls; a green pull request; a green **merge SHA**; a clean tree whose local bytes are identical to the merged bytes; the hosted list read; a dry run showing **exactly one** pending migration; application; hosted proof; parity advancing by **exactly one**; and zero residue.
- **Decision 5 — no paid AI call is authorized.** If the only honest way to prove the real producer requires the owner's credential, the phase **stops and asks**. A hosted half that cannot be spent is recorded as **unspendable rather than declined** — the treatment `2P-CHAT-007-JOURNEY` already has — and never as a pass. No BYOK credit is spent silently and no simulated evidence is offered as a real proof.
- **Decision 6 — fixtures are synthetic and owner-scoped, and they are removed.** No personal content reaches a fixture, a log or a report. Residue is proved gone by a **two-sided** control: plant a row, prove the probe sees it, remove it, prove the probe no longer does. A zero count over an empty table is not a control.
- **Decision 7 — everything passes through a pull request, including documentation.** No commit lands directly on `main`, and that includes handoff sections and post-merge records. The deviation this repository recorded in Phase 2P — documentation pushed straight to `main` — is not repeated.
- **Decision 8 — the closeout is conditioned on the owner.** Slice 2Q.5 generates the matrix **from the slices' own acceptance records** rather than from typed classifications, and the phase **stops at an owner device checkpoint** before any formal declaration of closure. The owner validates a new review with sources, a task link, an entry link, a historical review, a removed source, the absence of any preview, mobile navigation, and contrast in Safari/WebKit.
- **Decision 9 — nothing else moves.** Signup stays closed. The rollout gate stays **25 pass · 3 fail · 2 owner-signature**, and **`RG-DEP-3` still cannot be closed by writing a file**. Push HTTP 403 is not resumed. Phase 2P is not reopened. The residual worktrees stay. All six automation categories stay `suggest_only` with no automatic writer. **`2P-ACCESS-005` stays WAIVED, NOT PASSED**, and nothing in this phase may be reported as screen-reader evidence. **No successor phase is started or planned**, and the phase-start guard stays where ADR-126 pointed it.
- **Decision 10 — `2P-REVIEW-CITATIONS` is discharged by delivery, not by this ADR.** It stays **NOT DELIVERED** until a review on the owner's own device offers a working link to a real record. Authorizing the work that would deliver it is not delivering it, and a "Fontes" section without canonical links in it is still not delivery — ADR-125 Decision 4, inherited.
- **Alternatives considered.** Authorize slice 2Q.1 alone and return for each subsequent slice (rejected: the owner asked explicitly for an autonomous loop with named stop conditions, and the stop conditions in Decisions 3, 5 and 8 are what make that safe). Fold the implementation authorization into ADR-127 by amendment (rejected: ADR-127 states on its own face that it authorizes no implementation, and this series has never edited an accepted ADR into agreement with a later one). Allocate a second migration pre-emptively for telemetry (rejected: `OD-2Q-4` is signed as option A, and a budget whose ceiling is not also its stop condition is not a budget).
- **Consequences.** Phase 2Q moves from **planned → signed → integrated → *not started*** to **authorized and in implementation**. The declaration guard's planning-only absences are **inverted rather than deleted**, slice by slice, as each artifact it forbade legitimately appears. Migrations stay **99 local = 99 hosted, parity `202608190099`** until slice 2Q.1 spends the allocation, after which they are 100 = 100. The estimate stands at **5 / 9.5 / 15** working days with an eight-day critical path. **A start signal for the roadmap successor appearing in this repository remains a defect** until the owner authorizes one.

## ADR-129 — The owner corrects `OD-2Q-6`'s premise: `A11Y-WEBKIT-DARK-CONTRAST` was a test-lane defect, not a product defect

- **Date:** 2026-08-21
- **Status:** Accepted — an **owner decision**. It corrects a **premise** that ADR-127 Decision 6 was signed on, and it corrects nothing else. **ADR-127 is not edited**: the rule since ADR-108 is that an accepted ADR in this series is never rewritten into agreement with a later one, so Decision 6 stands as it was signed and is amended here, by name and in one respect only. The decision it recorded — *fix the defect, and only then widen CI* — is **unchanged and honoured**; only the location of the defect moved.
- **Context.** `OD-2Q-6` was signed as option A on the premise that `A11Y-WEBKIT-DARK-CONTRAST` is *"live: global search and the Work bulk bar fail axe `color-contrast` (serious) in dark mode on the WebKit lane"* and *"a live accessibility defect with a known cause"*. Slice 2Q.4 reproduced it first, as the decision required. **The reproduction contradicted the premise**, and the owner was asked before any product code was touched.

- **Decision 1 — the premise is corrected: the product was never affected.** Measured on the **real running app**, in the **same WebKit engine**, with the theme applied the way the product applies it, global search's select computes `rgb(240, 237, 231)` on `rgb(28, 27, 24)` — identical to Chromium and comfortably above threshold. The failure existed **only in the fixture lane**.
